## TIM-274 · Grace Period Recovery

Customer journey · category time · slug `grace-period-recovery`

**Objective.** Tell the holder that validity has lapsed into a bounded period with reduced function, what still works, when that period ends and the one route back - so grace is a state they are in knowingly rather than one they discover when something stops.

**Trigger.** `grace_period_started` (an authoritative grace state recorded against the entity after its primary validity ended; a fixed end date for that grace period; a recorded condition that would recover the active state) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative grace state recorded against the entity after its primary validity ended; a fixed end date for that grace period; a recorded condition that would recover the active state. Not enough on its own: a validity date that is merely approaching; a failed renewal attempt with no grace policy applied to it.

**Entity.** the entity whose primary validity ended, plus the fixed end of the grace period placed on it — instance `entity_ref + grace_period_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative grace state recorded against the entity after its primary validity ended
- a fixed end date for that grace period
- a recorded condition that would recover the active state
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the entity whose primary validity ended
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Grace is never announced before it is authoritatively recorded. A warning about a lapse that has not happened is a different journey and belongs before this one.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The message names what still works, not only what stopped.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One message before the window closes, never two.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The window does not move because somebody read a message. Engagement is not recovery.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Both endings are stated. Silence after a recovery and silence after an expiry are indistinguishable to the person living in the window.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **notify-restricted** (`a.notify-restricted`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does grace restrict anything the holder will actually notice?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: grace_deadline_at falls inside the urgent_horizon attribute and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Name what has stopped working, what still works, the date the window ends and the single condition that restores the active state.
2. **notify-quiet** (`a.notify-quiet`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does grace restrict anything the holder will actually notice?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: grace_deadline_at falls inside the urgent_horizon attribute and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: State that validity has lapsed, that nothing has changed yet, and the date it will.
3. **confirm** (`a.confirm`) — configure grace_period.grace relative to `grace_deadline_at`; cancelled by `recovery_condition_satisfied` (the condition that ends the recoverable state is satisfied), `entity_terminated` (the entity is terminated); re-read before sending: the the entity whose primary validity ended re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What ended the wait?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: grace_deadline_at falls inside the urgent_horizon attribute and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm the active state is back and name which reduced capabilities returned.
4. **last-call** (`a.last-call`) — configure grace_period.grace relative to `grace_deadline_at`; cancelled by `recovery_condition_satisfied` (the condition that ends the recoverable state is satisfied), `entity_terminated` (the entity is terminated); re-read before sending: the the entity whose primary validity ended re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: grace_deadline_at falls inside the urgent_horizon attribute and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Send one message naming the exact end date, what stops at it, and the same single recovery route.
5. **lost** (`a.lost`, after t4) — configure grace_period.final relative to `grace_deadline_at`; cancelled by `recovery_condition_satisfied` (the condition that ends the recoverable state is satisfied); re-read before sending: the the entity whose primary validity ended re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: grace_deadline_at falls inside the urgent_horizon attribute and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say plainly that the window has closed, what is no longer available, and whether a route back still exists on different terms.
   - destination: route-back-on-new-terms bound to `entity_ref`; must not claim: that the old validity can be revived

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.recovered` [success] recovered inside the window — re-entry: a later lapse opens a new grace period and a new instance
- `x.moot` [invalid-state] grace ended by termination before its window closed — re-entry: an entity reinstated and later lapsing again qualifies afresh
- `x.lost` [timeout] window closed unrecovered — re-entry: a validity granted later starts a fresh lifecycle; re-entry here needs a new lapse


**Configure.**
- `grace_period.grace` · reminder-before-attribute — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The point inside the window at which one further message can still be acted on.
- `grace_period.final` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — After the last call the instance waits until the grace end itself; recovery before it is confirmed, and its passing is the loss.
- `grace_period.touches` — **recommended: 3** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the longest path sends a notice, a last call and a confirmation or loss notice) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `grace_period.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the entity whose primary validity ended; a later instance concerns a different the entity whose primary validity ended and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `grace_period_started` (an authoritative grace state recorded against the entity after its primary validity ended; a fixed end date for that grace period; a recorded condition that would recover the active state); `recovery_condition_satisfied` (the condition that ends the recoverable state is satisfied); `entity_terminated` (the entity is terminated). Attributes: `entity_ref`, `grace_period_id`, `grace_deadline_at`, `recovery_condition`, `restricted_capabilities`, `holder_id` (optional: `urgent_horizon`).

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 3 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.recovered, x.moot, x.lost · business outcome: `recovery_condition_satisfied` (the condition that ends the recoverable state is satisfied) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** grace period recovery · grace period notice · lapsed subscription grace · reduced function period · expired but recoverable

## TIM-281 · Expired Access Recovery

Customer journey · category time · slug `expired-entity-re-entry`

**Objective.** Give somebody who has come back to something that expired the one route that actually restores it, at the moment they are asking - because naming the wrong route spends the only intent this ever gets.

**Trigger.** `holder_acted_on_expired_entity` (an authoritative expiry recorded against the entity; an attempt by the holder to use, renew or ask about it after that expiry; the holder being the party the entity belonged to) [behavioral]. Requires: an authoritative expiry recorded against the entity; an attempt by the holder to use, renew or ask about it after that expiry; the holder being the party the entity belonged to. Not enough on its own: an expiry with no attempt against it; a scheduled win-back date with no action from the holder.

**Entity.** the expired entity and the holder's attempt to act on it, kept distinct from any replacement issued — instance `entity_ref + attempt_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative expiry recorded against the entity
- an attempt by the holder to use, renew or ask about it after that expiry
- the holder being the party the entity belonged to
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the expired entity and the holder's attempt to act on it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The route is worked out before anything is said. One wrong route spends an intent that arrived willing.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An expiry is never undone by editing its date. The gap during which nothing was valid stays on the record.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The replacement and the expired entity stay separately addressable, and the holder is told which one they now have.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A dead end is said once, clearly, with whatever alternative exists - not softened into a route that does not work.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **renew** (`a.renew`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Which mechanism restores validity here?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Name renewal as the route, what it costs, and what carries across the gap.
   - destination: renewal-route bound to `entity_ref`; must not claim: that the gap did not happen
2. **requalify** (`a.requalify`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Which mechanism restores validity here?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say plainly that this is not a renewal and that the conditions have to be met again, listing them.
   - destination: requalification-conditions bound to `entity_ref`; must not claim: that this is a renewal
3. **replace** (`a.replace`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Which mechanism restores validity here?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Explain that the old one stays expired and a new one is issued in its place, and what differs between them.
   - destination: replacement-issuance bound to `entity_ref`; must not claim: that the old one is revived
4. **no-route** (`a.no-route`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Which mechanism restores validity here?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say there is no way back to this one and name what exists instead, if anything does.
5. **confirm** (`a.confirm`) — configure expired_entity.act after the previous touch; cancelled by `validity_restored` (the named route back is completed and validity restored), `recovery_attempt_abandoned` (the holder stops pursuing the route back); re-read before sending: the the expired entity and the holder's attempt to act on it re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: Did validity come back?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm what is valid now and, where a replacement was issued, that it is a new one rather than the old one revived.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.restored` [success] valid again by the route that was named — re-entry: the restored or replacement entity has its own expiry, and a later lapse enters here again
- `x.still-expired` [timeout] still expired; the route was offered and not taken — re-entry: a further attempt by the holder starts a new instance with the route re-evaluated from current rules
- `x.terminal` [invalid-state] expired with no route back — re-entry: nothing re-enters here - a new entitlement of this kind is a different object and starts at its own issuance


**Configure.**
- `expired_entity.act` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The named route is left open for a bounded response window from the moment it was named; an untaken route closes as still expired.
- `expired_entity.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; one route statement and one confirmation) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `expired_entity.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the expired entity and the holder's attempt to act on it; a later instance concerns a different the expired entity and the holder's attempt to act on it and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `holder_acted_on_expired_entity` (an authoritative expiry recorded against the entity; an attempt by the holder to use, renew or ask about it after that expiry; the holder being the party the entity belonged to); `validity_restored` (the named route back is completed and validity restored); `recovery_attempt_abandoned` (the holder stops pursuing the route back). Attributes: `entity_ref`, `attempt_id`, `holder_id`, `expired_at`, `restoring_mechanism`, `replacement_ref`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.restored, x.still-expired, x.terminal · business outcome: `validity_restored` (the named route back is completed and validity restored) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** expired access recovery · expired subscription re-entry · expired licence renewal route · lapsed entitlement recovery · came back after expiry

## ACC-261 · Access Recovery

Customer journey · category access · slug `access-restriction-route-back`

**Objective.** Tell the person holding the account what access is going away, when, and the one condition that would bring it back - so a restriction is a decision they can act on rather than a discovery they make later.

**Trigger.** `access_restriction_or_end_recorded` (an authoritative restriction, suspension or end date recorded against the account; a stated condition or deadline that would resolve it) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative restriction, suspension or end date recorded against the account; a stated condition or deadline that would resolve it. Not enough on its own: a risk signal that has not yet produced a restriction; an internal review that has not concluded.

**Entity.** person or account plus the specific restriction or end date placed on it — instance `account_id + restriction_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative restriction, suspension or end date recorded against the account
- a stated condition or deadline that would resolve it
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person or account plus the specific restriction or end date placed on it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A restriction is never announced before it is authoritatively recorded. Warning about a decision nobody has taken is how a support queue fills up.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The notice names what still works, not only what stopped.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Where the holder cannot resolve the condition, no call to action is attached.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Restoration is confirmed explicitly. Silence after a resolved restriction reads as the restriction continuing.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **inform-only** (`a.inform-only`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Can the holder do anything about this?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell them what is restricted and until when, with no call to action attached - because there is nothing for them to do.
2. **notify** (`a.notify`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Can the holder do anything about this? · Is there a permitted route to them?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: State exactly what is restricted, what still works, the deadline, and the single condition that lifts it.
   - destination: resolution-condition bound to `restriction_id`; must not claim: a restriction that is not yet authoritative
3. **confirm** (`a.confirm`, after t2) — configure access_restriction.resolve relative to `resolution_deadline_at`; cancelled by `release_condition_met` (the condition recorded to release the suppression is met), `restriction_lifted` (the restriction is lifted by its owner), `restriction_made_permanent` (the restriction is made permanent); re-read before sending: the person or account plus the specific restriction or end date placed on it re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: Where did it land?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm that access is back and name what was restored, so the person can tell the difference between a resolved restriction and a partial one

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.informed` [success] informed, resolution not theirs — re-entry: if the condition later becomes something they can satisfy, this qualifies again with the actionable path
- `x.security-owned` [suppression] restriction announced by the security response; no separate notice sent — re-entry: the security response clearing, or converting the restriction into an ordinary one, re-evaluates it here on its own terms
- `x.restored` [success] restored and confirmed — re-entry: a later restriction on the same account is a new instance
- `x.stands` [timeout] restriction stands past its deadline — re-entry: if the condition is satisfied afterwards, the restoration path runs from the lifting event
- handoff `h.unreachable` → CON-36 (Contactability Recalculation) — a restriction notice that cannot be delivered on any permitted route

**Configure.**
- `access_restriction.resolve` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The stated deadline or review point.
- `access_restriction.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; one notice and one confirmation) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `access_restriction.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per person or account plus the specific restriction or end date placed on it; a later instance concerns a different person or account plus the specific restriction or end date placed on it and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `access_restriction_or_end_recorded` (an authoritative restriction, suspension or end date recorded against the account; a stated condition or deadline that would resolve it); `release_condition_met` (the condition recorded to release the suppression is met); `restriction_lifted` (the restriction is lifted by its owner); `restriction_made_permanent` (the restriction is made permanent). Attributes: `account_id`, `restriction_id`, `restricted_capabilities`, `still_working`, `resolution_condition`, `resolution_deadline_at`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.informed, x.security-owned, x.restored, x.stands, h.unreachable · business outcome: `restriction_lifted` (the restriction is lifted by its owner) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** access recovery · restriction notice · account restricted notice · access ending notice · suspension notice

## ACC-263 · Activation Reminder

Customer journey · category access · slug `entitlement-activation-window`

**Objective.** Get somebody to actually use what they have been granted, before the window in which they can claim it closes - because an unredeemed entitlement is indistinguishable from one that was never granted.

**Trigger.** `entitlement_provisioned_and_reachable` (an entitlement or credential authoritatively granted to a named holder; confirmation that it is provisioned and reachable by that holder; an activation window or expiry) [authoritative]. Requires: an entitlement or credential authoritatively granted to a named holder; confirmation that it is provisioned and reachable by that holder; an activation window or expiry. Not enough on its own: an eligibility decision with no provisioning behind it; a grant whose resource is not yet reachable.

**Entity.** the issued entitlement or credential and its activation window — instance `entitlement_id + holder_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an entitlement or credential authoritatively granted to a named holder
- confirmation that it is provisioned and reachable by that holder
- an activation window or expiry
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the issued entitlement or credential and its activation window
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One reminder, never two. The window is the pressure; repetition is not.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A holder who has used this capability before is not re-onboarded onto it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Lapsed-unclaimed and revoked-before-use are recorded as different outcomes - one is about the holder, the other is not.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The message names one action, not the full capability surface.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **ready** (`a.ready`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is this the holder's first entitlement of this kind?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say what is now available, what it lets them do, and the single first action that uses it.
   - destination: first-use-action bound to `entitlement_id`
2. **brief** (`a.brief`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is this the holder's first entitlement of this kind?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm the new grant briefly and name only what changed from what they already had.
3. **remind** (`a.remind`) — configure entitlement_activation.first_use relative to `activation_window_ends_at`; cancelled by `capability_first_used` (the granted capability is used for the first time), `entitlement_revoked_or_replaced` (the entitlement is revoked or replaced); re-read before sending: the the issued entitlement or credential and its activation window re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: Is there still time to claim it?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Send one reminder naming the deadline and the same single first action.
   - destination: first-use-action bound to `entitlement_id`; must not claim: a moved deadline

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.activated` [success] activated — re-entry: a further entitlement to the same holder is a new instance
- `x.moot` [invalid-state] entitlement withdrawn before use — re-entry: a reissued entitlement starts a fresh window
- `x.lapsed` [timeout] lapsed unclaimed — re-entry: a new grant of the same capability starts a new window


**Configure.**
- `entitlement_activation.first_use` · reminder-before-attribute — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The single reminder is placed before the activation window closes, late enough that the first notice has had its chance and early enough that claiming is still possible.
- `entitlement_activation.last_chance` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — After the reminder the instance waits until the activation window itself closes; there is no second reminder.
- `entitlement_activation.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; one notice and one reminder) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `entitlement_activation.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the issued entitlement or credential and its activation window; a later instance concerns a different the issued entitlement or credential and its activation window and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `entitlement_provisioned_and_reachable` (an entitlement or credential authoritatively granted to a named holder; confirmation that it is provisioned and reachable by that holder; an activation window or expiry); `capability_first_used` (the granted capability is used for the first time); `entitlement_revoked_or_replaced` (the entitlement is revoked or replaced). Attributes: `entitlement_id`, `holder_id`, `provisioned_at`, `activation_window_ends_at`, `first_action`, `prior_familiarity`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.activated, x.moot, x.lapsed · business outcome: `capability_first_used` (the granted capability is used for the first time) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** activation reminder · unused entitlement reminder · claim your access · credential activation reminder · licence activation

## IDN-81 · Identity Verification

Customer journey · category identity · slug `identity-claim-verification`

**Objective.** Establish confidence in one specific identity claim, bound to the evidence that established it.

**Trigger.** `identity_verification_required` (a specific identity claim that a process or policy requires to be verified, at a stated scope) [authoritative]. Requires: a specific identity claim that a process or policy requires to be verified, at a stated scope. Not enough on its own: someone stating their name, which is a claim rather than a request to verify it; an account holding an attribute nobody has checked.

**Entity.** the individual identity claim - which attribute, at what assurance, for what purpose — instance `claim_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a specific identity claim that a process or policy requires to be verified, at a stated scope
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the individual identity claim
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An identity supplied is not an identity verified.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Verifying one attribute does not verify unrelated attributes.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Evidence is scoped to the claim being verified. Asking for more than the claim needs creates liability without confidence.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Evidence rounds are bounded by policy rather than repeated until something is accepted.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **request** (`a.request`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the required evidence already available?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Request the minimum evidence this claim requires, and record the state as EVIDENCE_REQUIRED.
   - destination: evidence-submission bound to `claim_id`; must not claim: that unrelated attributes are verified
2. **request-more** (`a.request-more`, after t1) — configure identity_claim.evidence after the previous touch; cancelled by `verification_evidence_received` (the requested evidence for the claim is received); re-read before sending: the the individual identity claim re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: Is the required evidence already available? · What did validation conclude? · Does the policy allow another evidence round?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Request the specific additional evidence that would settle it, naming what is missing rather than repeating the original request
   - destination: evidence-submission bound to `claim_id`

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.expired` [timeout] EXPIRED; the claim was never verified — re-entry: a new verification instance may be opened for the same claim. Expired is not rejected - nobody assessed the claim and found against it
- `x.verified` [success] VERIFIED for this claim, at this scope and assurance — re-entry: a different claim is verified on its own evidence, and this one is re-verified when its own validity lapses
- handoff `h.review` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a claim that automated validation cannot settle
- handoff `h.failure` → IDN-84 (Verification Recovery) — verification failing against a claim

**Configure.**
- `identity_claim.evidence` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The verification timeout for this claim.
- `identity_claim.validate_budget` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — This loop runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; when it is spent the instance takes its timeout path (GLB-24).
- `identity_claim.touches` — **recommended: 0** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch in this plan is the verification itself and is mandatory; nothing discretionary exists to cap.
- `identity_claim.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the individual identity claim; a later instance concerns a different the individual identity claim and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `identity_verification_required` (a specific identity claim that a process or policy requires to be verified, at a stated scope); `verification_evidence_received` (the requested evidence for the claim is received). Attributes: `claim_id`, `identity_id`, `attribute`, `required_assurance`, `purpose`, `evidence_policy`, `round_limit`, `verification_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default security · pressure class none · local cap 0 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.expired, x.verified, h.review, h.failure · business outcome: `verification_evidence_received` (the requested evidence for the claim is received) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** identity verification · KYC verification · ID check · identity proofing · document verification

## IDN-84 · Verification Recovery

Customer journey · category identity · slug `verification-failure-routing`

**Objective.** Route a failed verification by why it failed, and keep our own failures out of the customer's verification record.

**Trigger.** `verification_attempt_failed` (a verification attempt that did not establish its claim) [authoritative]. Requires: a verification attempt that did not establish its claim. Not enough on its own: a verification that expired with no attempt made, which is IDN-81's expiry; a policy change invalidating an existing verification without anyone having attempted one.

**Entity.** the verification instance that failed — instance `verification_instance_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a verification attempt that did not establish its claim
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the verification instance that failed
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Retry limits are policy-defined and bounded, and the bound is tighter where the claim is security-sensitive.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A technical failure is never recorded as an identity rejection.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Repeated retries do not bypass security controls. Exhausting the budget routes to a person, not to an acceptance.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The failure reason is preserved, because the route out depends on it.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **explain** (`a.explain`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: What kind of failure was it? · Does the retry budget for this claim have room?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Explain exactly what needs correcting and offer the bounded retry.
   - destination: retry-verification bound to `verification_instance_id`
2. **explain-terminal** (`a.explain-terminal`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: What kind of failure was it?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say that this claim cannot be verified on this basis, and name what basis would be accepted if any is.
   - destination: alternative-verification-basis bound to `verification_instance_id`

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.retry` [success] bounded retry available; the verification instance stays open — re-entry: the retry runs as part of the same verification instance, against the same budget - a new instance would reset the count, which is how a bounded retry becomes an unbounded one
- `x.terminal` [failure] verification not possible on this basis — re-entry: a different basis or a different claim is assessed on its own terms; what would have to change here is the policy rather than the evidence
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a technical failure that is not clearing
- handoff `h.review` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a failure requiring human judgement, or a budget reached

**Configure.**
- `verification_failure.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `verification_failure.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the verification instance that failed; a later instance concerns a different the verification instance that failed and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `verification_attempt_failed` (a verification attempt that did not establish its claim). Attributes: `verification_instance_id`, `claim_id`, `failure_class`, `retry_budget`, `backoff_budget`, `verification_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default security · pressure class none · local cap 1 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.retry, x.terminal, h.escalate, h.review · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** verification recovery · failed verification · ID check failed · verification retry

## IDN-85 · Login Verification

Customer journey · category identity · slug `authentication-challenge`

**Objective.** Establish that whoever is present controls the required identity, at the assurance the context demands.

**Trigger.** `authentication_required` (a context requiring the actor's control of an identity to be established) [authoritative]. Requires: a context requiring the actor's control of an identity to be established. Not enough on its own: a session that already holds the required assurance; an authorization question about what an authenticated actor may do.

**Entity.** the authentication session, bound to an actor and an account — instance `session_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a context requiring the actor's control of an identity to be established
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the authentication session
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Authentication is not authorization.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A successful login does not prove entitlement to any particular resource.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An authenticated session carries an explicit assurance level and an explicit validity.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An unanswered challenge is not recorded as a failed authentication.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `single-notice`

1. **challenge** (`a.challenge`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does the existing authentication already meet the required level?
   - channel roles, in order: urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded) → low-friction (push: a valid token or app session exists and the message is a single step from the notification) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Issue the challenge appropriate to the required assurance level

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.satisfied` [success] already authenticated at the required level — re-entry: a context requiring higher assurance re-opens this. Being authenticated says who the actor is and nothing about what they may do
- `x.timeout` [timeout] challenge unanswered; nothing authenticated and nothing recorded against the actor — re-entry: a new attempt issues a new challenge
- `x.authenticated` [success] authenticated, at a stated assurance and for a stated validity — re-entry: authorization for any particular action is decided separately, every time - a successful login proves control of an account and entitlement to nothing
- handoff `h.stepup` → IDN-86 (Step-Up Authentication) — control established below the assurance the context requires
- handoff `h.failure` → IDN-87 (Authentication Risk Assessment) — an authentication failure

**Configure.**
- `authentication_challenge.auth` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The challenge window.
- `authentication_challenge.touches` — **recommended: 0** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — The challenge is the authentication itself and is mandatory; nothing discretionary exists to cap.
- `authentication_challenge.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the authentication session; a later instance concerns a different the authentication session and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `authentication_required` (a context requiring the actor's control of an identity to be established); `authentication_succeeded` (the authentication challenge succeeds); `authentication_failed` (the authentication challenge fails). Attributes: `session_id`, `identity_id`, `account_id`, `required_assurance`, `existing_assurance`, `authentication_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default security · pressure class none · local cap 0 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.satisfied, x.timeout, x.authenticated, h.stepup, h.failure · business outcome: `authentication_succeeded` (the authentication challenge succeeds) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** login verification · OTP · 2FA · MFA challenge · one-time code · login code

## IDN-270 · Account Recovery

Customer journey · category identity · slug `account-recovery-guidance`

**Objective.** Carry somebody who cannot authenticate through the evidence their recovery actually requires, inside a stated window, on a route that is no weaker than the login it is standing in for.

**Trigger.** `account_recovery_case_opened` (a recovery case authoritatively opened against one existing account; the recovery basis and the evidence that basis requires; a recovery window with a stated end) [authoritative]. Requires: a recovery case authoritatively opened against one existing account; the recovery basis and the evidence that basis requires; a recovery window with a stated end. Not enough on its own: a failed authentication attempt; a support conversation about being locked out; a destination supplied inside the request itself.

**Entity.** one existing account plus this recovery case and its window — instance `recovery_case_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a recovery case authoritatively opened against one existing account
- the recovery basis and the evidence that basis requires
- a recovery window with a stated end
- no instance of this journey is already open for the one existing account plus this recovery case and its window
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The recovery route goes to a destination the account already held, never to one supplied with the request.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Recovery is a different route to the same assurance, not a lower one. Nothing here is easier than the authentication it stands in for.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The window is never restarted by repeated attempts, and never extended by engagement.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One reminder, never two. On a security route, repetition is indistinguishable from pressure.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] What was invalidated is named in the confirmation, or the next refused credential reads as a fresh attack.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **issue** (`a.issue`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is this account already under an open security incident?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Send the recovery route to a destination the account already held, and state exactly what evidence is required and when the window closes.
   - destination: recovery-route bound to `recovery_case_id`; must not claim: a destination supplied inside the request
2. **restored** (`a.restored`, after t1) — configure account_recovery.proof relative to `recovery_window_ends_at`; cancelled by `proof_of_control_provided` (sufficient proof of control or identity is provided), `access_regained` (the requester regains access by ordinary authentication), `request_withdrawn` (the requester withdraws the request); re-read before sending: the one existing account plus this recovery case and its window re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What ended the wait?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm that control is back and name what was invalidated on the way - the sessions and credentials that will no longer work.
3. **remind** (`a.remind`, after t1) — configure account_recovery.proof relative to `recovery_window_ends_at`; cancelled by `proof_of_control_provided` (sufficient proof of control or identity is provided), `access_regained` (the requester regains access by ordinary authentication), `request_withdrawn` (the requester withdraws the request); re-read before sending: the one existing account plus this recovery case and its window re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: Is one reminder still worth sending?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority security · [RECOMMENDED_DEFAULT]
   - purpose: Send one reminder on the same already-held destination, naming the deadline and the evidence still outstanding.
   - destination: recovery-route bound to `recovery_case_id`; must not claim: a restarted window

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.restored` [success] control restored and confirmed — re-entry: a later recovery case on the same account is a new instance with its own window
- `x.moot` [success] recovery closed without being used — re-entry: a new request opens a new case; this one is not resumed
- `x.closed` [timeout] recovery window closed without sufficient proof — re-entry: a fresh request opens a new case and a new window; the closed one is never extended
- handoff `h.security` → IDN-90 (Account Compromise Recovery) — a recovery attempt on an account that is already under an open compromise signal

**Configure.**
- `account_recovery.proof` · reminder-before-attribute — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The point in the window at which a reminder would still leave time to act.
- `account_recovery.final` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — After the reminder the instance waits until the recovery window itself closes; the window is never restarted.
- `account_recovery.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `account_recovery.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per one existing account plus this recovery case and its window; a later instance concerns a different one existing account plus this recovery case and its window and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `account_recovery_case_opened` (a recovery case authoritatively opened against one existing account; the recovery basis and the evidence that basis requires; a recovery window with a stated end); `proof_of_control_provided` (sufficient proof of control or identity is provided); `access_regained` (the requester regains access by ordinary authentication); `request_withdrawn` (the requester withdraws the request). Attributes: `recovery_case_id`, `account_id`, `recovery_basis`, `required_evidence`, `recovery_window_ends_at`, `held_destinations`.

**Collision & priority.** default security · pressure class none · local cap 1 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.restored, x.moot, x.closed, h.security · business outcome: `proof_of_control_provided` (sufficient proof of control or identity is provided) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** account recovery · forgot password recovery · locked-out recovery · recovery link · regain access

## IDN-271 · Account Security Alert

Customer journey · category identity · slug `compromise-alert-verification`

**Objective.** Tell the account owner, at every verified destination the signal does not implicate, what was seen and what was restricted - and let their answer resolve it: cleared, or into secure recovery.

**Trigger.** `compromise_incident_opened_with_scope_determined` (a security incident authoritatively opened against the account; the affected scope determined - which sessions, credentials and capabilities; a record of whether containment was applied and how wide) [authoritative]. Requires: a security incident authoritatively opened against the account; the affected scope determined - which sessions, credentials and capabilities; a record of whether containment was applied and how wide. Not enough on its own: a single failed authentication; a risk score with no incident behind it; an unusual location with no other signal.

**Entity.** the account plus this security incident and the scope it affects — instance `account_id + incident_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a compromise incident is opened for the account with its scope determined
- at least one verified destination exists that the signal does not implicate
- the incident has a review point set

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No alert goes to a destination the signal implicates; where every route is implicated the alert is withheld and the incident proceeds on the security side alone.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Suspected is not confirmed: the alert says what was restricted only where something was, and says plainly that nothing was restricted where nothing was.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The alert goes to every verified, unimplicated destination at once; a single destination that may itself be compromised is never the only one told.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An incident open past its review point is stated to the owner as open, with what remains restricted; it is not silently left in place.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Security alerts are exempt from pressure caps and marketing permission; only hard gates (GLB-31) apply, and a fraud hold does not stop an alert to the owner at an unimplicated destination.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **alert** (`a.alert-contained`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is there a destination the signal does not implicate? · Has anything actually been restricted?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: a verified address the signal does not implicate exists; the alert must be kept and re-read) → urgent (sms: a verified number the signal does not implicate exists; the shortest route to the confirmation question)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say what was restricted, why, and that confirming whether the recent activity was theirs is what resolves it.
   - destination: activity-confirmation bound to `incident_id`; must not claim: that the account is confirmed compromised, a restriction that was not applied
2. **alert** (`a.alert-watch`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is there a destination the signal does not implicate? · Has anything actually been restricted?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: a verified address the signal does not implicate exists; the alert must be kept and re-read) → urgent (sms: a verified number the signal does not implicate exists; the shortest route to the confirmation question)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Ask whether the recent activity was theirs and say plainly that nothing has been restricted.
   - destination: activity-confirmation bound to `incident_id`; must not claim: that anything was restricted, that the account is confirmed compromised
3. **resolution** (`a.cleared`) — configure security_alert.review_point after the trigger; cancelled by `activity_confirmed_own` (the owner confirms the activity was theirs), `activity_reported_not_own` (the owner reports the activity was not theirs), `security_review_concluded` (the security review concludes); re-read before sending: the incident re-read: the owner's answer if any, the security review's state, and what remains restricted
   - checks: What did the answer establish?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: a verified address the signal does not implicate exists; the alert must be kept and re-read) → urgent (sms: a verified number the signal does not implicate exists; the shortest route to the confirmation question)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say the signal is cleared and name anything that has been lifted, so a restriction met yesterday is not still assumed today.
4. **resolution** (`a.standing`) — configure security_alert.review_point after the trigger; cancelled by `activity_confirmed_own` (the owner confirms the activity was theirs), `activity_reported_not_own` (the owner reports the activity was not theirs), `security_review_concluded` (the security review concludes); re-read before sending: the incident re-read: the owner's answer if any, the security review's state, and what remains restricted
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: a verified address the signal does not implicate exists; the alert must be kept and re-read) → urgent (sms: a verified number the signal does not implicate exists; the shortest route to the confirmation question)
   - mandatory · priority security · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell the owner the incident is still open past its review point, what remains restricted, and that nothing further is assumed from their silence.
   - destination: incident-status bound to `incident_id`; must not claim: that the incident is resolved

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel · simultaneous: every verified destination the signal does not implicate is told at once, because a single destination may itself be the compromised one. No action when: s.implicated-route, s.no-claim, s.multi-destination, s.owner-informed-past-review, s.hard-gates-only (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.withheld` [no-action] no destination outside the suspicion; no alert sent — re-entry: if a destination outside the affected scope is established, the verification question runs from there
- `x.cleared` [success] cleared; restrictions lifted and the owner told — re-entry: a further signal on the same account is a new incident and a new instance
- `x.open` [timeout] incident open past its review point; the owner knows where it sits — re-entry: the conclusion, whenever it lands, re-enters on the cleared or the recovery path
- handoff `h.recovery` → IDN-88 (Account Recovery Verification) — a confirmed compromise where the owner needs secure control of the account rebuilt

**Configure.**
- `security_alert.review_point` · decision-sla — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The wait is the review point set for this incident by the security process; an owner still silent at the review point is told the incident stands open, and silence is never read as confirmation.
- `security_alert.discretionary_touches` — **recommended: 0** (confidence high; when every touch in the plan is marked mandatory) — Every touch in this plan is mandatory; nothing is rationed and nothing discretionary exists to cap.
- `security_alert.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high) — Alerts are per incident; a new incident is its own instance and no cooldown applies between incidents.

**Required data.** Events: `compromise_incident_opened_with_scope_determined` (a security incident authoritatively opened against the account; the affected scope determined - which sessions, credentials and capabilities; a record of whether containment was applied and how wide); `activity_confirmed_own` (the owner confirms the activity was theirs); `activity_reported_not_own` (the owner reports the activity was not theirs); `security_review_concluded` (the security review concludes). Attributes: `account_id`, `incident_id`, `scope`, `implicated_destinations`, `verified_destinations`, `containment_applied`, `review_point_at` (optional: `restrictions_applied`, `signal_summary`).

**Collision & priority.** default security · pressure class none · local cap 0 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.cleared, x.open, x.withheld, h.recovery · business outcome: `activity_confirmed_own` (the owner confirms the activity was theirs) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution entered-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: alert_to_implicated_destination, restriction_claimed_not_applied, complaint, support_contact_within_24h

**Aliases.** account security alert · suspicious login alert · unusual activity alert · compromise notification · security notification · new device alert

## REL-284 · Invitation Reminder

Customer journey · category structure · slug `relationship-invitation`

**Objective.** Put a proposed link in front of the party who has to accept it, on terms they can see before they answer, and close the question one way or the other before the invitation goes stale.

**Trigger.** `relationship_invitation_issued` (an invitation recorded by a party holding the authority to extend it; a named counterparty, the scope of the proposed link and its direction; an expiry on the invitation) [authoritative]. Requires: an invitation recorded by a party holding the authority to extend it; a named counterparty, the scope of the proposed link and its direction; an expiry on the invitation. Not enough on its own: a shared attribute between two records - an address, a name, a device; an intention to invite that has not been recorded as an invitation.

**Entity.** the invitation, plus the two parties and the scope of the link it proposes, in the direction it was issued — instance `invitation_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an invitation recorded by a party holding the authority to extend it
- a named counterparty, the scope of the proposed link and its direction
- an expiry on the invitation
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the invitation
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An invitation is not a relationship. Nothing is linked, and nothing is shown to either side as linked, until the counterparty accepts.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Acceptance links two records and merges none. Both parties keep their own identity, history and lifecycle.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Scope and direction are stated in the invitation itself, not discovered after acceptance.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One reminder, never two. The expiry is the pressure; repetition is not.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Every invitation expires. A pending claim on somebody who never agreed to it does not sit open indefinitely.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **invite-known** (`a.invite-known`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does the counterparty already exist here in their own right?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Name who is inviting them, exactly what the link would let that party do, and what it would not change about their own record.
   - destination: invitation-acceptance bound to `invitation_id`; must not claim: that accepting transfers anything they hold
2. **invite-new** (`a.invite-new`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does the counterparty already exist here in their own right?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: State who is inviting them and into what, and say plainly that accepting creates a link rather than a transfer of anything they hold.
   - destination: invitation-acceptance bound to `invitation_id`; must not claim: that accepting transfers anything they hold
3. **confirm** (`a.confirm`) — configure relationship_invitation.response relative to `expires_at`; cancelled by `invitation_accepted` (the counterparty accepts the invitation), `invitation_declined` (the counterparty declines the invitation), `invitation_withdrawn` (the inviting party withdraws the invitation); re-read before sending: the the invitation re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: How was the invitation answered?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm to both sides that the link is active, naming its scope and its direction and what each side can now see or do.
4. **remind** (`a.remind`) — configure relationship_invitation.response relative to `expires_at`; cancelled by `invitation_accepted` (the counterparty accepts the invitation), `invitation_declined` (the counterparty declines the invitation), `invitation_withdrawn` (the inviting party withdraws the invitation); re-read before sending: the the invitation re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: Is a reminder still worth sending?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Send one reminder naming who is waiting and the date the invitation expires.
   - destination: invitation-acceptance bound to `invitation_id`; must not claim: a moved expiry

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.active` [success] link active, both identities intact — re-entry: a further invitation between the same parties, for a different scope, is a new instance
- `x.closed` [failure] invitation declined or withdrawn — re-entry: the inviting party may issue a fresh invitation, which is a new instance; a declined one is never revived
- `x.expired` [timeout] expired unanswered — re-entry: a fresh invitation from the same party starts a new window


**Configure.**
- `relationship_invitation.response` · reminder-before-attribute — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The point at which one reminder would still leave time to act before expiry.
- `relationship_invitation.final` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — After the one reminder the instance waits until the invitation's own expiry; there is no second reminder.
- `relationship_invitation.touches` — **recommended: 3** (confidence high; when GLB-24; an invitation, one reminder and a confirmation) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `relationship_invitation.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the invitation; a later instance concerns a different the invitation and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `relationship_invitation_issued` (an invitation recorded by a party holding the authority to extend it; a named counterparty, the scope of the proposed link and its direction; an expiry on the invitation); `invitation_accepted` (the counterparty accepts the invitation); `invitation_declined` (the counterparty declines the invitation); `invitation_withdrawn` (the inviting party withdraws the invitation). Attributes: `invitation_id`, `inviting_party`, `counterparty`, `scope`, `direction`, `expires_at`, `counterparty_known`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 3 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.active, x.closed, x.expired · business outcome: `invitation_accepted` (the counterparty accepts the invitation) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** invitation reminder · invite to link accounts · counterparty invitation · team invite reminder · pending invitation

## TRM-106 · Account Closure

Customer journey · category terminal · slug `account-closure-request`

**Objective.** End an account relationship once the obligations that legitimately block it are resolved, and end nothing else.

**Trigger.** `account_closure_requested` (a closure request against an identified account) [authoritative]. Requires: a closure request against an identified account. Not enough on its own: a cancelled subscription, which ends a commercial relationship rather than the account; a data deletion request, which is a different lifecycle with a different authority; a long period of inactivity.

**Entity.** the account and this closure request — instance `account_id + closure_request_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a closure request against an identified account
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the account and this closure request
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Account closure is not data deletion.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Account closure is not subscription cancellation unless a contract explicitly couples them.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Closure does not silently erase audit or history.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A blocker is named specifically. An unexplained refusal is not a blocker, it is a support case.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **notify-unauthorized** (`a.notify-unauthorized`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does the requester hold authority to close this account?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell the requester that no closure took place and why this request cannot perform it, without disclosing account state they are not entitled to.
2. **notify-cannot-close** (`a.notify-cannot-close`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does the requester hold authority to close this account? · Is closure permitted now?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Name the state that prevents closure, without inventing a route around it.
3. **confirm-closure** (`a.confirm-closure`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does the requester hold authority to close this account? · Is closure permitted now? · Did the closure take effect?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm the account relationship is closed and state plainly what closure is not - it is not subscription cancellation and it is not data deletion, both of which have their own lifecycles and their own evidence.
4. **surface** (`a.surface`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does the requester hold authority to close this account? · Is closure permitted now?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Surface the exact blocker.
   - destination: closure-blocker bound to `closure_request_id`; must not claim: a route around the blocker that does not exist

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.unauthorized` [invalid-state] closure not requested by an authorised party; account unchanged — re-entry: a request from an authorised party is assessed on its own terms
- `x.still-blocked` [failure] one blocker cleared, others remain and are surfaced — re-entry: each remaining blocker is named and resolved on its own, and closure is requested again - stacking them into one message tells the account holder nothing they can act on
- `x.lapsed` [timeout] closure request lapsed with blockers outstanding — re-entry: a fresh request re-enters against whatever blockers still stand
- `x.cannot-close` [failure] closure not permitted — re-entry: the preventing condition changing re-opens this. The account stays open and the reason is on record rather than left as an unexplained refusal
- handoff `h.dependencies` → TRM-107 (Account Closure Reconciliation) — a closure that has taken effect on the account itself
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a closure that did not fully apply

**Configure.**
- `account_closure.blockers` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The closure request's validity window.
- `account_closure.touches` — **recommended: 0** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch in this plan is the closure process itself and is mandatory; nothing discretionary exists to cap.
- `account_closure.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the account and this closure request; a later instance concerns a different the account and this closure request and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `account_closure_requested` (a closure request against an identified account); `closure_blocker_resolved` (the blocker that prevented closure is resolved). Attributes: `account_id`, `closure_request_id`, `requester`, `requester_authority`, `blockers`, `closure_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default transactional · pressure class none · local cap 0 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.unauthorized, x.still-blocked, x.lapsed, x.cannot-close, h.dependencies, h.escalate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** account closure · close account · close my account request · account termination request
