## TRM-275 · Data Deletion Confirmation

Customer journey · category terminal · slug `deletion-request-confirmation`

**Objective.** Give the person who asked for their data to be removed a durable record of what went, what stayed, under which obligation, and the date the request closed - because a deletion nobody can point to is indistinguishable from one that never happened.

**Trigger.** `data_deletion_requested_by_subject` (a deletion request recorded against a named data subject; the scope the request itself names; the response window the governing obligation sets) [declared]. Requires: a deletion request recorded against a named data subject; the scope the request itself names; the response window the governing obligation sets. Not enough on its own: a closure of the relationship; a request to stop receiving messages; a support conversation that mentions deletion.

**Entity.** the data subject and the single deletion request they raised, bounded by the scope that request names — instance `deletion_request_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a deletion request recorded against a named data subject
- the scope the request itself names
- the response window the governing obligation sets
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the data subject and the single deletion request they raised
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Nothing is deleted on an unverified request, and nothing is asked for beyond the proof of control the obligation actually requires.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Ending the relationship is not a deletion request and is never answered as one.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No retention is described that an authoritative obligation does not require. Where the policy is silent, nothing is claimed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The response window belongs to the obligation, not to the work. It is answered inside it or escalated at it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The closing message is a record: scope, outcome, obligation, date - readable a year later by somebody who was not there.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **acknowledge** (`a.acknowledge`) — on classification, no wait
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Acknowledge the request, state the date by which the obligation requires an answer, and say that establishing who is asking comes first.
2. **verify** (`a.verify`, after t1) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the requester's control of the named scope already established?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Ask for exactly the proof of control the obligation requires and nothing beyond it.
   - destination: proof-of-control bound to `deletion_request_id`; must not claim: a need for identifying data beyond what the obligation requires
3. **unverified** (`a.unverified`, after t2) — configure deletion_request.verify after the previous touch; cancelled by `proof_of_control_provided` (sufficient proof of control or identity is provided); re-read before sending: the the data subject and the single deletion request they raised re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Close the request as unverified, saying plainly that nothing was deleted, why, and that a fresh request can be raised at any time.
4. **closed-full** (`a.closed-full`) — configure deletion_request.decision relative to `response_deadline_at`; cancelled by `deletion_resolved` (scope, retained items and deletion outcome are all resolved); re-read before sending: the the data subject and the single deletion request they raised re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What does the resolved outcome actually say?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm what was deleted, the scope it covered and the date the request closed.
5. **closed-partial** (`a.closed-partial`) — configure deletion_request.decision relative to `response_deadline_at`; cancelled by `deletion_resolved` (scope, retained items and deletion outcome are all resolved); re-read before sending: the the data subject and the single deletion request they raised re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What does the resolved outcome actually say?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: State what was deleted, what is retained, which obligation requires it and when that obligation ends.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.unverified` [invalid-state] closed unverified, nothing deleted — re-entry: a fresh request from the same person is a new instance with its own window and its own verification
- `x.closed` [success] answered and closed on the record — re-entry: a later deletion request from the same person is a new instance, verified again from the start
- handoff `h.overdue` → external:data-protection-escalation — a deletion request that reaches its mandated response window with no authoritative outcome to state · contract: person_id, handed_at, reason

**Configure.**
- `deletion_request.verify` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The verification period held inside the mandated response window.
- `deletion_request.decision` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The resolution is waited for until the response deadline the governing obligation sets; reaching it unanswered escalates to data-protection ownership.
- `deletion_request.touches` — **recommended: 0** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch in this plan is the request's own record and is mandatory; nothing discretionary exists to cap.
- `deletion_request.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the data subject and the single deletion request they raised; a later instance concerns a different the data subject and the single deletion request they raised and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `data_deletion_requested_by_subject` (a deletion request recorded against a named data subject; the scope the request itself names; the response window the governing obligation sets); `proof_of_control_provided` (sufficient proof of control or identity is provided); `deletion_resolved` (scope, retained items and deletion outcome are all resolved). Attributes: `deletion_request_id`, `data_subject_id`, `requested_scope`, `response_deadline_at`, `governing_obligation`, `control_established`.

**Collision & priority.** default transactional · pressure class none · local cap 0 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.unverified, x.closed, h.overdue · business outcome: `deletion_resolved` (scope, retained items and deletion outcome are all resolved) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution entered-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** data deletion confirmation · right to erasure · data deletion request · delete my data · data subject request

## INT-269 · Integration Recovery

Customer journey · category integration · slug `integration-reconnect-prompt`

**Objective.** Tell the person who owns a broken connection which of their capabilities stopped, whether the fix is theirs to perform, and what one step restores it - so a silent dependency failure becomes a decision instead of a slow discovery.

**Trigger.** `integration_connection_failed_or_deauthorized` (an authoritative failed or disconnected state recorded against a named connection; a diagnosed failure cause; the list of capabilities that depended on it) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative failed or disconnected state recorded against a named connection; a diagnosed failure cause; the list of capabilities that depended on it. Not enough on its own: a single failed call that has not been classified; a provider rate limit that clears itself; a scheduled provider maintenance window.

**Entity.** one connection to one external provider, plus the failure recorded against it — instance `connection_id + failure_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative failed or disconnected state recorded against a named connection
- a diagnosed failure cause
- the list of capabilities that depended on it
- no instance of this journey is already open for the one connection to one external provider
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Nothing is sent before the failure has a diagnosed cause. A notice that cannot say what to do is a notice that generates the question it should have answered.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A reduced authorization disables what depended on it, never the whole connection.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Where the holder cannot perform the fix, no reconnection step is attached.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Restoration is confirmed capability by capability, because a partial recovery and a full one are told apart nowhere else.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **inform-only** (`a.inform-only`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the fix the holder's to perform?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say which capabilities have stopped and that nothing is required from them, with no reconnection step attached - because there is no step that would work.
2. **partial** (`a.partial`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the fix the holder's to perform? · How much of the connection is affected?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Name exactly which capabilities stopped, which are still running, and the single re-authorisation step.
   - destination: re-authorisation-step bound to `connection_id`; must not claim: a reduced authorisation as a full one
3. **total** (`a.total`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the fix the holder's to perform? · How much of the connection is affected?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say the connection is carrying nothing at present, name what has stopped depending on it, and give the single re-authorisation step.
   - destination: re-authorisation-step bound to `connection_id`
4. **restored** (`a.restored`) — configure integration_reconnect.revalidate after the previous touch; cancelled by `connection_revalidated` (the connection revalidates against a new authorization), `connection_removed` (the holder removes the connection), `cause_reclassified_not_holders` (the failure is reclassified as not the holder's to fix); re-read before sending: the one connection to one external provider re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What ended the wait?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm which capabilities are working again and name any that came back reduced.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.informed` [success] informed, recovery not the holder's to perform — re-entry: if the cause is later reclassified as holder-fixable, this qualifies again on the re-authorisation path
- `x.restored` [success] reconnected and confirmed — re-entry: a later break on the same connection is a new instance
- `x.removed` [invalid-state] connection deliberately removed by the holder — re-entry: connecting the same provider again is a new connection and enters through activation, not through this journey
- `x.unreconnected` [timeout] recovery window closed, connection still broken, relationship not labelled at risk — re-entry: a further disconnection event, or a re-authorisation attempt, opens recovery again
- handoff `h.abandoned` → RET-24 (Churn Risk Escalation) — a connection the holder could re-authorise and has not, past the point where the break is still being treated as an interruption

**Configure.**
- `integration_reconnect.revalidate` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The re-authorisation step is left open for a bounded response window from the notice; a connection still broken past it is judged against independent relationship evidence, never labelled from this break alone.
- `integration_reconnect.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; one notice and one confirmation) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `integration_reconnect.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per one connection to one external provider; a later instance concerns a different one connection to one external provider and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `integration_connection_failed_or_deauthorized` (an authoritative failed or disconnected state recorded against a named connection; a diagnosed failure cause; the list of capabilities that depended on it); `connection_revalidated` (the connection revalidates against a new authorization); `connection_removed` (the holder removes the connection); `cause_reclassified_not_holders` (the failure is reclassified as not the holder's to fix). Attributes: `connection_id`, `failure_id`, `provider`, `holder_id`, `failure_cause`, `dependent_capabilities`, `reauthorisation_step`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.informed, x.restored, x.removed, x.unreconnected, h.abandoned · business outcome: `connection_revalidated` (the connection revalidates against a new authorization) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** integration recovery · reconnect integration · integration disconnected · re-authorise connection · broken integration notice

## INT-278 · Integration Setup

Customer journey · category integration · slug `integration-setup-outcome`

**Objective.** Tell the person who started a connection which stage it actually failed at and what would fix that stage - because a generic failure sends a capable person to support and an incapable one away for good.

**Trigger.** `integration_connection_attempt_resolved` (a connection attempt recorded against a named integration by a named configurer; a recorded outcome from the authentication, scope and capability checks) [authoritative]. Requires: a connection attempt recorded against a named integration by a named configurer; a recorded outcome from the authentication, scope and capability checks. Not enough on its own: an integration browsed or selected; a credential entered but never submitted.

**Entity.** the single connection attempt and the integration it configures — instance `connection_attempt_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a connection attempt recorded against a named integration by a named configurer
- a recorded outcome from the authentication, scope and capability checks
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the single connection attempt and the integration it configures
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The stage that failed is named, or the failure is admitted as unidentified. A generic error is a support ticket with extra steps.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A saved credential is never described as a connection.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The message states the capabilities that were validated, never the ones that were configured.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No fix is suggested that the configurer cannot perform. Where the cause sits with the provider, the message says so.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One message per named failure. A fix already given is not repeated on the next attempt.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **active** (`a.active`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: How far did the attempt get?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm the connection is active and name the capabilities that were actually validated rather than the ones that were configured.
2. **fix-auth** (`a.fix-auth`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: How far did the attempt get? · Which stage failed?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say the credential itself was refused and give the one step that re-establishes it.
   - destination: re-enter-credential bound to `connection_attempt_id`
3. **fix-scope** (`a.fix-scope`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: How far did the attempt get? · Which stage failed?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Name the specific permission that is missing and where it is granted.
   - destination: grant-missing-permission bound to `connection_attempt_id`
4. **fix-capability** (`a.fix-capability`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: How far did the attempt get? · Which stage failed?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say that access was granted but the minimum operation could not be performed, and name which operation.
   - destination: capability-check bound to `connection_attempt_id`; must not claim: that a saved credential is a connection
5. **generic** (`a.generic`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: How far did the attempt get?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say the attempt failed, that the stage has not been identified, and that nothing further is needed from the configurer while it is looked at.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.active` [success] active, with validated capabilities stated — re-entry: a further connection to the same provider is a new instance
- `x.unresolved` [failure] still not connected after the named fix — re-entry: a later attempt re-enters at the outcome check; a fix already given is not sent twice
- `x.abandoned` [timeout] abandoned before the connection was ever made — re-entry: a new attempt on the same integration starts a fresh instance
- handoff `h.diagnose` → INT-111 (Integration Activation) — a failed connection attempt with no stage authoritatively identified

**Configure.**
- `integration_setup.fix_auth_budget` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — This loop runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; when it is spent the instance takes its timeout path (GLB-24).
- `integration_setup.fix_scope_budget` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — This loop runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; when it is spent the instance takes its timeout path (GLB-24).
- `integration_setup.fix_capability_budget` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — This loop runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; when it is spent the instance takes its timeout path (GLB-24).
- `integration_setup.retry` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The period beyond which an unfinished connection is treated as abandoned.
- `integration_setup.touches` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — Stage messages are repeated only as retries recur, against a total budget fixed at the first attempt; the budget is the company's, and the plan never repeats a stage message without a new attempt behind it.
- `integration_setup.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the single connection attempt and the integration it configures; a later instance concerns a different the single connection attempt and the integration it configures and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `integration_connection_attempt_resolved` (a connection attempt recorded against a named integration by a named configurer; a recorded outcome from the authentication, scope and capability checks); `connection_attempt_recorded` (a further connection attempt is recorded); `connection_removed` (the holder removes the connection). Attributes: `connection_attempt_id`, `integration_id`, `configurer_id`, `stage_outcomes`, `validated_capabilities`, `retry_budget`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap configure integration_setup.touches (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.active, x.unresolved, x.abandoned, h.diagnose · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** integration setup · connect integration · integration failed at setup · connection failed · integration onboarding

## FIN-134 · Payment Failure Recovery

Customer journey · category financial · slug `payment-failure-recovery`

**Objective.** Get the obligation paid by responding to the failure that actually happened, while the obligation stays alive and the relationship's own state is decided elsewhere.

**Trigger.** `authoritative_payment_failure` (a payment system stating that the attempt failed, with whatever reason it gave) [authoritative]. Requires: a payment system stating that the attempt failed, with whatever reason it gave. Not enough on its own: a timeout, which establishes nothing about whether money moved.

**Entity.** the failed attempt, the obligation behind it and the customer relationship it belongs to — instance `obligation_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- the obligation is still outstanding in the system of record
- the failure class is established from the provider's own response - temporary, customer-fixable, or declined with or without a usable reason - before anything is retried or said
- no recovery instance is already open for this obligation; a second failure on the same obligation is an event inside the open instance (GLB-19)
- the relationship has not been terminated
- hard gates (GLB-31) permit obligation communication to this person - a closed account still receives it, a fraud or security hold does not

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Exit the moment the obligation is satisfied, cancelled or waived by any means. A recovery message about a paid obligation is the failure this journey exists to prevent.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No corrective request is sent while a system-side retry is scheduled and may still succeed. The failure class decides: a temporary failure retries silently first.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No corrective request is sent for a decline whose reason cannot be turned into an instruction the customer can act on; the alternate-route path applies instead.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An attempt whose outcome is unknown is reconciled first (FIN-135, GLB-20). Unknown is not failed, and nothing is sent or retried on it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Hard gates (GLB-31) apply. Pressure caps do not: this is transactional communication about an obligation the person already holds, and it is deduplicated by obligation and touch rather than rationed.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **corrective-request** (`a.corrective`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: What kind of failure was it?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person has an active session in the product - the corrective action is a form, and the shortest route to it is inside the product) → persistent (email: no active session, or the instruction and its link have to survive until the person can act)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Name the exact corrective action - update the method, complete the authentication, choose another method - and what is owed. Provider risk detail and internal decline codes are never shown.
   - destination: payment-method-update bound to `obligation_id`; must not claim: the reason the provider gave, beyond its class, that the obligation is waived, reduced or extended
2. **offer-alternate** (`a.offer-alternate`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: What kind of failure was it? · Is an alternative valid payment route available, and whose decision is it?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person has an active session in the product - the corrective action is a form, and the shortest route to it is inside the product) → persistent (email: no active session, or the instruction and its link have to survive until the person can act)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Put the available alternative methods in front of the customer and ask which to use, stating that the obligation stands either way. Choosing a payment method is theirs to make.
   - destination: payment-method-choice bound to `obligation_id`; must not claim: that a method was chosen for them
3. **reminder** (`a.remind`, after t1) — 2 days–3 days (example) relative to `consequence_date`; cancelled by `obligation_satisfied` (the obligation is satisfied in the system of record), `recovery_attempt_abandoned` (the holder stops pursuing the route back); re-read before sending: the obligation is still outstanding and the method is still invalid, read from the system of record immediately before the reminder
   - checks: Is a reminder still useful, and is there a consequence to name?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: no active session, or the instruction and its link have to survive until the person can act) → urgent (sms/push: an asserted consequence date exists inside the urgent horizon and permission for service messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority transactional · [OPTIONAL_STRATEGY]
   - purpose: One reminder naming the consequence and its date, with the same corrective action. It is not repeated.
   - destination: payment-method-update bound to `obligation_id`; must not claim: the reason the provider gave, beyond its class
4. **confirmation** (`a.confirmed`, after t1) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: How did recovery end?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: no active session, or the instruction and its link have to survive until the person can act) → in-session (in-app: the person has an active session in the product - the corrective action is a form, and the shortest route to it is inside the product)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm the obligation is discharged and that nothing further is expected. An obligation met and never acknowledged is one the person keeps checking.
   - destination: obligation-receipt bound to `obligation_id`

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.resolved, s.retry-in-progress, s.no-instruction, s.unknown-outcome, s.hard-gates (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.recovered` [success] obligation satisfied through recovery — re-entry: a future failure against a future obligation is its own instance
- handoff `h.grace` → TIM-65 (Grace Period Management) — an unpaid obligation entering a grace period · contract: obligation_id, amount_outstanding, failure_class, attempts, consequence_policy
- handoff `h.overdue` → TIM-62 (Overdue State Recalculation) — an unpaid obligation becoming overdue · contract: obligation_id, amount_outstanding, failure_class, attempts, consequence_policy
- handoff `h.restrict` → ACC-78 (Access Suspension) — policy restricting capability while an obligation is unpaid · contract: obligation_id, amount_outstanding, failure_class, attempts, consequence_policy, restriction_scope

**Configure.**
- `payment.alternate_choice_window` · response-window — **example: 3 days–7 days** (confidence low; avoid when a consequence date is sooner - the choice window ends at the consequence date) — The customer's choice of method is theirs to make and is waited for. An unanswered choice is not a refusal to pay, and the obligation goes on to its consequence rather than being treated as declined.
- `payment.reminder_point` · reminder-before-attribute — **example: 2 days–3 days** (confidence low; when an asserted consequence date exists for this obligation; avoid when no consequence date - the recovery window is the only bound) — One reminder, placed so the customer can still act before the consequence date. Where no consequence date exists the reminder point is the recovery window itself and the reminder is skipped.
- `payment.recovery_window` · recovery-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The recovery window is the obligation class's own grace or consequence policy. Its end is the consequence, owned by the grace, overdue or restriction lifecycle - never an extension invented here.
- `payment.discretionary_touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence medium; when GLB-24: every reminder runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened) — Only discretionary messages count against the cap. The corrective request and the discharge confirmation are obligations, not touches to ration.
- `payment.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: a future failure against a future obligation is its own instance) — Recovery is per obligation. A later failure against a later obligation is its own instance, and no cooldown applies between obligations.

**Required data.** Events: `authoritative_payment_failure` (a payment system stating that the attempt failed, with whatever reason it gave); `alternate_method_selected` (the customer selects or authorises an alternative payment method); `obligation_satisfied` (the obligation is satisfied in the system of record); `recovery_attempt_abandoned` (the holder stops pursuing the route back). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `person_id`, `amount_outstanding`, `currency`, `failure_class`, `failed_at`, `method_id`, `alternate_methods`, `consequence_date` (optional: `provider_reason_code`, `grace_policy_id`, `has_active_session`).

**Collision & priority.** default transactional · pressure class none · local cap 1 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.recovered, h.grace, h.overdue, h.restrict · business outcome: `obligation_satisfied` (the obligation is satisfied in the system of record) per instance, observed through TIM-65 until `grace_period_ended` (the grace period recorded for a recoverable state ends), attribution entered-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, support_contact_within_24h, duplicate_charge, message_after_success

**Aliases.** dunning · failed payment recovery · card decline recovery · involuntary churn · payment retry

## FIN-137 · Refund Request

Customer journey · category financial · slug `refund-request-decision`

**Objective.** Turn a refund request into an authorised decision, without money moving on the request itself.

**Trigger.** `refund_requested` (a refund requested against an identified original transaction) [declared]. Requires: a refund requested against an identified original transaction. Not enough on its own: dissatisfaction expressed about a purchase, which is feedback until it is made as a request.

**Entity.** the refund request and the original transaction it is made against — instance `refund_request_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a refund requested against an identified original transaction
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the refund request and the original transaction it is made against
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A refund requested is not a refund owed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A refund request does not modify the original payment history.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Eligibility policy is never invented. Where none exists the decision goes to a person.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **reject-scope** (`a.reject-scope`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the request valid and within the process scope?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Reject with the actual reason and whatever process does apply.
2. **notify-rejection** (`a.notify-rejection`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the request valid and within the process scope? · Is a refund eligibility policy defined for this transaction type? · What does the policy determine?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell the requester the refund was refused and the governing reason, whether that came from policy directly or from a reviewer.
3. **acknowledge-review** (`a.acknowledge-review`, after t4) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the request valid and within the process scope? · Is a refund eligibility policy defined for this transaction type? · What does the policy determine?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell the requester the refund is under review and what that state means, without implying an outcome.
4. **review** (`a.review`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the request valid and within the process scope? · Is a refund eligibility policy defined for this transaction type? · What does the policy determine?
   - channel roles, in order: human (task: the step is carried out by a person - a call, a task, a visit - and recorded as done by them)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Record UNDER_REVIEW and gather whatever the decision requires.
5. **approve** (`a.approve`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the request valid and within the process scope? · Is a refund eligibility policy defined for this transaction type? · What does the policy determine?
   - channel roles, in order: human (task: the step is carried out by a person - a call, a task, a visit - and recorded as done by them)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Record APPROVED with the authorised amount and the authority that approved it.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.rejected` [failure] REJECTED; no money moves and the original transaction stands — re-entry: a differently grounded request, or new evidence, is assessed on its own. A refund requested is not a refund owed
- handoff `h.undefined` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a refund request with no governing eligibility policy
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a refund decision outliving its SLA
- handoff `h.execute` → FIN-138 (Refund Execution Verification) — a refund authorised for execution

**Configure.**
- `refund_request.decision` · decision-sla — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The decision SLA.
- `refund_request.touches` — **recommended: 0** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch in this plan is the request's own process and is mandatory; nothing discretionary exists to cap.
- `refund_request.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the refund request and the original transaction it is made against; a later instance concerns a different the refund request and the original transaction it is made against and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `refund_requested` (a refund requested against an identified original transaction); `decision_recorded` (an authorised decision is recorded on the request). Attributes: `refund_request_id`, `original_transaction_id`, `requester_id`, `eligibility_policy`, `decision_sla`, `refund_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default transactional · pressure class none · local cap 0 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.rejected, h.undefined, h.escalate, h.execute · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** refund request · refund eligibility · money-back request · refund decision

## FUL-146 · Delivery Delay Alert

Customer journey · category fulfillment · slug `fulfillment-delay`

**Objective.** Hold lateness as its own state, with the original commitment intact behind whatever the new estimate is.

**Trigger.** `expected_fulfillment_timing_slipped` (a material slip against the timing this obligation was committed to) [authoritative]. Requires: a material slip against the timing this obligation was committed to. Not enough on its own: a slip within the tolerance the commitment already allows.

**Entity.** the obligation and its timing commitment, with the history of what was promised — instance `obligation_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a material slip against the timing this obligation was committed to
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the obligation and its timing commitment
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Delayed is not failed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Dates that will not hold are not promised repeatedly. Where no reliable estimate exists, that is what is said.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A changed ETA preserves the original commitment history.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Communicating a delay does not resolve the operational delay.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Once the obligation is handed to a delivery executor, an in-transit slip on the same obligation is FUL-265's delay-or-tracking state to hold and report; this journey does not open a second, competing delay narrative about a slip FUL-265 is already tracking under its own wait.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **delay-update** (`a.delay-update`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is there a reliable new completion estimate? · Does the changed timing alter what the recipient should plan around?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: State the original commitment, the current estimate or the explicit fact that there is not a reliable one, and what is still owed.
2. **no-choice-update** (`a.no-choice-update`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is there a reliable new completion estimate? · Does the changed timing alter what the recipient should plan around? · Does the delay exceed the acceptable threshold? · Does the counterparty have a decision to make?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say that the delay is beyond what was committed, that no option is currently available to them, and that it is being escalated rather than left.
3. **offer** (`a.offer`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is there a reliable new completion estimate? · Does the changed timing alter what the recipient should plan around? · Does the delay exceed the acceptable threshold? · Does the counterparty have a decision to make?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Offer the choices that are actually available - wait, reschedule, an alternative, or cancel.
   - destination: delay-choices bound to `obligation_id`; must not claim: a date that will not hold, a choice that cannot be honoured

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.resume` → FUL-144 (Fulfillment Execution) — a delayed obligation resuming
- handoff `h.exception` → FUL-145 (Fulfillment Exception Recovery) — a counterparty choosing an alternative over waiting
- handoff `h.cancel` → FUL-150 (Fulfillment Cancellation Reconciliation) — a counterparty cancelling rather than waiting
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a delay beyond tolerance with nothing to offer, or outliving its revised horizon

**Configure.**
- `fulfillment_delay.decision` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The decision window.
- `fulfillment_delay.resume` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The revised horizon.
- `fulfillment_delay.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; one delay update and one offer or no-choice update) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `fulfillment_delay.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the obligation and its timing commitment; a later instance concerns a different the obligation and its timing commitment and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `expected_fulfillment_timing_slipped` (a material slip against the timing this obligation was committed to); `choice_made` (the person makes the offered choice); `fulfillment_resumed_or_completed` (fulfilment resumes or completes). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `original_commitment`, `current_estimate`, `tolerance`, `available_choices`, `fulfillment_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.resume, h.exception, h.cancel, h.escalate · business outcome: `fulfillment_resumed_or_completed` (fulfilment resumes or completes) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** delivery delay alert · shipping delay notice · late order notification · ETA change · delay with options

## FUL-148 · Failed Delivery Recovery

Customer journey · category fulfillment · slug `delivery-attempt-failure`

**Objective.** Recover a failed delivery according to why it failed, within a bounded number of attempts.

**Trigger.** `authoritative_delivery_attempt_failure` (a delivery executor confirming an attempt was made and delivery did not occur) [authoritative]. Requires: a delivery executor confirming an attempt was made and delivery did not occur. Not enough on its own: a tracking status that has not advanced, which is silence rather than a failure.

**Entity.** the individual delivery attempt and the obligation it was serving — instance `delivery_attempt_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a delivery executor confirming an attempt was made and delivery did not occur
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the individual delivery attempt and the obligation it was serving
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Attempted is not delivered.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Delivery is not retried indefinitely - the budget is bounded and does not reset.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Refused and unavailable are different outcomes with different consequences.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The failure reason is never invented. What the executor reported is what is acted on.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **correct** (`a.correct`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: What kind of failure was it?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Request the exact correction - the address, the access instruction, the contact.
   - destination: delivery-correction bound to `delivery_attempt_id`
2. **offer-route** (`a.offer-route`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: What kind of failure was it? · Is a reattempt available within the bounded policy? · Would an alternative route serve better than another attempt at the same one?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Put the concrete alternatives in front of the recipient - the collection point, the different window, the other executor - and ask which they want, stating that the attempt budget does not reset either way.
   - destination: delivery-alternatives bound to `delivery_attempt_id`; must not claim: an alternative that is not actually available

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.retry` → FUL-147 (Delivery Outcome Tracking) — a further delivery attempt being dispatched
- handoff `h.return` → REM-151 (Post-Purchase Issue Recovery) — a delivery that cannot be completed - refused, uncorrectable, or out of attempts · contract: issue_id, order_id
- handoff `h.exception` → FUL-145 (Fulfillment Exception Recovery) — an item that arrived damaged

**Configure.**
- `delivery_attempt.correction` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The correction is waited for as long as a reattempt inside the bounded policy is still possible; past it the delivery goes to return.
- `delivery_attempt.route_choice` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The choice of alternative is waited for as long as a reattempt inside the bounded policy is still possible; an unanswered choice reattempts the same route.
- `delivery_attempt.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `delivery_attempt.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the individual delivery attempt and the obligation it was serving; a later instance concerns a different the individual delivery attempt and the obligation it was serving and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `authoritative_delivery_attempt_failure` (a delivery executor confirming an attempt was made and delivery did not occur); `delivery_correction_provided` (the recipient provides the requested correction); `delivery_alternative_selected` (the recipient selects an alternative route); `delivery_alternatives_declined` (the recipient declines every alternative). Attributes: `delivery_attempt_id`, `obligation_id`, `failure_reason`, `attempt_budget`, `alternative_routes`, `delivery_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.retry, h.return, h.exception · business outcome: `delivery_correction_provided` (the recipient provides the requested correction) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** failed delivery recovery · delivery attempt failed · redelivery · missed delivery options · address correction request

## FUL-265 · Delivery Tracking

Customer journey · category fulfillment · slug `dispatch-to-acceptance`

**Objective.** Carry the recipient from the moment execution left our hands to the moment they agree the obligation was discharged correctly - because arriving and being agreed to have arrived correctly are two different facts, and only one of them has a recipient as its source.

**Trigger.** `obligation_handed_to_delivery_executor` (an authoritative dispatch record naming the executor and the destination; a recipient with a permitted route for a service notice) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative dispatch record naming the executor and the destination; a recipient with a permitted route for a service notice. Not enough on its own: a preparation or packing status; a label or reference created with nothing handed over behind it.

**Entity.** the dispatched obligation, its recipient, and the acceptance window running against it — instance `obligation_id + person_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative dispatch record naming the executor and the destination
- a recipient with a permitted route for a service notice
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the dispatched obligation
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Dispatched is not delivered, and delivered is not accepted. Each is told at the point it becomes true and never before.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An intermediate tracking movement is never reported as an outcome.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No acceptance window is invented beyond what policy defines; where none exists, nothing is asked for.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An executor gone quiet is reported as unknown, not as failure.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Acceptance by agreement and acceptance by expiry stay separable forever.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A pre-dispatch slip against the original commitment is FUL-146's delay narrative, not this journey's; this journey's own tracking begins at dispatch and reports what the executor authoritatively confirms from there.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **dispatch** (`a.dispatch`) — on classification, no wait
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say it is on its way, with the expected window and whatever reference genuinely follows it.
2. **no-arrival** (`a.no-arrival`, after t1) — configure dispatch_to.delivery after the previous touch; cancelled by `delivery_confirmed` (delivery is confirmed by the executor or the recipient), `delivery_failed` (the delivery attempt is confirmed failed), `delivery_delay_reported` (the executor reports a meaningful delay); re-read before sending: the the dispatched obligation re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell them it has not arrived and say which of the two it is - a confirmed failure, or an executor we have lost sight of.
3. **arrived** (`a.arrived`, after t1) — configure dispatch_to.delivery after the previous touch; cancelled by `delivery_confirmed` (delivery is confirmed by the executor or the recipient), `delivery_failed` (the delivery attempt is confirmed failed), `delivery_delay_reported` (the executor reports a meaningful delay); re-read before sending: the the dispatched obligation re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What did the executor authoritatively report?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm it arrived and what the evidence for that is.
4. **accept-request** (`a.accept-request`, after t3) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does acceptance carry any consequence here?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Ask them to confirm it arrived correctly or to raise an issue, and name the date after which it is treated as accepted.
   - destination: accept-or-raise-issue bound to `obligation_id`; must not claim: an acceptance window policy does not define

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.delivered` [success] delivered; no acceptance was required — re-entry: a later obligation to the same recipient is a new instance
- `x.accepted` [success] accepted by the recipient — re-entry: rights policy independently provides afterwards do not run through here
- `x.finalized` [timeout] finalised on expiry of the acceptance window, with no explicit acceptance — re-entry: an issue raised later runs on whatever right policy independently provides, not on this window
- handoff `x.unresolved` → FUL-148 (Failed Delivery Recovery) — a confirmed non-arrival, or an executor gone quiet long enough that the obligation needs active recovery rather than a further wait · contract: delivery_attempt_id, obligation_id, failure_reason
- handoff `h.issue` → FUL-149 (Delivery Acceptance Finalization) — a delivered obligation the recipient has raised an issue against inside its acceptance window

**Configure.**
- `dispatch_to.delivery` · external-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The executor's own expected window, from the dispatch record; its passing without an authoritative report is a non-arrival to be reconciled, never assumed delivered.
- `dispatch_to.acceptance` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The acceptance window policy defines.
- `dispatch_to.touches` — **recommended: 3** (confidence high; when GLB-24; a dispatch notice, an arrival or non-arrival notice, and an acceptance request) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `dispatch_to.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the dispatched obligation; a later instance concerns a different the dispatched obligation and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `obligation_handed_to_delivery_executor` (an authoritative dispatch record naming the executor and the destination; a recipient with a permitted route for a service notice); `delivery_confirmed` (delivery is confirmed by the executor or the recipient); `delivery_failed` (the delivery attempt is confirmed failed); `delivery_delay_reported` (the executor reports a meaningful delay); `delivery_accepted` (the recipient accepts what was delivered); `delivery_issue_raised` (the recipient raises an issue about the delivery). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `person_id`, `executor`, `expected_window`, `tracking_reference`, `acceptance_window_ends_at`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 3 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.unresolved, x.delivered, x.accepted, x.finalized, h.issue · business outcome: `delivery_accepted` (the recipient accepts what was delivered) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** delivery tracking · shipping notification · order shipped · out for delivery · delivery confirmation and acceptance

## FUL-276 · Substitution Approval

Customer journey · category fulfillment · slug `substitution-offer`

**Objective.** Put a defined alternative in front of the person the obligation was made to, with a real decline path and a stated deadline, so that nothing different is ever supplied on the assumption they would not have minded.

**Trigger.** `substitution_required_with_defined_alternative` (an authoritative exception recorded against a named scope of the obligation; a specific alternative identified and actually available; the difference between what was promised and what would be supplied instead) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative exception recorded against a named scope of the obligation; a specific alternative identified and actually available; the difference between what was promised and what would be supplied instead. Not enough on its own: a delay with no alternative identified; a supply warning that has not yet touched this obligation.

**Entity.** the affected scope of the obligation and the single alternative offered against it — instance `obligation_id + substitution_offer_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative exception recorded against a named scope of the obligation
- a specific alternative identified and actually available
- the difference between what was promised and what would be supplied instead
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the affected scope of the obligation and the single alternative offered against it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Nothing different is supplied on the strength of silence. Only an explicit acceptance moves the obligation.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The decline path sits in the same message as the accept path and costs the same effort to take.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A decline is not a cancellation. The affected scope remains open and owed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Scope already fulfilled is untouched. The offer covers only what the exception actually affects.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One reminder before the window closes, never two.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **offer** (`a.offer`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is there a permitted route that reaches them in time to decide?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: State what cannot be supplied, name the one alternative and the difference in plain terms, and give explicit accept and decline paths with the date the offer ends.
   - destination: accept-or-decline-substitute bound to `substitution_offer_id`; must not claim: that silence means acceptance
2. **remind** (`a.remind`, after t1) — configure substitution_offer.decision relative to `offer_closes_at`; cancelled by `substitute_approved` (the substitute is approved), `substitute_declined` (the substitute is declined); re-read before sending: the the affected scope of the obligation and the single alternative offered against it re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Send one reminder naming the same alternative, the same two paths and the exact date the offer closes.
   - destination: accept-or-decline-substitute bound to `substitution_offer_id`; must not claim: a moved closing date
3. **confirm-accept** (`a.confirm-accept`) — configure substitution_offer.decision relative to `offer_closes_at`; cancelled by `substitute_approved` (the substitute is approved), `substitute_declined` (the substitute is declined); re-read before sending: the the affected scope of the obligation and the single alternative offered against it re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What came back?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm what will now be supplied, on what terms, and what is unchanged about the rest of the obligation.
4. **confirm-decline** (`a.confirm-decline`) — configure substitution_offer.decision relative to `offer_closes_at`; cancelled by `substitute_approved` (the substitute is approved), `substitute_declined` (the substitute is declined); re-read before sending: the the affected scope of the obligation and the single alternative offered against it re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What came back?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm the decline, say that the obligation stays open and unfulfilled for the affected scope, and name what happens to it next.
5. **lapse** (`a.lapse`) — configure substitution_offer.final relative to `offer_closes_at`; cancelled by `substitute_approved` (the substitute is approved), `substitute_declined` (the substitute is declined); re-read before sending: the the affected scope of the obligation and the single alternative offered against it re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Close the offer, release the alternative and say plainly that nothing was substituted and the affected scope is still open.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.accepted` [success] alternative accepted and confirmed — re-entry: a further exception on the same obligation is a new instance with its own offer
- `x.declined` [failure] alternative declined, affected scope still owed — re-entry: a different alternative found later is a new offer
- `x.lapsed` [timeout] offer lapsed undecided, affected scope still open — re-entry: a new alternative identified later starts a new offer
- handoff `h.unreachable` → CON-36 (Contactability Recalculation) — a substitution offer that cannot be delivered on any permitted route before its window closes

**Configure.**
- `substitution_offer.decision` · reminder-before-attribute — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The point in the window at which one reminder can still be acted on.
- `substitution_offer.final` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — After the reminder the offer stays open until its stated closing date and no longer; silence at that date lapses it and nothing is substituted.
- `substitution_offer.touches` — **recommended: 3** (confidence high; when GLB-24; an offer, one reminder and one closing message) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `substitution_offer.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the affected scope of the obligation and the single alternative offered against it; a later instance concerns a different the affected scope of the obligation and the single alternative offered against it and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `substitution_required_with_defined_alternative` (an authoritative exception recorded against a named scope of the obligation; a specific alternative identified and actually available; the difference between what was promised and what would be supplied instead); `substitute_approved` (the substitute is approved); `substitute_declined` (the substitute is declined). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `substitution_offer_id`, `affected_scope`, `alternative`, `difference_statement`, `offer_closes_at`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 3 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.accepted, x.declined, x.lapsed, h.unreachable · business outcome: `substitute_approved` (the substitute is approved) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** substitution approval · substitute item offer · replacement item consent · out-of-stock substitution

## REM-151 · Post-Purchase Issue Recovery

Customer journey · category remedy · slug `post-completion-issue`

**Objective.** Establish whether something delivered has left an obligation unresolved, and which recovery mechanism could satisfy it.

**Trigger.** `post_completion_issue_reported` (a concrete problem with a completed fulfillment or service: a wrong item or result, damaged output, a missing component, a quality problem, a service defect, an incorrect configuration or an incomplete outcome) [declared]. Requires: a concrete problem with a completed fulfillment or service: a wrong item or result, damaged output, a missing component, a quality problem, a service defect, an incorrect configuration or an incomplete outcome. Not enough on its own: negative feedback about the experience, which reports a feeling rather than naming a problem with what was delivered; a low satisfaction score.

**Entity.** the completed fulfillment or service, and the specific problem reported against it — instance `issue_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a concrete problem with a completed fulfillment or service: a wrong item or result, damaged output, a missing component, a quality problem, a service defect, an incorrect configuration or an incomplete outcome
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the completed fulfillment or service
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An issue reported is not a confirmed defect.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Negative feedback alone does not establish remedy eligibility.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Not every issue defaults to a refund.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An existing case covering the same obligation suppresses a second recovery lifecycle.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `single-notice`

1. **acknowledge** (`a.acknowledge`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does an existing recovery case already cover this problem? · Is there an actionable unresolved obligation?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Acknowledge and explain, closing according to policy.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.attached` [suppression] attached to the existing recovery case — re-entry: if that case closes with the problem still present, the recurrence is assessed on its own terms rather than as a fresh report
- `x.no-defect` [no-action] heard; no unresolved obligation and no remedy owed — re-entry: new evidence of an actual defect re-opens this. Repetition of the same report is itself worth reading, without becoming a defect by repetition
- handoff `h.remedy` → REM-157 (Remedy Confirmation) — a confirmed unresolved obligation needing a remedy decision

**Configure.**
- `post_completion.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `post_completion.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the completed fulfillment or service; a later instance concerns a different the completed fulfillment or service and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `post_completion_issue_reported` (a concrete problem with a completed fulfillment or service: a wrong item or result, damaged output, a missing component, a quality problem, a service defect, an incorrect configuration or an incomplete outcome). Attributes: `issue_id`, `order_id`, `reported_problem`, `existing_case_ref`, `assessment`, `issue_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 1 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.attached, x.no-defect, h.remedy · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** post-purchase issue recovery · order problem report · wrong item received · damaged on arrival · post-delivery complaint

## REM-152 · Return Request

Customer journey · category remedy · slug `return-authorization`

**Objective.** Decide whether something may enter a return process, as a decision separate from whether money is owed.

**Trigger.** `return_requested` (a request to send back an identified item, resource or deliverable) [declared]. Requires: a request to send back an identified item, resource or deliverable. Not enough on its own: an expression of dissatisfaction, which is a report rather than a request to return anything.

**Entity.** the return request and the original fulfillment it concerns — instance `return_request_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a request to send back an identified item, resource or deliverable
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the return request and the original fulfillment it concerns
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A return requested is not a return authorized.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Return eligibility rules are never invented.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Refund eligibility and return eligibility may be different decisions with different answers.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **notify-rejection** (`a.notify-rejection`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does a return process apply to this at all? · Is a return eligibility policy defined for this? · What does the policy determine?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell the requester the return was refused and the governing reason, whether policy ruled it out directly or a reviewer did.
2. **notify-authorization** (`a.notify-authorization`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does a return process apply to this at all? · Is a return eligibility policy defined for this? · What does the policy determine?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell the requester the return is authorised, within what scope and by what method, and that authorisation permits the resource to come back without deciding that money is owed.
   - destination: return-instructions bound to `return_request_id`; must not claim: that a refund is owed
3. **review** (`a.review`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does a return process apply to this at all? · Is a return eligibility policy defined for this? · What does the policy determine?
   - channel roles, in order: human (task: the step is carried out by a person - a call, a task, a visit - and recorded as done by them)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Record RETURN_UNDER_REVIEW and gather what the decision requires.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.rejected` [failure] RETURN_REJECTED; the original fulfillment is unchanged — re-entry: a return refused does not settle whether another remedy is owed - that question is separate and is asked separately
- handoff `h.alternative` → REM-157 (Remedy Confirmation) — a return requested against something that cannot be returned · contract: issue_id, obligation_id
- handoff `h.undefined` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a return request with no governing eligibility policy
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a return decision outliving its SLA
- handoff `h.transit` → REM-153 (Return Transit Resolution) — an authorised return

**Configure.**
- `return_authorization.decision` · decision-sla — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The decision SLA.
- `return_authorization.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; a review record and one decision notice) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `return_authorization.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the return request and the original fulfillment it concerns; a later instance concerns a different the return request and the original fulfillment it concerns and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `return_requested` (a request to send back an identified item, resource or deliverable); `decision_recorded` (an authorised decision is recorded on the request). Attributes: `return_request_id`, `original_fulfillment_id`, `requester_id`, `eligibility_policy`, `decision_sla`, `return_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.rejected, h.alternative, h.undefined, h.escalate, h.transit · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** return request · return authorisation · RMA · return eligibility · send it back
