## REM-157 · Remedy Confirmation

Customer journey · category remedy · slug `remedy-selection`

**Objective.** Choose the remedy that would actually satisfy the unresolved obligation, from the ones that genuinely exist.

**Trigger.** `remedy_decision_required` (a confirmed issue with an unresolved obligation and no remedy yet selected) [authoritative]. Requires: a confirmed issue with an unresolved obligation and no remedy yet selected. Not enough on its own: a complaint received but not yet established as an operational fault, which is FBK-43's question; a refund request, which presumes the remedy this journey has not yet decided.

**Entity.** the confirmed issue and the unresolved obligation behind it — instance `issue_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a confirmed issue with an unresolved obligation and no remedy yet selected
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the confirmed issue and the unresolved obligation behind it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A refund is not the universal remedy.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Compensation and resolution may be separate, and resolving the obligation does not require compensating for it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Remedies that are not actually available are not offered.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The selection starts from the unresolved obligation rather than from the complaint.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **present** (`a.present`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does the counterparty choose between remedies?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Present only the options that are genuinely available, with what each would mean
   - destination: remedy-options bound to `issue_id`; must not claim: a remedy that is not actually available
2. **no-remedy** (`a.no-remedy`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does the counterparty choose between remedies? · Which remedy resolves the obligation?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: State that the obligation is considered satisfied, or that policy provides no remedy for the facts as confirmed, and name a separate appeal or escalation route only where one actually exists.
   - destination: appeal-or-escalation-route bound to `issue_id`

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.no-remedy` [success] no remedy owed; the obligation is satisfied or none applies — re-entry: new evidence about the obligation re-opens this. Compensation for impact, if any is appropriate, is a separate decision that this outcome does not settle either way
- handoff `h.correction` → REM-156 (Corrective Reperformance) — correction or reperformance selected
- handoff `h.replacement` → REM-155 (Replacement Fulfillment) — replacement selected
- handoff `h.return` → REM-152 (Return Request) — a return required before the remedy can be settled
- handoff `h.financial` → FIN-137 (Refund Request) — a refund or credit selected as the remedy

**Configure.**
- `remedy_selection.selection` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The choice of remedy is waited for a bounded period from the options being presented; an unanswered choice takes the policy default.
- `remedy_selection.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `remedy_selection.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the confirmed issue and the unresolved obligation behind it; a later instance concerns a different the confirmed issue and the unresolved obligation behind it and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `remedy_decision_required` (a confirmed issue with an unresolved obligation and no remedy yet selected); `remedy_selected` (the person selects a remedy). Attributes: `issue_id`, `obligation_id`, `available_remedies`, `counterparty_chooses`, `remedy_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.no-remedy, h.correction, h.replacement, h.return, h.financial · business outcome: `remedy_selected` (the person selects a remedy) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** remedy confirmation · remedy selection · choose a remedy · resolution options · refund or replacement choice

## SUB-163 · Renewal Reminder

Customer journey · category subscription · slug `renewal-decision`

**Objective.** Bring a renewal cycle to a recorded decision before the notice deadline: give the notice the terms require, put the decision to whoever holds it where one is needed, and apply what the terms say when none is made.

**Trigger.** `renewal_decision_window_opens` (a relationship whose term end is inside the renewal decision window its governing terms define) [authoritative]. Requires: a relationship whose term end is inside the renewal decision window its governing terms define. Not enough on its own: a renewal reminder having been sent, which is a communication and not a decision window opening.

**Entity.** the continuing relationship and the renewal cycle currently open on it — instance `relationship_id + renewal_cycle_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a renewal decision window has opened on a continuing relationship with a current term
- the renewal terms, model and required notice are defined - or the cycle goes to decision resolution first
- no renewal instance is already open for this cycle
- no open payment recovery process exists on the relationship - payment recovery owns the relationship until it resolves

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A window with no defined notice period or renewing terms goes to decision resolution (DEC-181); nothing is asked or noticed on undefined terms.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An outstanding blocker puts the cycle in review; the relationship stays active on its current term throughout and no decision is requested until the blocker is settled.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A recorded, authorised decision ends asking; nothing further is sent by this journey once the decision exists.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Putting the decision to its holder is not deciding; a non-response is resolved by what the governing terms define, never assumed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An open payment recovery process on the relationship suppresses the renewal decision request entirely; a routine renewal ask is not put to someone whose current term is already in question over an unresolved payment failure.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Hard gates (GLB-31) apply; pressure caps do not to the required notice, which is an obligation of the terms rather than outreach.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `deadline-countdown`

1. **required-notice** (`a.notice`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Are the renewal terms and required notice period defined? · Do the governing terms require notice to actually be given?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the notice and the terms must be kept, and are addressed to whoever holds the decision - the default for a renewal) → in-session (in-app: the decision holder is active in the product and the decision is taken there)
   - mandatory · priority transactional (a notice the governing terms require is an obligation of the relationship, not outreach; it is never rationed or deferred) · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Give the notice the terms require: the renewal model that will apply, the terms it renews on, and what happens if no decision is made.
   - destination: renewal-terms bound to `renewal_cycle_id`
2. **decision-request** (`a.request`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does an outstanding blocker prevent the renewal from being decided? · What does the renewal model require?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the notice and the terms must be kept, and are addressed to whoever holds the decision - the default for a renewal) → in-session (in-app: the decision holder is active in the product and the decision is taken there) → urgent (sms/push: the notice deadline is inside the urgent horizon and permission for service messages on the channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Put the renewal decision to whoever holds it, with the terms that would apply and the point by which the notice period requires an answer.
   - destination: renewal-decision bound to `renewal_cycle_id`; must not claim: that a decision has been made

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.undefined-terms, s.blocked, s.decided, s.asking-not-deciding, s.payment-recovery, s.hard-gates (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.undefined` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a renewal window with no defined notice period or renewing terms
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a renewal review outliving the notice period
- handoff `h.execute` → SUB-164 (Renewal Execution) — a renewal decided and ready for execution
- handoff `h.scheduled-end` → SUB-168 (Scheduled Cancellation Revalidation) — a non-renewal scheduling an end at the current term's end

**Configure.**
- `renewal.decision_deadline` · attribute-bound — **recommended: term_end_at minus the notice period the terms require** (confidence high) — The decision is waited for until the last point at which the required notice period still allows one; then the governing terms decide.
- `renewal.notice_deadline` · attribute-bound — **recommended: term_end_at minus the notice period the terms require** (confidence high) — A review that outlives the notice deadline is escalated to ownership; the relationship stays on its current term meanwhile.
- `renewal.discretionary_touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when the graph puts the decision once) — Only the decision request counts against the cap; the notice the terms require is an obligation, not a touch to ration.
- `renewal.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high) — Renewal is per cycle; the next cycle is its own instance and no cooldown applies between cycles.

**Required data.** Events: `renewal_decision_window_opens` (a relationship whose term end is inside the renewal decision window its governing terms define); `renewal_decision_recorded` (an authorised renewal decision is recorded). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `renewal_cycle_id`, `term_end_at`, `renewal_model`, `notice_period`, `renewing_terms`, `decision_holder` (optional: `blockers`, `has_active_session`, `urgent_channel_permission`).

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 1 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition relationship-continuity (subscription; below a cancellation in motion, below an active risk state, and below an open payment recovery process on the same relationship; on loss: suppressed)

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.execute, h.scheduled-end, h.escalate, h.undefined · business outcome: `renewal_decision_recorded` (an authorised renewal decision is recorded) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution entered-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, notice_missed, decision_assumed, support_contact_within_24h

**Aliases.** renewal reminder · renewal notice · auto-renewal notice · contract renewal · subscription renewal reminder · term-end reminder

## SUB-262 · Cancellation Confirmation

Customer journey · category subscription · slug `cancellation-wind-down-notice`

**Objective.** Carry somebody through the period between deciding to leave and actually losing access, so the end date is never a surprise and returning stays possible right up to it.

**Trigger.** `cancellation_confirmed` (an authoritative cancellation recorded against the relationship; an effective end date) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative cancellation recorded against the relationship; an effective end date. Not enough on its own: a stated intention to cancel; a failed payment; a support conversation about leaving.

**Entity.** the cancelled relationship plus its effective end date — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative cancellation recorded against the relationship
- an effective end date
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the cancelled relationship plus its effective end date
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The cancellation is never re-litigated. A save attempt after the decision is a different journey and belongs before this one.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Paid-for access runs to its end date. Cancelling early does not shorten it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The end date is stated in the first message and never moves silently.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Withdrawal exits the journey immediately - a reminder that access is ending, sent to somebody who has just stayed, is worse than sending nothing.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **confirm** (`a.confirm`) — on classification, no wait
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm the cancellation, the exact date access ends, and what remains available until then.
2. **ending** (`a.ending`, after t1) — configure cancellation_wind.window relative to `effective_end_at`; cancelled by `cancellation_withdrawn` (the person withdraws the cancellation before it takes effect), `effective_end_reached` (the effective end date is reached); re-read before sending: the the cancelled relationship plus its effective end date re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say that access ends shortly and what will and will not survive it - exports, history, outstanding obligations.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.returned` [invalid-state] cancellation withdrawn, relationship continues — re-entry: a later cancellation starts a new wind-down
- `x.ended` [success] ended with nothing outstanding — re-entry: a former customer returning enters through acquisition or reactivation, not here
- handoff `h.obligations` → SUB-170 (Continuing Relationship End Reconciliation) — a cancelled relationship with obligations that continue past its end

**Configure.**
- `cancellation_wind.window` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The wind-down runs to the effective end date stated in the first message; the date never moves.
- `cancellation_wind.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `cancellation_wind.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the cancelled relationship plus its effective end date; a later instance concerns a different the cancelled relationship plus its effective end date and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `cancellation_confirmed` (an authoritative cancellation recorded against the relationship; an effective end date); `cancellation_withdrawn` (the person withdraws the cancellation before it takes effect); `effective_end_reached` (the effective end date is reached). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `cancellation_confirmed_at`, `effective_end_at`, `surviving_items`, `outstanding_obligations`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.returned, x.ended, h.obligations · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** cancellation confirmation · subscription cancelled notice · access ends on date · wind-down notice

## SCH-180 · Booking Reschedule

Customer journey · category scheduling · slug `provider-cancellation`

**Objective.** Recover a commitment we cannot keep, without any of the cost landing on the person who was ready.

**Trigger.** `provider_or_resource_cannot_fulfil` (a confirmed reservation that can no longer be fulfilled by its assigned provider or resource - unavailability, resource failure, location closure, withdrawn capacity or an operational incident) [authoritative]. Requires: a confirmed reservation that can no longer be fulfilled by its assigned provider or resource - unavailability, resource failure, location closure, withdrawn capacity or an operational incident. Not enough on its own: a customer asking to move the booking, which is their reschedule and not our failure; a provider's tentative doubt that has not become an inability.

**Entity.** the affected reservations and the provider or resource failure behind them — instance `booking_id + provider_failure_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a confirmed reservation that can no longer be fulfilled by its assigned provider or resource - unavailability, resource failure, location closure, withdrawn capacity or an operational incident
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the affected reservations and the provider or resource failure behind them
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A provider cancellation is not a customer cancellation.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An affected customer is never classified as a no-show.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A replacement must satisfy the service's actual requirements rather than merely being available.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Financial and remedy consequences remain separate lifecycle decisions.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **inform** (`a.inform`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is an equivalent replacement available for the same time? · Does the reallocation change anything the customer needs to know?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell them what changed, distinguished from a reschedule because the time did not move.
2. **notify-provider-cancel** (`a.notify-provider-cancel`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is an equivalent replacement available for the same time? · Do the applicable decision rules permit rescheduling this booking?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell the customer the confirmed commitment can no longer be kept, that the failure is ours and not theirs, and that they are not recorded as having cancelled or missed it.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.reallocated` [success] reallocated; the time and the commitment both stand — re-entry: the booking continues to its scheduled occurrence and revalidates there like any other
- `x.cancelled-provider` [failure] cancelled on the provider side; recorded as ours, with nothing attributed to the customer — re-entry: a new booking is a new commitment. This cancellation stays in the record as a provider failure, which is what any later question about the customer's booking history depends on
- handoff `h.reschedule` → SCH-175 (Reschedule Validation) — a provider-side failure that can be recovered at another time
- handoff `h.remedy` → REM-157 (Remedy Confirmation) — a service obligation left unresolved by a provider-side failure · contract: issue_id, obligation_id
- handoff `h.financial` → FIN-137 (Refund Request) — a provider-cancelled booking that was paid for · contract: refund_request_id

**Configure.**
- `provider_cancellation.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `provider_cancellation.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the affected reservations and the provider or resource failure behind them; a later instance concerns a different the affected reservations and the provider or resource failure behind them and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `provider_or_resource_cannot_fulfil` (a confirmed reservation that can no longer be fulfilled by its assigned provider or resource - unavailability, resource failure, location closure, withdrawn capacity or an operational incident). Attributes: `booking_id`, `provider_failure_id`, `affected_reservations`, `replacement_candidates`, `reschedule_rules`, `reservation_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.reallocated, x.cancelled-provider, h.reschedule, h.remedy, h.financial · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** provider cancellation · appointment cancelled by provider · reallocation · we cannot keep your booking · provider-side reschedule

## SCH-266 · Appointment Reminder

Customer journey · category scheduling · slug `appointment-readiness-reminder`

**Objective.** Get the customer's side of a confirmed commitment done before it arrives, and remind them from what the booking is at the moment of sending.

**Trigger.** `confirmed_booking_with_customer_owed_prerequisites` (a confirmed booking with a scheduled time; at least one prerequisite recorded as outstanding and owned by the customer) [authoritative]. Requires: a confirmed booking with a scheduled time; at least one prerequisite recorded as outstanding and owned by the customer. Not enough on its own: a request that has not been confirmed; a prerequisite owned by the provider rather than by the customer.

**Entity.** the confirmed booking occurrence plus the prerequisites its customer owes against it — instance `booking_id + occurrence_id`, one-active-per-key — supersession [CANONICAL_RULE]: A rescheduled occurrence is the same instance re-timed from the new scheduled time. A cancellation or a material change supersedes it; the replacement booking runs its own readiness from its own confirmation.

**Who enters.**
- the booking is confirmed in the system of record and its scheduled time is in the future
- the customer is contactable for service messages about this booking
- no reminder has been sent for this occurrence
- hard gates (GLB-31) permit service communication to this person

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Exit on cancellation or material change. A reminder is never built from a stored copy of the booking: every touch re-reads authoritative state first, which is how somebody who cancelled is not told to turn up.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One reminder per occurrence. A second reminder sent because nothing could tell whether the first arrived is what teaches people to stop reading them.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Where the confirmation already falls inside the pre-start window, no prerequisite prompt is sent; the journey goes straight to revalidation and the reminder.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Deduplicated by booking and occurrence against the confirmation (SCH-277) and any reschedule notice (SCH-180) about the same booking.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Hard gates (GLB-31) apply. The service pressure class deduplicates against other service messages about this booking; it does not ration a reminder the person is owed.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `deadline-countdown`

1. **prerequisite-prompt** (`a.prompt`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is there enough time left for a prompt to be worth sending?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message lists things to read and do over time, or no urgent channel is permitted) → in-session (in-app: the person is in the product)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Every outstanding prerequisite, whose it is and the point by which each must be done - in one message rather than one per requirement.
   - destination: booking-prerequisites bound to `booking_id`
2. **at-risk-notice** (`a.at-risk`) — 24 hours–72 hours (example) relative to `scheduled_at`; cancelled by `booking_materially_changed` (the booking is cancelled, moved or materially changed); re-read before sending: the booking re-read from authoritative state before either the at-risk notice or the reminder is built
   - checks: Does the booking still stand as it was just read? · Is anything outstanding that would stop the service happening?
   - channel roles, in order: urgent (sms: the scheduled time is within the same day, or a critical prerequisite is outstanding, and permission for service SMS is recorded) → persistent (email: the message lists things to read and do over time, or no urgent channel is permitted)
   - mandatory · priority service-critical (the service cannot be delivered without this prerequisite - not saying so costs the person the appointment) · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: The reminder plus the one thing that will stop this going ahead, with the last point at which it can still be done. The confirmed time is not moved.
   - destination: booking-prerequisites bound to `booking_id`
3. **reminder** (`a.remind`) — 24 hours–72 hours (example) relative to `scheduled_at`; cancelled by `booking_materially_changed` (the booking is cancelled, moved or materially changed); re-read before sending: the booking re-read from authoritative state before either the at-risk notice or the reminder is built
   - checks: Does the booking still stand as it was just read? · Is anything outstanding that would stop the service happening?
   - channel roles, in order: urgent (sms: the scheduled time is within the same day, or a critical prerequisite is outstanding, and permission for service SMS is recorded) → persistent (email: the message lists things to read and do over time, or no urgent channel is permitted) → in-session (in-app: the person is in the product)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: The time, the place or joining route, and anything still outstanding that does not block the service.
   - destination: booking-detail bound to `booking_id`

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.changed, s.once, s.too-close, s.dedup, s.hard-gates (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.superseded` [invalid-state] superseded; the booking changed before the reminder was due — re-entry: the booking that replaced it runs its own readiness from its own confirmation
- handoff `h.at-risk` → SCH-174 (Reservation Readiness) — a booking reaching its pre-start point with a critical prerequisite still outstanding · contract: booking_id, occurrence_id, scheduled_at, missing_prerequisite, last_point_to_complete
- handoff `h.prestart` → SCH-177 (Pre-Service Revalidation) — reminded against a revalidated booking; the start window and attendance belong to pre-service revalidation · contract: booking_id, occurrence_id, scheduled_at, reminded_at, outstanding_prerequisites

**Configure.**
- `scheduling.pre_start_window` · reminder-before-attribute — **example: 24 hours–72 hours** (confidence low; when appointments the customer must prepare for; avoid when same-day bookings) — The same pre-start point: the reminder is sent against the booking as it is at that moment.
- `scheduling.reminder_touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence medium; when GLB-24 and this journey's own guardrail: one reminder per occurrence) — A prerequisite prompt and one reminder per occurrence. The at-risk notice is an obligation to the person and sits outside the cap.
- `scheduling.reminder_cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: each further occurrence of a recurring commitment is its own instance) — Per occurrence. The next occurrence of a recurring commitment is its own instance and no cooldown applies between occurrences.

**Required data.** Events: `confirmed_booking_with_customer_owed_prerequisites` (a confirmed booking with a scheduled time; at least one prerequisite recorded as outstanding and owned by the customer); `prerequisites_completed` (every customer-owed prerequisite is recorded complete); `booking_materially_changed` (the booking is cancelled, moved or materially changed); `attendance_recorded` (attendance or check-in is authoritatively established). Attributes: `booking_id`, `occurrence_id`, `person_id`, `scheduled_at`, `timezone`, `location_or_joining_route`, `prerequisites`, `provider_id` (optional: `service_type`, `travel_required`, `has_active_session`).

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff h.prestart, h.at-risk · business outcome: `attendance_recorded` (attendance or check-in is authoritatively established) per instance, observed through SCH-177 until `service_completion_recorded` (the service completes), attribution entered-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, reminder_sent_for_cancelled_booking, duplicate_reminder_per_occurrence

**Aliases.** appointment reminder · booking reminder · pre-appointment preparation · reservation reminder · event reminder

## SCH-277 · Booking Confirmation

Customer journey · category scheduling · slug `reservation-outcome-notice`

**Objective.** Tell the requester whether the specific time they asked for is now a commitment, and where it is not, offer the nearest time that actually exists - because the availability they were shown earlier was a picture and never a hold.

**Trigger.** `reservation_request_recorded` (a reservation request recorded against a named requester, resource and slot; a permitted contact point for a booking notice) [authoritative]. Requires: a reservation request recorded against a named requester, resource and slot; a permitted contact point for a booking notice. Not enough on its own: availability being searched or displayed; a slot held inside a session that was never submitted.

**Entity.** the reservation request, the requester, and the resource and slot it names — instance `booking_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a reservation request recorded against a named requester, resource and slot
- a permitted contact point for a booking notice
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the reservation request
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A request acknowledged is never worded as a request confirmed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] What is offered after a failure is current availability, never the set the requester was originally shown.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Nothing is described as held unless the booking semantics actually hold it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A lapse is stated. Silence after a request is read as a commitment.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The confirmation restates the concrete slot every time. A reference is not a time and a place.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **received** (`a.received`) — on classification, no wait
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Acknowledge the request and say explicitly that it is not yet a commitment, naming when the outcome will come.
2. **confirm** (`a.confirm`, after t1) — configure reservation_outcome.outcome after the previous touch; cancelled by `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed), `booking_request_rejected` (the request is rejected for want of capacity or eligibility); re-read before sending: the the reservation request re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What did revalidation decide?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: State the committed slot, the resource and the terms concretely - the date, the time, the place, what is needed on arrival.
3. **reoffer** (`a.reoffer`) — configure reservation_outcome.outcome after the previous touch; cancelled by `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed), `booking_request_rejected` (the request is rejected for want of capacity or eligibility); re-read before sending: the the reservation request re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What did revalidation decide?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say the requested time is gone, name the slots available now, and give a deadline for choosing.
   - destination: available-slots bound to `booking_id`; must not claim: that a slot is held, that the requested time is available
4. **decline** (`a.decline`) — configure reservation_outcome.outcome after the previous touch; cancelled by `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed), `booking_request_rejected` (the request is rejected for want of capacity or eligibility); re-read before sending: the the reservation request re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What did revalidation decide?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say plainly that the time could not be committed, that nothing is being held, and when capacity of this kind is next expected.
5. **lapse** (`a.lapse`, after t1) — configure reservation_outcome.outcome after the previous touch; cancelled by `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed), `booking_request_rejected` (the request is rejected for want of capacity or eligibility); re-read before sending: the the reservation request re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → urgent (sms: an asserted time bound lies inside the urgent horizon and permission for messages on this channel is recorded)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Close the request as lapsed and say that nothing is held and nothing was booked.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.confirmed` [success] committed and stated to the requester — re-entry: a change or cancellation to this commitment is its own instance; a further request is a new one
- `x.declined` [success] no commitment made, requester informed — re-entry: a fresh request for a different time enters as a new instance
- `x.lapsed` [timeout] request lapsed unresolved, nothing held — re-entry: a fresh request starts the sequence again
- handoff `h.rebook` → SCH-173 (Reservation Validation) — a requester choosing one of the alternative slots offered to them

**Configure.**
- `reservation_outcome.outcome` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The period the booking semantics allow a request to stay unresolved.
- `reservation_outcome.choice` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The stated deadline for choosing.
- `reservation_outcome.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; an acknowledgement and one outcome message) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `reservation_outcome.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the reservation request; a later instance concerns a different the reservation request and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `reservation_request_recorded` (a reservation request recorded against a named requester, resource and slot; a permitted contact point for a booking notice); `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed); `booking_request_rejected` (the request is rejected for want of capacity or eligibility); `offered_slot_requested` (the requester picks one of the offered slots); `offered_slots_declined` (the requester declines every offered slot). Attributes: `booking_id`, `requester_id`, `resource_ref`, `slot_ref`, `contact_point`, `choice_deadline_at`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.confirmed, x.declined, x.lapsed, h.rebook · business outcome: `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** booking confirmation · reservation confirmation · appointment confirmed · booking request outcome · slot no longer available

## SCH-280 · No-Show Follow-Up

Customer journey · category scheduling · slug `no-show-rebooking`

**Objective.** After a genuinely missed booking, state the fact without penalty language and give the single route to a new booking where one exists - after re-reading the booking, and never where our side could not have delivered.

**Trigger.** `no_show_recorded_against_booking` (a no-show authoritatively recorded against one confirmed booking; the semantics it was judged under and the window that closed; policy naming rebooking rather than a fee as what follows) [authoritative]. Requires: a no-show authoritatively recorded against one confirmed booking; the semantics it was judged under and the window that closed; policy naming rebooking rather than a fee as what follows. Not enough on its own: a service window that has passed with no attendance event yet recorded; a provider running late; a booking with no arrival semantics defined.

**Entity.** the single booking occurrence that did not take place — instance `booking_id + occurrence_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a no-show is recorded against one booking occurrence by the attendance source
- the booking, re-read after the recording lag, genuinely did not happen - not moved, cancelled or attended after all
- the provider or resource could have delivered; a failure on our side goes to reschedule, not follow-up
- hard gates (GLB-31) permit service communication

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Attendance and cancellation events lag; the booking's latest events are re-read before anything leaves, and a booking moved, cancelled or attended after all sends nothing.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A missed booking the provider or resource could not have delivered is ours, not the customer's; it goes to reschedule and no follow-up is sent.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The miss is stated as a fact; no penalty language, and no penalty applied here - the commitment's own terms own consequences.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Where nothing can be rebooked, the notice acknowledges the miss and what it means, with no rebooking prompt attached.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Hard gates (GLB-31) apply; pressure caps do not, because this concerns a commitment the person made.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `single-notice`

1. **rebooking-offer** (`a.offer`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Did the booking genuinely not happen? · Could the provider or resource actually have delivered? · Is there anything to offer?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the offer carries the rebooking route and should be kept - the default) → urgent (sms: permission for service messages on the channel is recorded and the rebooking window is short)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say that the booking was missed as a fact, without penalty language, and give the single route to a new one within the stated window.
   - destination: rebooking bound to `booking_id`; must not claim: a penalty, that a slot is held
2. **acknowledgement** (`a.acknowledge`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Did the booking genuinely not happen? · Could the provider or resource actually have delivered? · Is there anything to offer?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the offer carries the rebooking route and should be kept - the default)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: State plainly that the booking did not happen and what that means, with no rebooking prompt attached because there is nothing to rebook onto.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.reread, s.provider-fault, s.no-penalty-language, s.nothing-to-rebook, s.hard-gates (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.suppressed` [suppression] nothing sent; the booking was moved, cancelled or attended after all — re-entry: a later booking that is genuinely missed is its own instance
- `x.rebooked` [success] rebooked — re-entry: the new booking has its own lifecycle and a later miss enters here again
- `x.closed` [timeout] closed with no rebooking — re-entry: a request from the customer later reopens booking through the ordinary route, not through this one
- handoff `h.provider` → SCH-180 (Booking Reschedule) — a missed booking the provider or resource could not have delivered

**Configure.**
- `no_show.rebooking_window` · response-window — **example: 7 days–14 days** (confidence low) — The offer stands for the window stated in it; when the window passes the instance closes and the offer is not repeated.
- `no_show.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when the graph reaches at most one message per instance) — One follow-up per missed occurrence - an offer or an acknowledgement, never both; an unanswered offer is not repeated.
- `no_show.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high) — Follow-up is per occurrence; a later miss is its own instance, though repeated misses are evidence the commitment's own policy reads.

**Required data.** Events: `no_show_recorded_against_booking` (a no-show authoritatively recorded against one confirmed booking; the semantics it was judged under and the window that closed; policy naming rebooking rather than a fee as what follows); `rebooked` (a new booking is created for the same commitment); `rebooking_declined` (the customer declines a further booking). Attributes: `booking_id`, `occurrence_id`, `person_id`, `scheduled_at`, `no_show_recorded_at`, `attendance_source`, `provider_status`, `rebooking_availability` (optional: `rebooking_window`, `urgent_channel_permission`, `commitment_terms_ref`).

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 1 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.rebooked, x.closed, x.suppressed, h.provider · business outcome: `rebooked` (a new booking is created for the same commitment) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_on_superseded_booking, penalty_language_used, support_contact_within_24h

**Aliases.** no-show follow-up · missed appointment follow-up · missed booking · rebooking offer · did-not-attend follow-up · DNA follow-up

## SCH-282 · Availability Search Abandonment

Customer journey · category scheduling · slug `availability-searched-no-booking`

**Objective.** Follow up an availability question that produced no booking with something that is genuinely bookable now, or with a waitlist place where nothing fits - because what was shown was never held and is probably already gone.

**Trigger.** `availability_query_closed_without_reservation` (an availability query recorded for a named person against a specific resource and window; no reservation or hold created by that person for that window since) [behavioral]. Requires: an availability query recorded for a named person against a specific resource and window; no reservation or hold created by that person for that window since. Not enough on its own: a query by somebody who already holds a booking for that window; a browse with no resource or window attached to it.

**Entity.** the availability question, the resource and window it asked about, and the absence of any reservation from it — instance `person_id + availability_query_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an availability query recorded for a named person against a specific resource and window
- no reservation or hold created by that person for that window since
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the availability question
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Availability is re-evaluated before the offer is sent. What the query returned was never held and is not evidence of anything now.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A different window is labelled as a different window.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A waitlist place is stated as reserving nothing.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One offer per query. A second offer for the same request is pressure rather than help.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The delay before the offer is bounded and never extended by the person browsing again.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **offer** (`a.offer`) — configure availability_searched.settle after the trigger; cancelled by `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed), `availability_lost` (the requested window is taken or withdrawn); re-read before sending: the the availability question re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What is actually available now?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → low-friction (push: a valid token or app session exists and the message is a single step from the notification)
   - discretionary · priority promotional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Offer the nearest bookable window, labelled as a different window rather than dressed up as the one that was asked for, and say that it is not held.
   - destination: nearest-bookable-window bound to `availability_query_id`; must not claim: that the window is held, that it is the window asked for
2. **waitlist** (`a.waitlist`) — configure availability_searched.settle after the trigger; cancelled by `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed), `availability_lost` (the requested window is taken or withdrawn); re-read before sending: the the availability question re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What is actually available now?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → low-friction (push: a valid token or app session exists and the message is a single step from the notification)
   - discretionary · priority promotional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Offer a waitlist place and state that it reserves nothing.
   - destination: waitlist-place bound to `availability_query_id`; must not claim: that a place is reserved

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.no-route` [no-action] no offer made; the query cannot be attributed to a reachable person — re-entry: a later query from an identified person qualifies normally
- `x.booked` [success] booked; no offer was needed — re-entry: a later query with no booking behind it starts a new instance
- `x.waitlisted` [success] waitlisted; nothing is reserved — re-entry: capacity reaching the waitlist is that mechanism's business, not a new instance of this one
- `x.nothing` [no-action] nothing to offer; no message sent — re-entry: a later query for a window that does have capacity qualifies again
- `x.lapsed` [timeout] offer made and not taken — re-entry: a new availability query is a new instance; this one is never re-offered


**Configure.**
- `availability_searched.settle` · recovery-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The offer waits long enough after the query that an unprompted booking has had its chance, and no longer than the question stays live.
- `availability_searched.waitlist` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The validity of the waitlist offer.
- `availability_searched.respond` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The validity of the offered window.
- `availability_searched.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `availability_searched.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the availability question; a later instance concerns a different the availability question and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `availability_query_closed_without_reservation` (an availability query recorded for a named person against a specific resource and window; no reservation or hold created by that person for that window since); `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed); `availability_lost` (the requested window is taken or withdrawn); `waitlist_place_taken` (the person takes the offered waitlist place). Attributes: `person_id`, `availability_query_id`, `resource_ref`, `requested_window`, `last_query_at`, `permission_position`.

**Collision & priority.** default promotional · pressure class promotional · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition commerce-recovery (person; below process recovery and selection recovery; alongside interest recovery - an availability enquiry that asked for a specific window outranks inferred interest for the same person; on loss: suppressed)

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.no-route, x.booked, x.waitlisted, x.nothing, x.lapsed · business outcome: `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** availability search abandonment · searched but did not book · no availability follow-up · waitlist offer · nearest slot offer

## DEC-184 · More Information Request

Customer journey · category decision · slug `information-requirement`

**Objective.** Pause a decision for the fact it is actually missing, without losing the review already done.

**Trigger.** `information_requirement_identified` (a reviewer identifying a specific fact the decision turns on and which is not available) [authoritative]. Requires: a reviewer identifying a specific fact the decision turns on and which is not available. Not enough on its own: a reviewer's curiosity about something the decision does not turn on; information missing before the case was opened, which DEC-181 holds as pending information.

**Entity.** the decision case and the specific information requirement blocking it — instance `decision_case_id + requirement_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a reviewer identifying a specific fact the decision turns on and which is not available
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the decision case and the specific information requirement blocking it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] More information required is not rejected.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The review history is never reset when information arrives.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Unrelated information is not requested alongside the requirement.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Deadline behaviour comes from policy rather than from a default closure.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `notice-then-confirm`

1. **request** (`a.request`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Who actually holds the outstanding information?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Request what is still outstanding from the external source, naming it precisely.
   - destination: provide-outstanding-information bound to `requirement_id`; must not claim: a moved deadline, that the case is rejected
2. **request-internal** (`a.request-internal`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Who actually holds the outstanding information?
   - channel roles, in order: human (task: the step is carried out by a person - a call, a task, a visit - and recorded as done by them)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Raise the outstanding requirement as owned work against the internal holder, carrying the blocked decision and its unchanged deadline.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.moot` [invalid-state] no longer requiring a decision; the review history stands unclosed rather than concluded — re-entry: a renewed need for the same judgment is a new request, which can reference this case and its evidence
- `x.closed-noinfo` [timeout] closed for want of information; no decision was made on the merits — re-entry: a new request with the information present is assessed fresh, and this closure does not count against it
- handoff `h.escalate` → DEC-189 (Decision Escalation) — an information deadline expiring with escalation or with no defined closure semantics
- handoff `h.resume` → DEC-183 (Evidence Review) — an information requirement resolved, or exhausted under policy

**Configure.**
- `information_requirement.request_budget` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — This loop runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; when it is spent the instance takes its timeout path (GLB-24).
- `information_requirement.request_internal_budget` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — This loop runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; when it is spent the instance takes its timeout path (GLB-24).
- `information_requirement.info` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The deadline policy defines for this requirement.
- `information_requirement.touches` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — A request is repeated only for what is still outstanding and only within the request budget policy allows; the budget is the company's and never re-requests the whole requirement.
- `information_requirement.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the decision case and the specific information requirement blocking it; a later instance concerns a different the decision case and the specific information requirement blocking it and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `information_requirement_identified` (a reviewer identifying a specific fact the decision turns on and which is not available); `requested_information_received` (the requested information is received); `request_withdrawn` (the requester withdraws the request). Attributes: `decision_case_id`, `requirement_id`, `holder`, `information_deadline_at`, `request_budget`, `decision_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap configure information_requirement.touches (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.moot, x.closed-noinfo, h.escalate, h.resume · business outcome: `requested_information_received` (the requested information is received) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** more information request · request for information · missing information on a case · pending information · information requirement

## DEC-267 · Adverse Decision Recovery

Customer journey · category decision · slug `adverse-decision-remediation`

**Objective.** Tell somebody a decision went against them and which of exactly three things is true - correctable now, reapplicable later, or final - so the outcome arrives as a position they can act on instead of a verdict they have to interpret.

**Trigger.** `adverse_decision_recorded` (an authorised rejection, or a violation confirmed against the applicable rule version; the recorded reason and the scope it affects; the remediation, reapplication or finality semantics policy defines for that reason) [authoritative]. Requires: an authorised rejection, or a violation confirmed against the applicable rule version; the recorded reason and the scope it affects; the remediation, reapplication or finality semantics policy defines for that reason. Not enough on its own: a detection that has not been assessed; a review still in progress; a score or a flag with no decision behind it.

**Entity.** the adverse decision and the subject it was issued against — instance `decision_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authorised rejection, or a violation confirmed against the applicable rule version
- the recorded reason and the scope it affects
- the remediation, reapplication or finality semantics policy defines for that reason
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the adverse decision and the subject it was issued against
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Nothing is said before the decision is authoritatively recorded. A warning about an outcome nobody has reached is how a support queue fills with cases that do not exist.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Correction, reapplication and appeal rights are stated only where policy defines them. None is ever invented to make the message land more softly.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The decision itself is never re-argued here. Whether it was correct belongs upstream.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Where the path is final, no call to action is attached.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A resolved decision is confirmed explicitly - silence is read as it still standing.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **correctable** (`a.correctable`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Which path does policy actually define for this reason?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: State what was decided, why, exactly what would have to change, and by when - taken from the policy that defines the path rather than from what sounds achievable.
   - destination: correction-path bound to `decision_id`; must not claim: a route policy does not define, that the decision is under appeal
2. **cleared** (`a.cleared`, after t1) — configure adverse_decision.remediate relative to `correction_deadline_at`; cancelled by `correction_recorded_complete` (the correction is recorded as complete), `correction_failed` (the correction fails), `decision_withdrawn` (the decision is withdrawn or overturned); re-read before sending: the the adverse decision and the subject it was issued against re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What ended the window?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Confirm the decision is resolved and name what it no longer affects.
3. **expired** (`a.expired`, after t1) — configure adverse_decision.remediate relative to `correction_deadline_at`; cancelled by `correction_recorded_complete` (the correction is recorded as complete), `correction_failed` (the correction fails), `decision_withdrawn` (the decision is withdrawn or overturned); re-read before sending: the the adverse decision and the subject it was issued against re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say once that the window has closed and what now stands.
4. **reapply** (`a.reapply`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Which path does policy actually define for this reason?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Name the conditions policy actually defines for a fresh request - after what period, on what basis, with what evidence - and nothing beyond them.
   - destination: reapplication-conditions bound to `decision_id`; must not claim: conditions beyond what policy defines
5. **final** (`a.final`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Which path does policy actually define for this reason?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say the decision is final and that no route back exists, with no the channel to action attached to soften it.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.corrected` [success] adverse decision remediated within its window — re-entry: a further decision against the same subject is a new instance
- `x.withdrawn` [invalid-state] decision withdrawn before remediation — re-entry: if the decision is reinstated, it enters again as a new adverse decision
- `x.reapply` [failure] decided against, with a stated route to a fresh request — re-entry: a request made under those conditions is a new decision, not a continuation of this one
- `x.final` [failure] final; no path onward is defined — re-entry: a change in the governing policy or in the underlying facts is a new decision case, not a reopening of this one


**Configure.**
- `adverse_decision.remediate` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The remediation deadline policy defines.
- `adverse_decision.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; one outcome statement and one follow-up) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `adverse_decision.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the adverse decision and the subject it was issued against; a later instance concerns a different the adverse decision and the subject it was issued against and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `adverse_decision_recorded` (an authorised rejection, or a violation confirmed against the applicable rule version; the recorded reason and the scope it affects; the remediation, reapplication or finality semantics policy defines for that reason); `correction_recorded_complete` (the correction is recorded as complete); `correction_failed` (the correction fails); `decision_withdrawn` (the decision is withdrawn or overturned). Attributes: `decision_id`, `subject_id`, `reason`, `affected_scope`, `path_semantics`, `correction_deadline_at`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.corrected, x.withdrawn, x.reapply, x.final · business outcome: `correction_recorded_complete` (the correction is recorded as complete) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** adverse decision recovery · application rejected notice · declined with a path · rejection with next steps · decision against the customer

## RSK-273 · Usage Limit Alert

Customer journey · category risk · slug `usage-limit-capacity-path`

**Objective.** Meet somebody at the moment a limit stops them with the three facts that decide what happens next - what the limit is, when it resets, and whether more capacity can be bought - without any of it reading as an accusation.

**Trigger.** `limit_reached_and_action_blocked` (an authoritative usage figure against a defined limit and measurement window; an action actually blocked or held by that limit) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative usage figure against a defined limit and measurement window; an action actually blocked or held by that limit. Not enough on its own: a figure from a lagging or non-authoritative counter; approaching a threshold with nothing yet stopped.

**Entity.** the entity and the one limit constraining it, in one measurement window — instance `entity_ref + limit_id + window_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative usage figure against a defined limit and measurement window
- an action actually blocked or held by that limit
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the entity and the one limit constraining it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A limit reached is not abuse, and the message never borrows the vocabulary of one.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A reset point is never invented. If the authoritative source has no date, no date is stated.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The free path is named wherever it exists, even in the message that offers the paid one.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Where the capacity decision belongs to somebody else, both parties are told - the one waiting and the one who can end the wait.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **at-the-wall** (`a.at-the-wall`) — on classification, no wait
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: At the point the action is stopped, say which limit was reached, the usage against it, and when the window resets.
2. **offer-self** (`a.offer-self`, after t1) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: What path exists from here? · Does the person who hit the limit hold the capacity decision?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Name the capacity that would release the held action and what it costs, alongside the reset that would release it for nothing.
   - destination: purchase-capacity bound to `limit_id`; must not claim: a reset point the source does not assert, that the limit is abuse
3. **offer-holder** (`a.offer-holder`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: What path exists from here? · Does the person who hit the limit hold the capacity decision?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell the party who holds the decision what is blocked, for whom, what capacity would release it and what the reset alternative is.
   - destination: purchase-capacity bound to `limit_id`; must not claim: a reset point the source does not assert
4. **notify-blocked-party** (`a.notify-blocked-party`, after t3) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: What path exists from here? · Does the person who hit the limit hold the capacity decision?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Tell the person whose action is held that the decision now sits with somebody else, name who, and give the authoritative point at which the window resets anyway.
5. **reset** (`a.reset`) — configure usage_limit.capacity relative to `window_resets_at`; cancelled by `capacity_authorised` (additional capacity is authorised), `limit_reset` (the usage limit resets), `held_action_abandoned` (the held action is abandoned); re-read before sending: the the entity and the one limit constraining it re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: What changed?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say the window has reset and the held action can proceed, taken from the authoritative reset rather than an assumed clock.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.reset` [success] window reset; capacity available again under the same limit — re-entry: reaching the limit again in a later window re-enters here
- `x.blocked` [no-action] at the limit with no route to more capacity in this window — re-entry: a reset, or a change to the limit authorised elsewhere, qualifies this again as a new instance
- handoff `h.capacity` → SUB-166 (Plan Change Validation) — an authorised capacity increase that has to be executed as a change of plan or terms

**Configure.**
- `usage_limit.capacity` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The held action waits until the authoritative reset of the window or an authorised capacity increase; no reset point is ever invented.
- `usage_limit.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — The wall notice and the reset notice are mandatory; the discretionary offers run against a budget of the plan's own length.
- `usage_limit.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the entity and the one limit constraining it; a later instance concerns a different the entity and the one limit constraining it and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `limit_reached_and_action_blocked` (an authoritative usage figure against a defined limit and measurement window; an action actually blocked or held by that limit); `capacity_authorised` (additional capacity is authorised); `limit_reset` (the usage limit resets); `held_action_abandoned` (the held action is abandoned). Attributes: `entity_ref`, `limit_id`, `window_id`, `usage`, `limit_value`, `window_resets_at`, `capacity_decider`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.reset, x.blocked, h.capacity · business outcome: `capacity_authorised` (additional capacity is authorised) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** usage limit alert · quota reached · rate limit reached notice · plan limit hit · over the limit

## DOC-214 · Document Delivery

Customer journey · category document · slug `document-distribution`

**Objective.** Get a specific issued version to the party who should have it, without either fact touching the other.

**Trigger.** `issued_document_requires_distribution` (an issued document version with a party who is to receive it) [authoritative]. Requires: an issued document version with a party who is to receive it. Not enough on its own: a document drafted but not issued; a document already viewed or downloaded by its recipient, which is delivery having happened.

**Entity.** the issued version, the intended recipient, and the distribution of one to the other — instance `document_version_id + recipient_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an issued document version with a party who is to receive it
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the issued version
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A document being issued is not the recipient having received it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Sending a wrong or outdated version is prevented by binding distribution to a version.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Distribution reuses the canonical communication mechanics rather than duplicating them.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `single-notice`

1. **distribute** (`a.distribute`) — on classification, no wait
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Raise the actual delivery through the canonical communication mechanism, which owns channels, permissions, retries and delivery evidence.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.distributed` [success] the identified version reached the intended recipient; the document's validity is unchanged by this — re-entry: a superseding version requires its own distribution. Nothing about this one is retracted by that - the recipient was correctly given what was current then
- handoff `h.reconcile` → external:external-status-reconciliation — a distribution whose outcome could not be established · contract: document_version_id, issue_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.recover` → CMS-208 (Message Delivery Recovery) — a failed document distribution

**Configure.**
- `document_distribution.distribution` · external-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The distribution outcome is waited for as long as the communication mechanism's own delivery window runs; past it the outcome is reconciled, never assumed.
- `document_distribution.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `document_distribution.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the issued version; a later instance concerns a different the issued version and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `issued_document_requires_distribution` (an issued document version with a party who is to receive it); `distribution_confirmed` (distribution is confirmed to the required level); `distribution_failed` (distribution fails). Attributes: `document_version_id`, `recipient_id`, `issued_at`, `distribution_ref`, `document_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default transactional · pressure class none · local cap 1 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.distributed, h.reconcile, h.recover · business outcome: `distribution_confirmed` (distribution is confirmed to the required level) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** document delivery · send a document · policy document distribution · contract copy delivery · document distribution
