## DOC-215 · Signature Reminder

Customer journey · category document · slug `signature-process`

**Objective.** Collect every required signature on one exact document version: request once from each required signer, remind outstanding signers once while a reminder can still change the outcome, and end honestly as fully signed, declined, superseded or expired.

**Trigger.** `document_requires_signature` (an issued or prepared document version requiring signature by identified parties) [authoritative]. Requires: an issued or prepared document version requiring signature by identified parties. Not enough on its own: a document that requires acknowledgement rather than an authorised mark; a signature request already sent, which is the state this journey creates and not the one that starts it.

**Entity.** the document version, the signature process against it, and each required signer — instance `document_version_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a document version requires signature, with required signers, their signing authority and any signing order defined
- a signature validity window is either defined by the request's own terms or explicitly recorded as not set
- no signature process is already open for this version
- no DOC-220 conflict review is open for this document's lineage - a version under active conflict review is not simultaneously collecting signatures against it as though it were already settled

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Every request and reminder is bound to the exact document version; a superseded version ends the process and suppresses its outstanding reminders.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A reminder goes only to signers still outstanding, naming the action left and the real validity boundary; a signer who signed is never reminded.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One reminder per signer; a second is a re-request the process does not make.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Where no expiry was set none is invented; an invented signature deadline voids a request nobody agreed to time-limit.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Hard gates (GLB-31) apply; pressure caps do not, because a signature request is the process itself, not outreach.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **signature-request** (`a.request`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is a signature validity window defined?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the request is bound to a document version and must be kept; the signing destination is reached from it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Issue the request to each required signer, bound to the exact document version, naming the authority they sign under and the validity boundary where one is defined.
   - destination: signing-page-for-version bound to `document_version_id`; must not claim: a deadline the request's terms do not set
2. **reminder** (`a.remind`, after t-request) — 3 days–5 days (example) relative to `validity_ends_at`; cancelled by `signer_signed` (a required signer signs), `signer_declined` (a signer declines), `document_version_superseded` (the document version is superseded); re-read before sending: the version and each signer re-read: who has signed the correct version, who declined, whether the version still stands
   - checks: Would a reminder still change anything?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the request is bound to a document version and must be kept; the signing destination is reached from it)
   - discretionary · priority transactional · [RECOMMENDED_DEFAULT]
   - purpose: Remind only the signers still outstanding, once, naming the action left and the real validity boundary.
   - destination: signing-page-for-version bound to `document_version_id`; must not claim: a deadline the request's terms do not set

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.version, s.outstanding-only, s.one-reminder, s.no-invented-deadline, s.hard-gates (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.superseded` [invalid-state] signature process ended; its version was superseded before completion — re-entry: the successor version raises its own signature process. The signatures collected here remain evidence about the version they were made against
- `x.expired` [timeout] SIGNATURE_EXPIRED; signatures collected so far remain evidence, the set is incomplete — re-entry: a fresh request against the same version is a new process. Whether the partial signatures still count is governed by the document's own rules rather than assumed
- handoff `h.effective` → DOC-216 (Document Effectiveness Validation) — a document with all required signatures complete
- handoff `h.declined` → external:operational-resolution — a required signer declining · contract: document_version_id, signer_id, declined_at, reason_if_given, signatures_collected

**Configure.**
- `signature.reminder_point` · reminder-before-attribute — **example: 3 days–5 days** (confidence low; when a validity window of weeks; avoid when no validity window - the review point is the bound) — The reminder is placed at the last point at which it could still change the outcome, taken from the request's own validity where one is defined and from the process's review point where none is.
- `signature.validity_window` · attribute-bound — **recommended: validity_ends_at where defined, otherwise the process's recorded review point** (confidence high) — The process ends at the validity window where one is defined, and at the process's own review point where none is; nothing is invented.
- `signature.required_signers` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The signature loop runs once per required signer; the budget is the signer list fixed when the process opened.
- `signature.reminders` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when the graph sends one reminder per signer) — Only the reminder counts against the cap; the request itself is the process and is never rationed.
- `signature.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high) — Signature is per document version; a new version is a new process and no cooldown applies between versions.

**Required data.** Events: `document_requires_signature` (an issued or prepared document version requiring signature by identified parties); `signer_signed` (a required signer signs); `signer_declined` (a signer declines); `document_version_superseded` (the document version is superseded). Attributes: `document_version_id`, `document_id`, `required_signers`, `signing_authority`, `signing_order`, `validity_ends_at`, `signer_id` (optional: `review_point_at`, `signer_destinations`).

**Collision & priority.** default transactional · pressure class none · local cap 1 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff h.effective, h.declined, x.superseded, x.expired · business outcome: `signer_signed` (a required signer signs) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution entered-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: complaint, reminder_to_signed_signer, invented_deadline_named, wrong_version_signed

**Aliases.** signature reminder · e-signature request · document signing reminder · contract signature follow-up · unsigned document reminder · multi-party signing

## DOC-220 · Document Conflict Review

Customer journey · category document · slug `document-conflict`

**Objective.** Work out which version actually governs, and explain the conflict rather than deleting it.

**Trigger.** `document_version_inconsistency_detected` (a material inconsistency - different content under one identifier, an incorrect version distributed, a signature attached to the wrong version, a local copy differing from the authoritative record, several versions claiming current status, or a missing amendment link) [authoritative]. Requires: a material inconsistency - different content under one identifier, an incorrect version distributed, a signature attached to the wrong version, a local copy differing from the authoritative record, several versions claiming current status, or a missing amendment link. Not enough on its own: two versions that differ by design, such as a draft alongside its issued form; a formatting difference between two renderings of the same version.

**Entity.** the document lineage and every conflicting record claiming to be part of it — instance `document_lineage_id + conflict_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a material inconsistency - different content under one identifier, an incorrect version distributed, a signature attached to the wrong version, a local copy differing from the authoritative record, several versions claiming current status, or a missing amendment link
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the document lineage and every conflicting record claiming to be part of it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The newest file is not automatically the authoritative version.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Conflicting evidence is never deleted to fix the state.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Signature validity is checked against the exact version.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Reconciliation preserves lineage and prior use.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `single-notice`

1. **correct-distribution** (`a.correct-distribution`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is the authoritative version deterministically identifiable? · Is any signature bound to a version other than the authoritative one? · Was a non-authoritative version distributed or acted upon?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Redistribute the authoritative version, saying explicitly what changed and which version it replaces.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.reconciled` [success] authoritative version established; conflicting records preserved and explained — re-entry: a further inconsistency in this lineage is assessed with this reconciliation as part of its evidence, which is why none of it was deleted
- handoff `h.review` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a conflict where the authoritative version cannot be safely determined
- handoff `h.resign` → DOC-215 (Signature Reminder) — signatures that bind to a version other than the authoritative one
- handoff `h.remedy` → REM-157 (Remedy Confirmation) — a business action taken on a non-authoritative document version · contract: issue_id, obligation_id

**Configure.**
- `document_conflict.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `document_conflict.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the document lineage and every conflicting record claiming to be part of it; a later instance concerns a different the document lineage and every conflicting record claiming to be part of it and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `document_version_inconsistency_detected` (a material inconsistency - different content under one identifier, an incorrect version distributed, a signature attached to the wrong version, a local copy differing from the authoritative record, several versions claiming current status, or a missing amendment link). Attributes: `document_lineage_id`, `conflict_id`, `conflicting_records`, `signature_bindings`, `distribution_history`, `document_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default transactional · pressure class none · local cap 1 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.reconciled, h.review, h.resign, h.remedy · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** document conflict review · version conflict · which version governs · duplicate document reconciliation

## DOC-286 · Document Activation

Customer journey · category document · slug `document-effective-notice`

**Objective.** Tell the holder that what they signed has actually started, and what it now lets them do, at the moment it becomes true rather than the moment they signed.

**Trigger.** `document_recorded_effective` (an authoritative record that this version is in force, revalidated at its effective moment; the entitlement it confers, expressed as something the holder can act on; a named holder with a permitted route to them) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative record that this version is in force, revalidated at its effective moment; the entitlement it confers, expressed as something the holder can act on; a named holder with a permitted route to them. Not enough on its own: a complete set of signatures; a scheduled effective date that has not been revalidated; a condition that has not yet resolved.

**Entity.** the effective document version and the entitlement it confers on its holder — instance `document_version_id + person_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative record that this version is in force, revalidated at its effective moment
- the entitlement it confers, expressed as something the holder can act on
- a named holder with a permitted route to them
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the effective document version and the entitlement it confers on its holder
- no DOC-220 conflict review is open for this document's lineage - a version review can retroactively make what looked authoritative not authoritative, and this journey does not tell a holder an entitlement is active while that question is still open
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Signed is not effective, and only the effective record starts this.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The entitlement is named as what the holder can do, never as what the document says.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An entitlement with nothing to exercise is never chased for use.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One reminder, never two. The entitlement stands whether or not anybody takes it up.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Dormant and no-longer-in-force are recorded as different outcomes - one is about the holder, the other is about the document.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **in-force-actionable** (`a.in-force-actionable`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is this entitlement exercised, or held?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say the document is in force, from when, and name the single first thing the entitlement now lets them do.
   - destination: first-action-under-document bound to `document_version_id`; must not claim: that signing made it effective
2. **in-force-standing** (`a.in-force-standing`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Is this entitlement exercised, or held?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - mandatory · priority transactional · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say the document is in force and what it covers, and name the moment at which it would matter rather than an action to take now.
3. **reminder** (`a.reminder`, after t1) — configure document_effective.first_use after the previous touch; cancelled by `capability_first_used` (the granted capability is used for the first time), `document_version_superseded` (the document version is superseded), `effective_period_ended` (the effective period ends); re-read before sending: the the effective document version and the entitlement it confers on its holder re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: Does the document still stand?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority transactional · [RECOMMENDED_DEFAULT]
   - purpose: Send one reminder naming what is in force and the same single first action, not a second explanation of the document.
   - destination: first-action-under-document bound to `document_version_id`

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4, s.g5 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.standing` [success] in force, nothing for the holder to exercise — re-entry: a superseding version, or a change that gives the entitlement something to exercise, is a new instance
- `x.used` [success] entitlement exercised — re-entry: a further document taking effect for the same holder is its own instance
- `x.moot` [invalid-state] in force and ended without ever being used — re-entry: a superseding version taking effect starts its own instance
- `x.dormant` [timeout] in force and dormant — re-entry: a renewal or a superseding version taking effect starts a new instance


**Configure.**
- `document_effective.first_use` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The period within which a holder of this entitlement would ordinarily have used it.
- `document_effective.dormancy` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — After the one reminder the instance observes until the effective period ends; an entitlement never exercised is recorded as dormant, not chased.
- `document_effective.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `document_effective.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the effective document version and the entitlement it confers on its holder; a later instance concerns a different the effective document version and the entitlement it confers on its holder and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `document_recorded_effective` (an authoritative record that this version is in force, revalidated at its effective moment; the entitlement it confers, expressed as something the holder can act on; a named holder with a permitted route to them); `capability_first_used` (the granted capability is used for the first time); `document_version_superseded` (the document version is superseded); `effective_period_ended` (the effective period ends). Attributes: `document_version_id`, `person_id`, `effective_at`, `effective_period_ends_at`, `entitlement`, `first_action`.

**Collision & priority.** default transactional · pressure class none · local cap 1 (recommended) (non-mandatory) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.standing, x.used, x.moot, x.dormant · business outcome: `capability_first_used` (the granted capability is used for the first time) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** document activation · agreement now in force · policy effective notice · contract start notice · entitlement active

## RLT-279 · Upgrade Blocker Reminder

Customer journey · category rollout · slug `upgrade-blocker-prompt`

**Objective.** Tell the holder of a blocked target the one specific thing standing between it and the change, while there is still enough of the preparation window left for them to clear it.

**Trigger.** `target_held_on_named_prerequisite` (a target authoritatively held for a planned change; the specific prerequisite that is blocking it, named rather than described; a preparation window with time still in it) [authoritative]. Requires: a target authoritatively held for a planned change; the specific prerequisite that is blocking it, named rather than described; a preparation window with time still in it. Not enough on its own: a change that is available but not yet scoped to this target; a hold whose cause has not been identified.

**Entity.** the target, the change waiting on it, and the named prerequisite that is blocking it — instance `target_id + change_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a target authoritatively held for a planned change
- the specific prerequisite that is blocking it, named rather than described
- a preparation window with time still in it
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the target
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The prompt names the specific blocker. A notice that an update is available, sent to somebody who cannot take it, is the failure this journey exists to stop.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Nothing is asked of a holder who cannot clear the prerequisite.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Two prompts at most, and both name the same blocker and the same date.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Not ready is not failed. A target that was never touched is recorded as unprepared, not as a failed change.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `offer-decide-remind`

1. **inform-hold** (`a.inform-hold`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Can the holder clear this blocker themselves?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Say that the change is held and why, with nothing asked of the holder - because there is nothing they can do.
2. **name-blocker** (`a.name-blocker`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Can the holder clear this blocker themselves?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Name the one prerequisite, what clearing it involves, and the date after which the change can no longer be applied in this window.
   - destination: clear-the-prerequisite bound to `target_id`; must not claim: a moved window
3. **last-call** (`a.last-call`, after t2) — configure upgrade_blocker.clear relative to `preparation_window_ends_at`; cancelled by `named_requirement_satisfied` (the specific named requirement that blocked progress is recorded as satisfied), `change_withdrawn` (the change request is withdrawn); re-read before sending: the the target re-read from the system of record before acting on the timeout
   - checks: Is a second prompt still worth sending?
   - channel roles, in order: in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there) → persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Send one further prompt naming the same prerequisite and the date it stops mattering.
   - destination: clear-the-prerequisite bound to `target_id`; must not claim: a moved window

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.held` [success] held on a blocker the holder cannot clear — re-entry: if the blocker later becomes theirs to clear, the target qualifies again on the resolvable path
- `x.moot` [invalid-state] change withdrawn before the blocker was cleared — re-entry: the next change with the same prerequisite scopes this target again
- `x.unprepared` [timeout] preparation window closed with the blocker outstanding — re-entry: the target stays an unprepared member of the population and is prompted again by the next change that needs the same prerequisite
- handoff `h.resume` → RLT-242 (Change Readiness) — a named prerequisite cleared by the holder inside the preparation window

**Configure.**
- `upgrade_blocker.clear` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The point in the preparation window past which a target still blocked cannot be made ready in time.
- `upgrade_blocker.final` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — After the last call the instance waits until the preparation window itself closes; there is no third prompt.
- `upgrade_blocker.touches` — **recommended: 2** (confidence high; when the corpus rule: two prompts at most, both naming the same blocker) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `upgrade_blocker.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the target; a later instance concerns a different the target and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `target_held_on_named_prerequisite` (a target authoritatively held for a planned change; the specific prerequisite that is blocking it, named rather than described; a preparation window with time still in it); `named_requirement_satisfied` (the specific named requirement that blocked progress is recorded as satisfied); `change_withdrawn` (the change request is withdrawn). Attributes: `target_id`, `change_id`, `holder_id`, `blocking_prerequisite`, `preparation_window_ends_at`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 2 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.held, x.moot, x.unprepared, h.resume · business outcome: `named_requirement_satisfied` (the specific named requirement that blocked progress is recorded as satisfied) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution touched-before-event, comparison pre-post · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** upgrade blocker reminder · update blocked by prerequisite · prepare for the upgrade · migration prerequisite reminder · rollout readiness prompt

## INC-254 · Incident Update

Customer journey · category incident · slug `incident-communication`

**Objective.** Tell the people actually affected something true and useful, through the mechanism that already owns delivery.

**Trigger.** `incident_reaches_communication_relevant_state` (an incident state change that could change what an affected party should do or expect) [authoritative]. Requires: an incident state change that could change what an affected party should do or expect. Not enough on its own: time having passed since the last update, which is not itself information.

**Entity.** the incident, the affected recipient cohort and the communication obligation it creates — instance `incident_id + recipient_cohort_id + state_change_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an incident state change that could change what an affected party should do or expect
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the incident
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No root cause or resolution is claimed before it is confirmed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No promotional content appears in incident communication.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Uncertain technical detail is never communicated as fact.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The affected cohort is identified as precisely as the evidence allows.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `single-notice`

1. **communicate** (`a.communicate`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Can the affected cohort be identified with reasonable precision? · Is what would be said actually confirmed? · Does this materially change the guidance the recipient already has?
   - channel roles, in order: persistent (email: the message has to be kept and survive until the person can act on it) → in-session (in-app: the person is active in the product and the action is taken there)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Raise the communication through the canonical communication mechanism, which owns the obligation, the recipient resolution, the channels, the permissions and the delivery evidence.

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.no-send` [no-action] no update sent; nothing material had changed — re-entry: the next material change, or the next point a commitment requires an update, brings this back
- `x.updated` [success] update issued; the next update is bound to a stated condition rather than to a clock — re-entry: the stated condition occurring, or a material change, produces the next update
- `x.closed-comms` [success] resolution communicated to this recipient scope — re-entry: a relapse is communicated as a relapse rather than as a new incident, because the recipients were told it was over


**Configure.**
- `incident_communication.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `incident_communication.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: one instance per entity) — This journey is per the incident; a later instance concerns a different the incident and no cooldown applies between them.

**Required data.** Events: `incident_reaches_communication_relevant_state` (an incident state change that could change what an affected party should do or expect). Attributes: `incident_id`, `recipient_cohort_id`, `state_change_id`, `confirmed_facts`, `prior_guidance`, `incident_log`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class service · local cap 1 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.no-send, x.updated, x.closed-comms · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** incident update · status page update · outage communication · service disruption notice · incident notification


# Silent lifecycle states

## ACQ-01 · Anonymous Identity Resolution

Silent lifecycle state · category acquisition · slug `anonymous-intent-to-qualified-entry`

**Objective.** Carry a meaningful but anonymous intent signal through identity resolution without inventing an identity, and decide lifecycle entry as a question separate from having resolved one.

**Trigger.** `anonymous_intent_threshold_reached` (repeated visits to high-intent pages; interaction with pricing; product or configuration exploration; a meaningful return after a first session) [behavioral]. Requires: repeated visits to high-intent pages; interaction with pricing; product or configuration exploration; a meaningful return after a first session. Not enough on its own: a single page view; one session with no return; an ad click that landed and bounced.

**Entity.** anonymous_profile, reconciled onto person or account — instance `anonymous_profile_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- repeated visits to high-intent pages
- interaction with pricing
- product or configuration exploration
- a meaningful return after a first session
- no instance of this journey is already open for the anonymous_profile
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Anonymous behaviour is not consent. Resolving an identity does not create permission to contact it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Identities are merged on deterministic signals only. A probabilistic match is a guess, and a wrong merge writes one person's history onto another.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Becoming known is not the same as becoming eligible, and neither is a reason to start messaging.

**Stops when.**
- `x.stale` [timeout] anonymous, intent stale, no identity claimed — re-entry: a fresh crossing of the intent threshold opens a new instance; nothing was merged and no permission was implied by waiting
- `x.known-only` [success] known profile, no lifecycle entered — re-entry: ACQ-06 re-evaluates eligibility when the underlying data changes; becoming known does not start nurture by itself
- handoff `h.qualification` → ACQ-05 (Qualification State Routing) — a known, eligible profile entering qualification for the first time

**Configure.**
- `anonymous_intent.identity` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The freshness window of the signals that opened the profile.

**Required data.** Events: `anonymous_intent_threshold_reached` (repeated visits to high-intent pages; interaction with pricing; product or configuration exploration; a meaningful return after a first session); `deterministic_identity_resolved` (a deterministic known identity is resolved for the anonymous profile). Attributes: `anonymous_profile_id`, `intent_signals`, `signal_freshness_window`, `identity_resolution_history`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.stale, x.known-only, h.qualification · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** anonymous identity resolution · anonymous visitor intent · identity stitching · known-visitor resolution


---

## ACQ-02 · Interest Qualification Routing

Silent lifecycle state · category acquisition · slug `captured-interest-to-destination`

**Objective.** Route first-party interest to the destination its own content justifies, instead of treating every capture as either a sales lead or a subscriber.

**Trigger.** `first_party_interest_captured` (a first-party submission: a form, a content request, a contact request, or an equivalent deliberate act) [declared]. Requires: a first-party submission: a form, a content request, a contact request, or an equivalent deliberate act. Not enough on its own: an ad click; a page visit; an email open.

**Entity.** lead, resolved onto person or account — instance `lead_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a first-party submission: a form, a content request, a contact request, or an equivalent deliberate act
- no instance of this journey is already open for the lead
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A captured lead is not a sales-qualified lead. Capture records interest; qualification is a separate decision with its own state.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A submitted form is not marketing consent unless permission was explicitly given in it. The submission and the permission are two facts, and only one of them may have happened.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Not every lead needs a sales handoff. A destination is chosen from what the person asked for, not from what the pipeline wants.

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.destination` → external:destination-lifecycle — a declared request that maps to an enterable destination · contract: account_id, lead_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.reason` → ACQ-05 (Qualification State Routing) — a disqualifying condition found at capture
- handoff `h.education` → ACQ-09 (Lead Nurture) — legitimate interest with no destination it is ready for

**Required data.** Events: `first_party_interest_captured` (a first-party submission: a form, a content request, a contact request, or an equivalent deliberate act). Attributes: `lead_id`, `capture_record`, `declared_destination`, `disqualifiers`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.destination, h.reason, h.education · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** interest qualification routing · lead routing · form submission routing · inbound interest triage


---

## ACQ-03 · Intent Escalation Handoff

Silent lifecycle state · category acquisition · slug `intent-escalation-handoff`

**Objective.** Move ownership when someone in a low-intent lifecycle does something that no longer fits it, and make sure the journey being left behind actually goes quiet.

**Trigger.** `intent_threshold_crossed` (an act materially stronger than the one that placed the person in their current journey: pricing after content, configuration after browsing, starting a quote after general interest) [behavioral]. Requires: an act materially stronger than the one that placed the person in their current journey: pricing after content, configuration after browsing, starting a quote after general interest. Not enough on its own: one email click; one page view; one return session.

**Entity.** person plus the entity the new intent is about — instance `person_id + intent_entity_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an act materially stronger than the one that placed the person in their current journey: pricing after content, configuration after browsing, starting a quote after general interest
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person plus the entity the new intent is about
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One weak engagement signal is not high intent, and escalating on it moves ownership to a lifecycle the person has not earned.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Suppression happens before the handoff, not after. A handoff that leaves queued sends alive delivers the state the person just left.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Escalation carries context forward. Starting the higher-intent journey from zero makes the person repeat themselves.

**Stops when.**
- `x.unchanged` [no-action] no ownership change — re-entry: a later, stronger or repeated signal opens a new instance
- `x.retained` [success] current journey retains ownership — re-entry: a further escalation re-opens the question; nothing about the current journey changed
- handoff `h.escalate` → external:higher-intent-lifecycle — intent materially increased for this entity · contract: person_id, handed_at, reason

**Required data.** Events: `intent_threshold_crossed` (an act materially stronger than the one that placed the person in their current journey: pricing after content, configuration after browsing, starting a quote after general interest). Attributes: `person_id`, `intent_entity_id`, `current_lifecycle`, `signal_strength_evidence`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.unchanged, x.retained, h.escalate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** intent escalation · intent upgrade · lifecycle escalation · higher-intent handoff


---

## ACQ-04 · High-Intent Lead Routing

Silent lifecycle state · category acquisition · slug `high-intent-human-or-automated-route`

**Objective.** Decide, after a commercially serious act, whether the next step needs a person's judgement or can continue automatically - and resolve what already exists before creating anything.

**Trigger.** `high_intent_commercial_action` (a pricing request, an enterprise contact request, a completed quote, a sales-qualified submission, or a high-value application) [declared]. Requires: a pricing request, an enterprise contact request, a completed quote, a sales-qualified submission, or a high-value application. Not enough on its own: viewing a pricing page without requesting anything; opening a sales email.

**Entity.** lead, opportunity or account — instance `lead_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a pricing request, an enterprise contact request, a completed quote, a sales-qualified submission, or a high-value application
- no instance of this journey is already open for the lead
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] High intent is not an automatic sales call. The human route is a decision with a real alternative, not the default dressed as one.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An open opportunity is updated, never duplicated. Two records for one pursuit produce two people contacting the same account.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An already-converted account does not receive acquisition communication, whatever the intent signal says.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Destination reached for this entity while the journey is in flight: ACQ-08 takes ownership and this journey's remaining steps are suppressed

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `single-notice`

1. **assign** (`a.assign`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Has this account already reached the destination this action would pursue? · Does this action need human judgement?
   - channel roles, in order: human (sales/task: the next step needs a person's judgement - an owner is assigned and a task raised with the evidence)
   - discretionary · priority service · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Create or update the internal commercial entity, assign an owner, and raise a task carrying the evidence that justified it - ownership changes are appended so the trail of who held it survives

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.p1 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.reached` → ACQ-08 (Acquisition Exit Handoff) — a high-intent action arriving after the destination was already reached
- handoff `h.human` → external:human-in-the-loop-lifecycle — an assigned owner now holding the next step · contract: account_id, lead_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.automated` → external:automated-continuation — a self-serve request that needs no human judgement · contract: account_id, lead_id, handed_at, reason

**Configure.**
- `high_intent.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `high_intent.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high) — Routing is per high-intent action; an open opportunity is updated, never duplicated, and no cooldown applies between actions.

**Required data.** Events: `high_intent_commercial_action` (a pricing request, an enterprise contact request, a completed quote, a sales-qualified submission, or a high-value application). Attributes: `lead_id`, `account_id`, `commercial_entity_link`, `opportunity`, `ownership_history`, `destination_reached`.

**Collision & priority.** default service · pressure class none · local cap 1 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition purchase-intent (product; above browse and decay, below a reached commercial destination; on loss: superseded)

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.reached, h.human, h.automated · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** high-intent lead routing · sales handoff · MQL to SQL routing · demo request routing · lead assignment

## ACQ-05 · Qualification State Routing

Silent lifecycle state · category acquisition · slug `qualification-state-change-routing`

**Objective.** Treat qualification as a reversible state whose routing depends on why it changed, rather than a label applied once and trusted afterwards.

**Trigger.** `qualification_state_changed` (an authoritative transition between UNQUALIFIED, QUALIFYING, QUALIFIED, DISQUALIFIED and RECYCLE_ELIGIBLE, carrying the reason it changed) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative transition between UNQUALIFIED, QUALIFYING, QUALIFIED, DISQUALIFIED and RECYCLE_ELIGIBLE, carrying the reason it changed. Not enough on its own: a score crossing a threshold with no reason recorded against it.

**Entity.** lead, account or opportunity — instance `lead_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative transition between UNQUALIFIED, QUALIFYING, QUALIFIED, DISQUALIFIED and RECYCLE_ELIGIBLE, carrying the reason it changed
- no instance of this journey is already open for the lead
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Disqualified is not permanently dead unless the reason is terminal. Four reasons arrive at the same label and only one of them ends anything.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A qualification state is never written without its reason, and never overwrites the reason that came before it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A recycle is tied to a condition or a date that was actually recorded. Where none exists, no follow-up schedule is invented to stand in for one.

**Stops when.**
- `x.in-progress` [no-action] qualifying, no commercial escalation yet — re-entry: the next authoritative state change opens a new instance
- `x.not-yet` [no-action] unqualified, nothing ruling it out — re-entry: new evidence can move this to QUALIFYING without anything having to be undone first
- `x.terminal` [invalid-state] disqualified, terminal mismatch — re-entry: none from this reason - only a change in what we serve, which is a change to the rule rather than to the account
- `x.recycled` [success] re-entered qualification — re-entry: already re-entered; the new instance owns what follows
- `x.recycle-expired` [timeout] recycle horizon passed without the condition being met — re-entry: a new inbound signal can start qualification again from the beginning, with the old history intact
- `x.requirement-lapsed` [timeout] disqualified, requirement never met — re-entry: satisfying the requirement later is a new authoritative state change and re-enters normally
- handoff `h.destination` → external:commercial-destination — qualification reaching QUALIFIED · contract: account_id, lead_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.merge` → external:account-master-data — a duplicate or already-held relationship · contract: account_id, lead_id, handed_at, reason

**Configure.**
- `qualification_state.recycle` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — A recycle is tied to the condition or date recorded alongside the reason; the wait ends at that recorded horizon and nothing invents one.
- `qualification_state.requirement` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The validity horizon of the requirement.

**Required data.** Events: `qualification_state_changed` (an authoritative transition between UNQUALIFIED, QUALIFYING, QUALIFIED, DISQUALIFIED and RECYCLE_ELIGIBLE, carrying the reason it changed); `reentry_condition_met` (the re-entry condition or date recorded when the lead was recycled is reached); `named_requirement_satisfied` (the specific named requirement that blocked progress is recorded as satisfied). Attributes: `lead_id`, `qualification_state`, `state_reason`, `recycle_horizon_at`, `requirement_validity_ends_at`, `qualification_history`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.in-progress, x.not-yet, x.terminal, x.recycled, x.recycle-expired, x.requirement-lapsed, h.destination, h.merge · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** qualification state routing · lead qualification · MQL and SQL state · disqualification routing · lead recycling


---

## ACQ-06 · Eligibility Recalculation

Silent lifecycle state · category acquisition · slug `dynamic-eligibility-consequence`

**Objective.** Re-decide eligibility as the underlying data changes, and separate what it forbids next from what it does not undo.

**Trigger.** `eligibility_inputs_changed_or_reevaluated` (a change in data an eligibility rule reads, or a scheduled re-evaluation of that rule) [authoritative]. Requires: a change in data an eligibility rule reads, or a scheduled re-evaluation of that rule. Not enough on its own: a change in data that no eligibility rule reads; a re-evaluation with no rule input changed and no schedule behind it; a score or segment moving without the rule that decides eligibility being re-run.

**Entity.** person, account or the business entity the rule is about — instance `entity_ref + rule_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a change in data an eligibility rule reads, or a scheduled re-evaluation of that rule
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Eligibility is not availability. Being permitted to have something says nothing about whether it can currently be supplied.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Eligibility is not an entitlement already granted. Losing the first does not retract the second.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A future eligibility loss does not automatically invalidate an existing obligation; the obligation is reconciled on its own terms.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Every eligibility result names the rule and the input that produced it. A bare no cannot be explained, appealed or debugged.

**Stops when.**
- `x.eligible` [success] eligible, recorded with the rule that decided it — re-entry: any change to the inputs re-opens the evaluation
- `x.ineligible` [success] ineligible for new actions, nothing outstanding reversed — re-entry: restored eligibility is an ordinary re-evaluation and needs no special case
- handoff `h.reconcile` → external:commitment-reconciliation — eligibility lost while an obligation is outstanding · contract: account_id, person_id, handed_at, reason

**Required data.** Events: `eligibility_inputs_changed_or_reevaluated` (a change in data an eligibility rule reads, or a scheduled re-evaluation of that rule). Attributes: `entity_ref`, `rule_id`, `rule_inputs`, `eligibility_decisions`, `outstanding_commitments`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.eligible, x.ineligible, h.reconcile · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** eligibility recalculation · dynamic eligibility · eligibility rule re-evaluation · entitlement eligibility check


---

## ACQ-07 · Intent Decay

Silent lifecycle state · category acquisition · slug `intent-decay-cooldown`

**Objective.** Let a recorded high-intent state expire when the evidence behind it goes stale, instead of pursuing someone on the strength of something they did once.

**Trigger.** `intent_freshness_threshold_passed` (a recorded qualified or high-intent state whose supporting evidence is now older than the freshness window for that signal type) [behavioral]. Requires: a recorded qualified or high-intent state whose supporting evidence is now older than the freshness window for that signal type. Not enough on its own: a fixed number of days since a signal, applied without reading the signal's own freshness; quiet in one channel while intent is being renewed elsewhere; a decline, which is a decision and belongs to decline routing.

**Entity.** person plus the intent context that was recorded — instance `person_id + intent_context_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a recorded qualified or high-intent state whose supporting evidence is now older than the freshness window for that signal type
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person plus the intent context that was recorded
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An old pricing visit does not create a permanent high-intent flag. Evidence expires whether or not anything replaces it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Intent decay does not change marketing permission. Consent was given deliberately and is only withdrawn deliberately.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Decay is not a decline. Nothing here writes a negative outcome against a person who simply went quiet.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A new strong signal can establish a new intent state; the decayed one does not have to be argued away first.

**Stops when.**
- `x.unchanged` [no-action] intent state maintained — re-entry: the next freshness threshold re-opens the question
- `x.cooled` [timeout] intent expired, permission unchanged, no decision recorded — re-entry: a new strong signal establishes a new intent state from scratch
- handoff `h.customer` → external:customer-lifecycle — decayed acquisition intent over a live customer relationship · contract: person_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.re-escalate` → ACQ-03 (Intent Escalation Handoff) — a new strong signal arriving during cooldown

**Configure.**
- `intent_decay.cooldown` · cooldown — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The cooldown horizon for this intent context; a new strong signal inside it re-escalates, and its end closes the decayed intent with no decision recorded.

**Required data.** Events: `intent_freshness_threshold_passed` (a recorded qualified or high-intent state whose supporting evidence is now older than the freshness window for that signal type); `strong_intent_signal` (a new behavioural signal strong enough to re-open intent arrives). Attributes: `person_id`, `intent_context_id`, `intent_state`, `supporting_evidence`, `freshness_threshold`, `intent_history`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.unchanged, x.cooled, h.customer, h.re-escalate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** intent decay · lead decay · intent expiry · stale intent de-prioritisation


---

## ACQ-08 · Acquisition Exit Handoff

Silent lifecycle state · category acquisition · slug `destination-reached-acquisition-suppression`

**Objective.** Make acquisition give up ownership the moment the outcome it existed to cause is recorded, and stop what it has already queued.

**Trigger.** `authoritative_destination_event` (a recorded business fact: trial started, subscription started, purchase completed, booking confirmed, application submitted, or an opportunity created where that is the destination) [authoritative]. Requires: a recorded business fact: trial started, subscription started, purchase completed, booking confirmed, application submitted, or an opportunity created where that is the destination. Not enough on its own: an email click; a landing page visit; a form view; a checkout that was started but not completed.

**Entity.** person or account plus the destination entity — instance `person_id + destination_entity_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a recorded business fact: trial started, subscription started, purchase completed, booking confirmed, application submitted, or an opportunity created where that is the destination
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person or account plus the destination entity
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An email click is not a conversion. A landing page visit is not a conversion. Only the system of record decides that the destination was reached.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Suppression is scoped to the entity: one order completing does not close the journeys about a different order.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Suppression reaches sends that are already queued, not only future scheduling.

**Stops when.**
- `x.not-conversion` [invalid-state] no destination recorded, nothing suppressed — re-entry: the real event, if it happens, arrives from the system of record and opens a proper instance
- handoff `h.next` → external:next-lifecycle — the destination state being recorded · contract: contact_point_id, account_id, handed_at, reason

**Required data.** Events: `authoritative_destination_event` (a recorded business fact: trial started, subscription started, purchase completed, booking confirmed, application submitted, or an opportunity created where that is the destination). Attributes: `person_id`, `destination_entity_id`, `destination_event`, `queued_acquisition_sends`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.not-conversion, h.next · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** acquisition exit · conversion handoff · acquisition suppression on conversion · stop marketing on purchase


---

## ACQ-10 · Commercial Decline Routing

Silent lifecycle state · category acquisition · slug `commercial-decline-reason-routing`

**Objective.** Route a negative commercial outcome by its cause rather than filing every one of them under lost.

**Trigger.** `explicit_decline_or_authoritative_lost_outcome` (a decline stated by the person or account, or a lost outcome recorded in the system of record) [authoritative]. Requires: a decline stated by the person or account, or a lost outcome recorded in the system of record. Not enough on its own: silence; a missed meeting; an unanswered email.

**Entity.** lead, opportunity or account — instance `lead_id + decline_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a decline stated by the person or account, or a lost outcome recorded in the system of record
- no instance of this journey is already open for the lead
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The lost reason history is preserved. Routing depends on it, so overwriting it destroys the ability to route at all.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Not now is not never. Only a terminal reason ends acquisition permanently.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A declined opportunity is not swept into generic marketing as a consolation.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Where no re-entry condition was recorded, none is invented. The absence of a date is not an invitation to pick one.

**Stops when.**
- `x.terminal` [invalid-state] declined, terminal mismatch — re-entry: none from this reason - what would have to change is what we sell, not what this account decided
- `x.cooldown` [no-action] declined, recycle-eligible, nothing scheduled — re-entry: a new inbound signal, or a re-entry condition recorded later; no cadence is invented to fill the silence
- handoff `h.decay` → ACQ-07 (Intent Decay) — an opportunity closed for silence rather than for a decision
- handoff `h.classify` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a decline recorded as OTHER
- handoff `h.requalify` → ACQ-05 (Qualification State Routing) — the recorded re-entry condition being met

**Configure.**
- `commercial_decline.reentry` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The re-entry wait ends at the event, date or condition recorded alongside the reason; nothing is scheduled where none is known.

**Required data.** Events: `explicit_decline_or_authoritative_lost_outcome` (a decline stated by the person or account, or a lost outcome recorded in the system of record); `reentry_condition_met` (the re-entry condition or date recorded when the lead was recycled is reached). Attributes: `lead_id`, `decline_id`, `reason_family`, `reentry_at`, `decline_history`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.terminal, x.cooldown, h.decay, h.classify, h.requalify · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** commercial decline routing · closed-lost routing · lost reason handling · closed-lost recycling

## ACT-11 · Onboarding Route Assignment

Silent lifecycle state · category activation · slug `onboarding-route-selection`

**Objective.** Choose the onboarding path from the work actually required to reach value, before any of that work starts.

**Trigger.** `authoritative_lifecycle_entry` (a recorded entry: account created, trial started, subscription started, or customer onboarding started) [authoritative]. Requires: a recorded entry: account created, trial started, subscription started, or customer onboarding started. Not enough on its own: a signup form submitted but not completed; an invitation sent but not accepted.

**Entity.** person, account, subscription or trial - the thing that was entered — instance `account_id + onboarding_instance_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a recorded entry: account created, trial started, subscription started, or customer onboarding started
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Plan tier is not onboarding complexity. A large customer with simple setup does not need an implementation, and a small one with an integration does.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A high-value account does not automatically get human assistance. Value decides how much the outcome matters, not how much work reaching it takes.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The route follows the setup and use-case actually in front of us, not who the account is.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `single-notice`

1. **assisted** (`a.assisted`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Does reaching value here require assisted onboarding?
   - channel roles, in order: human (task: the step is carried out by a person - a call, a task, a visit - and recorded as done by them)
   - discretionary · priority lifecycle · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Record the assisted route and raise the internal task that gives this onboarding a human owner - the route is a property of the onboarding, carried into every step that follows

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.requirement` → ACT-13 (Onboarding Blocker Reminder) — a mandatory prerequisite missing before onboarding can begin
- handoff `h.progress` → ACT-12 (Onboarding Nurture) — a route chosen and nothing blocking the start

**Configure.**
- `onboarding_route.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `onboarding_route.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high; when the entity note: the thing that was entered) — Route assignment is per onboarding instance; a later entry is its own instance and no cooldown applies.

**Required data.** Events: `authoritative_lifecycle_entry` (a recorded entry: account created, trial started, subscription started, or customer onboarding started). Attributes: `account_id`, `onboarding_instance_id`, `onboarding_context`, `onboarding_route`, `setup_required`.

**Collision & priority.** default lifecycle · pressure class lifecycle · local cap 1 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition none

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.requirement, h.progress · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** onboarding route assignment · assisted vs self-serve onboarding · onboarding path selection · onboarding triage


---

## ACT-16 · Onboarding Completion Handoff

Silent lifecycle state · category activation · slug `activation-stops-onboarding`

**Objective.** Make onboarding let go the moment activation is recorded, including the messages it has already queued.

**Trigger.** `authoritative_core_activation_event` (the product's own record that core value was produced) [authoritative]. Requires: the product's own record that core value was produced. Not enough on its own: a click on an onboarding email; a completed setup checklist; a login; a feature opened but not used to any end.

**Entity.** person or account plus the onboarding instance being closed — instance `account_id + person_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- the product's own record that core value was produced
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person or account plus the onboarding instance being closed
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Activation is never determined by a click on an onboarding email. The message is not the milestone.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Onboarding completion is not product mastery. Closing onboarding says value was produced once, nothing more.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Invalidation reaches queued sends, not only future scheduling. The reminder that arrives after success is the one people remember.

**Stops when.**
- `x.not-activation` [invalid-state] no activation recorded; onboarding continues untouched — re-entry: the real event, when it happens, arrives from the product and opens a proper instance
- handoff `h.adoption` → ACT-17 (Adoption Nurture) — activation recorded and onboarding closed

**Required data.** Events: `authoritative_core_activation_event` (the product's own record that core value was produced). Attributes: `account_id`, `person_id`, `onboarding_outcome`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.not-activation, h.adoption · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** onboarding completion · activation achieved · stop onboarding messages · onboarding close-out


---

## RET-21 · Engagement Reclassification

Silent lifecycle state · category retention · slug `engagement-state-reclassification`

**Objective.** Hold engagement as a state that moves in both directions, and decide separately whether a movement is worth acting on.

**Trigger.** `engagement_state_materially_changed` (a recalculated engagement state - HIGH, NORMAL, DECLINING, LOW or DORMANT - that differs from the one on record; computed from meaningful usage, frequency, recency, depth, value-producing actions, the expected cadence and the maturity of the relationship) [behavioral]. Requires: a recalculated engagement state - HIGH, NORMAL, DECLINING, LOW or DORMANT - that differs from the one on record; computed from meaningful usage, frequency, recency, depth, value-producing actions, the expected cadence and the maturity of the relationship. Not enough on its own: a raw session count; email opens or clicks; a login streak with nothing produced behind it.

**Entity.** person or account, per product or service relationship — instance `account_id + relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a recalculated engagement state - HIGH, NORMAL, DECLINING, LOW or DORMANT - that differs from the one on record
- computed from meaningful usage, frequency, recency, depth, value-producing actions, the expected cadence and the maturity of the relationship
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person or account
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A raw session count is not engagement. Someone opening the product daily and producing nothing is not more engaged than someone producing something monthly.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Email opens are not relationship health. They measure the message.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Expected engagement varies by product and use-case, so the state is always computed against this relationship's own cadence.

**Stops when.**
- `x.updated` [success] engagement state updated, nothing triggered — re-entry: the next material change re-opens this; most passes through this journey correctly end here, having changed a state and sent nothing
- handoff `h.health` → RET-23 (Health Deterioration Diagnosis) — an anomalous engagement decline worth diagnosing

**Required data.** Events: `engagement_state_materially_changed` (a recalculated engagement state - HIGH, NORMAL, DECLINING, LOW or DORMANT - that differs from the one on record; computed from meaningful usage, frequency, recency, depth, value-producing actions, the expected cadence and the maturity of the relationship). Attributes: `account_id`, `relationship_id`, `engagement_state_history`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.updated, h.health · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** engagement reclassification · engagement scoring · engagement state · engagement decline detection


---

## RET-22 · Usage Gap Assessment

Silent lifecycle state · category retention · slug `expected-usage-miss-context-check`

**Objective.** Read a missed usage expectation as evidence only where an expectation genuinely existed, and only where something else corroborates it.

**Trigger.** `expected_usage_milestone_or_cadence_missed` (a usage expectation that was actually established for this relationship and role, and was not met) [behavioral]. Requires: a usage expectation that was actually established for this relationship and role, and was not met. Not enough on its own: a fixed no-activity-for-N-days rule applied without an expected pattern behind it; one missed period in a rhythm that has always been irregular.

**Entity.** person or account plus the product or use-case the expectation belongs to — instance `account_id + use_case_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a usage expectation that was actually established for this relationship and role, and was not met
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person or account plus the product or use
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No login for seven days is not a universal churn rule. It is a rule about one product's cadence and it does not travel.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Different roles in the same account carry different usage expectations, and are measured separately.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Absence is evidence only relative to an expectation that actually existed. Where none did, nothing was missed.

**Stops when.**
- `x.normal-quiet` [no-action] quiet period within the normal pattern; observation continues, nothing sent — re-entry: a miss that departs from the episodic pattern itself - a season skipped, an event cycle missed - re-opens this properly
- `x.observe` [no-action] observation state; a miss with nothing behind it is not risk — re-entry: a second miss, or any corroborating signal, re-opens this - absence accumulates into evidence, it does not start as evidence
- handoff `h.health` → RET-23 (Health Deterioration Diagnosis) — a missed expectation corroborated by other negative evidence

**Required data.** Events: `expected_usage_milestone_or_cadence_missed` (a usage expectation that was actually established for this relationship and role, and was not met). Attributes: `account_id`, `use_case_id`, `expected_pattern`, `last_usage_at`, `corroborating_evidence`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.normal-quiet, x.observe, h.health · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** usage gap assessment · usage drop · missed usage milestone · inactivity check · expected cadence miss


---

## RET-23 · Health Deterioration Diagnosis

Silent lifecycle state · category retention · slug `health-deterioration-diagnosis`

**Objective.** Send a deteriorating relationship to the mechanism that is actually breaking it, and never to a generic retention campaign in its place.

**Trigger.** `health_state_crossed_deterioration_threshold` (a health state or score crossing a meaningful threshold, together with the underlying evidence that moved it) [authoritative]. Requires: a health state or score crossing a meaningful threshold, together with the underlying evidence that moved it. Not enough on its own: a composite score that cannot be decomposed into what moved it; a threshold crossing driven entirely by a change in how the score is calculated.

**Entity.** person, account, subscription or customer relationship - whichever the health state is held against — instance `account_id + relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a health state or score crossing a meaningful threshold, together with the underlying evidence that moved it
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A health score on its own is not a reason to contact anyone. The evidence behind it is.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A composite score has to say which input moved it. One that cannot be decomposed cannot be routed on, and routing on it anyway sends every cause the same message.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A marketing incentive is not the default recovery. It is the response to exactly one cause, and only where policy supports it.

**Stops when.**
- `x.need-changed` [success] health fell because the need changed; nothing is broken — re-entry: a new need, or a return of the old one, re-opens this - a relationship winding down because it is finished is not a failure to recover from
- `x.cause-found` [success] cause identified during the diagnostic window — re-entry: the identified cause opens a new instance and routes on the first pass, which keeps the routing decision in one place rather than duplicating it inside the diagnostic
- `x.unexplained` [timeout] deterioration real, cause not found; lower-frequency monitoring — re-entry: further deterioration or a corroborating signal re-opens this; an unexplained decline is watched rather than treated
- handoff `h.adoption` → ACT-18 (Adoption Recovery) — deterioration driven by adoption falling away
- handoff `h.setup` → ACT-13 (Onboarding Blocker Reminder) — deterioration traced to a missing or broken setup dependency
- handoff `h.technical` → external:human-in-the-loop-lifecycle — an unresolved technical issue or repeated support friction · contract: subscription_id, account_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.service` → RET-26 (Service Recovery) — deterioration caused by a service failure on our side
- handoff `h.payment` → external:payment-recovery — deterioration traced to billing or payment · contract: subscription_id, account_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.ownership` → external:relationship-ownership-reconciliation — the person the relationship ran through changing or leaving · contract: subscription_id, account_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.recovery` → RET-27 (Recovery Stability Check) — health improving during the diagnostic window

**Configure.**
- `health_deterioration.diagnostic` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — A bounded diagnostic window.

**Required data.** Events: `health_state_crossed_deterioration_threshold` (a health state or score crossing a meaningful threshold, together with the underlying evidence that moved it); `cause_identified` (a cause for the deterioration becomes identifiable from the evidence); `health_recovered` (the health signal returns to its normal range without intervention). Attributes: `account_id`, `relationship_id`, `health_state`, `score_inputs`, `health_evidence`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.need-changed, x.cause-found, x.unexplained, h.adoption, h.setup, h.technical, h.service, h.payment, h.ownership, h.recovery · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** health deterioration diagnosis · churn risk diagnosis · account health drop · health score decline routing
