## RET-24 · Churn Risk Escalation

Silent lifecycle state · category retention · slug `churn-risk-escalation`

**Objective.** Decide how hard to push back on a relationship at risk, in proportion to how much independent evidence there actually is.

**Trigger.** `churn_risk_threshold_crossed` (several independent churn-relevant signals crossing a defined threshold together: sustained meaningful usage decline, a failed renewal or payment, a negative support experience, repeated unresolved blockers, explicit dissatisfaction, exploration of cancellation, a key stakeholder leaving, falling account-wide adoption) [authoritative]. Requires: several independent churn-relevant signals crossing a defined threshold together: sustained meaningful usage decline, a failed renewal or payment, a negative support experience, repeated unresolved blockers, explicit dissatisfaction, exploration of cancellation, a key stakeholder leaving, falling account-wide adoption. Not enough on its own: a single weak signal; high account value, which describes what is at stake rather than the likelihood of losing it; a risk score with no decomposable evidence behind it.

**Entity.** customer, account or subscription relationship — instance `account_id + risk_episode_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- several independent churn-relevant signals crossing a defined threshold together: sustained meaningful usage decline, a failed renewal or payment, a negative support experience, repeated unresolved blockers, explicit dissatisfaction, exploration of cancellation, a key stakeholder leaving, falling account-wide adoption
- no instance of this journey is already open for the customer
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A single weak signal never constitutes churn risk. Corroboration between independent signals is what the threshold is measuring.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A high-value customer is not automatically at high risk. Value is what is at stake, not the probability of losing it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A risk score is not the outcome. It orders attention; it does not decide anything.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The size of the intervention tracks the strength of the evidence. An expensive save offer on thin evidence teaches customers what to do when they want one.

**Recommended orchestration** · strategy `single-notice`

1. **owner-task** (`a.owner-task`) — on classification, no wait
   - checks: Has explicit cancellation intent already been expressed? · Is the risk driven by a known operational problem, other than a payment failure already open in payment recovery? · Does the evidence justify a person?
   - channel roles, in order: human (task: the step is carried out by a person - a call, a task, a visit - and recorded as done by them)
   - discretionary · priority retention · [CANONICAL_RULE]
   - purpose: Raise a task for the account owner or customer success, carrying the evidence rather than the score, and suppress automated retention on this relationship so the person is not contradicted by a sequence while they work

Delivery fallback: same-role-other-channel. No action when: s.g1, s.g2, s.g3, s.g4 (see Suppressed when).

**Stops when.**
- `x.monitor` [no-action] risk recorded, nothing proportionate to do — re-entry: stronger or fresher evidence re-opens this at a higher level - doing nothing is a legitimate response to weak evidence, and doing something disproportionate is not
- handoff `h.cancellation` → RET-28 (Cancellation Save) — cancellation intent already on record when risk escalates
- handoff `h.resolve-first` → RET-23 (Health Deterioration Diagnosis) — risk with an identifiable operational cause
- handoff `h.human` → external:human-in-the-loop-lifecycle — risk strong enough to justify a person · contract: subscription_id, account_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.intervention` → RET-30 (Retention Offer Follow-Up) — a proportionate automated retention intervention being delivered

**Configure.**
- `churn_risk.touches` — **recommended: 1** (confidence high; when GLB-24; the graph's own touch count) — Every touch runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; the budget is the plan's own length, and no touch is repeated because nothing could tell whether it arrived.
- `churn_risk.cooldown` — **recommended: none** (confidence high) — Escalation is per risk episode; the same episode re-crossing the threshold is the same instance and a later episode is its own.

**Required data.** Events: `churn_risk_threshold_crossed` (several independent churn-relevant signals crossing a defined threshold together: sustained meaningful usage decline, a failed renewal or payment, a negative support experience, repeated unresolved blockers, explicit dissatisfaction, exploration of cancellation, a key stakeholder leaving, falling account-wide adoption). Attributes: `account_id`, `risk_episode_id`, `risk_evidence`, `cancellation_intent_ref`, `operational_cause_ref`, `retention_ownership`.

**Collision & priority.** default retention · pressure class none · local cap 1 (recommended) (all) · cooldown none (recommended) · competition retention-outreach (account; below an open issue under human ownership and below a declared cancellation intent on the same account, above generic retention intervention; on loss: suppressed)

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.monitor, h.cancellation, h.resolve-first, h.human, h.intervention · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: complaint, message_after_success, unsubscribe

**Aliases.** churn risk escalation · churn risk · at-risk account escalation · customer success alert · churn prevention (risk)

## RET-27 · Recovery Stability Check

Silent lifecycle state · category retention · slug `recovery-observation-buffer`

**Objective.** Keep the distance between a good sign and an actual recovery, so a relapse is still being watched for when it happens.

**Trigger.** `positive_signal_after_deterioration` (a positive behaviour in a context that had deteriorated or received a recovery intervention) [behavioral]. Requires: a positive behaviour in a context that had deteriorated or received a recovery intervention. Not enough on its own: a single login; a single payment attempt, which is an attempt rather than a restored payment relationship; an opened or clicked message.

**Entity.** person or account plus the health or risk context that deteriorated — instance `account_id + person_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a positive behaviour in a context that had deteriorated or received a recovery intervention
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person or account plus the health or risk context that deteriorated
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One login is not recovery. It is the event that starts watching for one.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One payment attempt may not be payment recovery. An attempt and a restored payment relationship are different facts.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The observation window is set from the use-case. A window that fits a daily product declares a quarterly one recovered before anything has been proven.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Recovery observation delays declaring the relationship stably recovered. It does not by itself require communication to be suppressed: an authoritative event - a purchase, a completed renewal, an explicit request to stay - can immediately end an inactivity or reactivation restriction and return the person to normal current-state orchestration while this journey is still observing. Operational eligibility restored and health confidently stable are different conclusions with different evidence.

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.rediagnose` → RET-23 (Health Deterioration Diagnosis) — deterioration returning inside the stability window
- handoff `h.normal` → external:customer-lifecycle — recovery sustained through the stability window · contract: account_id, person_id, handed_at, reason

**Configure.**
- `recovery_observation.stability` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The stability window appropriate to this use-case and this kind of deterioration.

**Required data.** Events: `positive_signal_after_deterioration` (a positive behaviour in a context that had deteriorated or received a recovery intervention); `deterioration_signal_returns` (the deterioration signal reappears after a recovery). Attributes: `account_id`, `person_id`, `recovery_state_history`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.rediagnose, h.normal · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** recovery stability check · recovery observation · relapse watch · post-recovery monitoring

## RET-29 · Cancellation Wind-Down

Silent lifecycle state · category retention · slug `cancellation-completed-wind-down`

**Objective.** End retention ownership the moment cancellation is real, and manage what is still outstanding without pretending the relationship is either fully over or still winnable.

**Trigger.** `authoritative_cancellation_completed` (the system of record showing the cancellation as executed) [authoritative]. Requires: the system of record showing the cancellation as executed. Not enough on its own: a cancel flow entered; a cancellation request that is still reversible; a stated intention to cancel.

**Entity.** the subscription, membership or service relationship that ended — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- the system of record showing the cancellation as executed
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the subscription
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Cancellation is not data deletion. Nothing here removes anything.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Cancellation is not account closure. The account survives the subscription that ended.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An end-of-period cancellation does not revoke paid entitlement early unless policy says so. They have paid for the remainder.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Invalidation runs before anything else, because everything else can wait and a save offer cannot be un-sent.

**Stops when.**
- `x.former` [success] former customer; relationship ended, account and data untouched — re-entry: returning is a new relationship handled by win-back, not by reviving this one. Closing the account and deleting the data are two further states with their own triggers, and neither of them happened here
- `x.wind-down` [success] cancelled, in wind-down until the end date — re-entry: reversal before the end date is possible and is its own event; the wind-down state exists precisely so that window is representable rather than collapsed into an ending
- handoff `h.obligations` → external:obligation-resolution — obligations outstanding after cancellation · contract: subscription_id, relationship_id, handed_at, reason

**Required data.** Events: `authoritative_cancellation_completed` (the system of record showing the cancellation as executed). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `cancellation_executed_at`, `effective_end_at`, `outstanding_obligations`, `termination_state`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.former, x.wind-down, h.obligations · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** cancellation wind-down · post-cancellation · stop retention after cancellation · cancelled relationship close-out

## CON-31 · Permission Validation

Silent lifecycle state · category consent · slug `permission-capture-and-scope`

**Objective.** Turn a permission decision into an auditable record of what exactly was authorised, rather than a flag that says yes.

**Trigger.** `explicit_permission_decision_received` (a deliberate act of granting or refusing permission, identifying what is being permitted) [declared]. Requires: a deliberate act of granting or refusing permission, identifying what is being permitted. Not enough on its own: creating an account; providing an email address or phone number; accepting terms of service where consent was not separately given; a pre-ticked box.

**Entity.** person plus the permission record, keyed by purpose, channel and scope — instance `person_id + purpose_channel_scope_key`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a deliberate act of granting or refusing permission, identifying what is being permitted
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person plus the permission record
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Creating an account is not consent. Nor is providing contact details, which is how someone reaches a service rather than an invitation to be marketed to.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Permission on one channel never transfers to another. Email consent is not SMS consent.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An ambiguous scope is read narrowly. The broad reading is the one that has to be asked for.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A refusal is recorded as a decision, not as an absence - so that nothing later reads the silence as room to assume.

**Stops when.**
- `x.rejected` [invalid-state] not recorded as permission — re-entry: a genuine permission decision later creates one normally; nothing partial is stored that a later process could mistake for consent
- `x.active` [success] permission active for the captured purpose, channel and scope — re-entry: any later decision on the same combination opens a new instance; changes to an active permission are CON-35's, not this journey's


**Required data.** Events: `explicit_permission_decision_received` (a deliberate act of granting or refusing permission, identifying what is being permitted). Attributes: `person_id`, `purpose_channel_scope_key`, `decision`, `scope_statement`, `captured_at`, `permission_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.rejected, x.active · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** permission validation · consent capture · consent record · opt-in validation · consent scope

## CON-32 · Preference Capture

Silent lifecycle state · category consent · slug `preference-capture`

**Objective.** Record how someone would like permitted communication done, in a store that structurally cannot become permission.

**Trigger.** `preference_explicitly_set_or_changed` (the person deliberately setting or changing a preference: frequency, topic, category, language, preferred channel, content type) [declared]. Requires: the person deliberately setting or changing a preference: frequency, topic, category, language, preferred channel, content type. Not enough on its own: behaviour that suggests a preference; the channel someone happened to reply on; a topic they clicked once.

**Entity.** person plus the declared-preference profile — instance `person_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- the person deliberately setting or changing a preference: frequency, topic, category, language, preferred channel, content type
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person plus the declared
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A preference is not consent. Preferring email says nothing about wanting marketing.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Inferred behaviour and declared preference stay in separate stores. The confidence attached to them is different and the merge cannot be undone.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A preference set where no permission exists is stored and applied to nothing.

**Stops when.**
- `x.stored-only` [success] preference stored, shaping nothing — re-entry: if permission is granted later the stored preference applies from that moment; the preference waited rather than authorised anything
- handoff `h.recalculate` → CON-33 (Preference Recalculation) — a preference that will change what already-running journeys should do

**Required data.** Events: `preference_explicitly_set_or_changed` (the person deliberately setting or changing a preference: frequency, topic, category, language, preferred channel, content type). Attributes: `person_id`, `declared_preferences`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.stored-only, h.recalculate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** preference capture · preference centre · communication preferences · channel preference

## CON-33 · Preference Recalculation

Silent lifecycle state · category consent · slug `preference-change-recalculation`

**Objective.** Make a preference change reach the messages already sitting in a queue, not just the profile field.

**Trigger.** `authoritative_preference_changed` (a recorded preference change with its previous value) [authoritative]. Requires: a recorded preference change with its previous value. Not enough on its own: a preference inferred from behaviour rather than declared; a permission change, which is a different fact and reaches queued sends through suppression, not here.

**Entity.** person plus the preference plus every active journey instance and queued action it touches — instance `person_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a recorded preference change with its previous value
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person plus the preference plus every active journey instance and queued action it touches
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A preference change does not rewrite what has already been sent.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A stale queued action never overrides a newer preference. Execution re-validates; scheduling is not a decision.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A preference change is not a permission change, however much it reduces what gets sent.

**Stops when.**
- `x.recalculated` [success] future orchestration aligned to the new preference; permission unchanged — re-entry: the next preference change re-opens this


**Required data.** Events: `authoritative_preference_changed` (a recorded preference change with its previous value). Attributes: `person_id`, `declared_preferences`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.recalculated · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** preference recalculation · preference change propagation · queued message recalculation · apply preference to running journeys


---

## CON-38 · Communication Suppression

Silent lifecycle state · category consent · slug `communication-suppression-state`

**Objective.** Make every reason something is not being sent an explicit, scoped, releasable state rather than an absence.

**Trigger.** `suppression_condition_became_active` (a condition that stops communication: permission withdrawn, a frequency policy, a contactability failure, a higher-priority journey taking ownership, a legal or policy restriction, a cooldown, a duplicate, an existing human resolution, or a temporary incident) [authoritative]. Requires: a condition that stops communication: permission withdrawn, a frequency policy, a contactability failure, a higher-priority journey taking ownership, a legal or policy restriction, a cooldown, a duplicate, an existing human resolution, or a temporary incident. Not enough on its own: a soft delivery failure, which is a transient route fact; a preference reduction, which reshapes communication rather than stopping it; an internal hold with no recorded reason.

**Entity.** the person, message or journey instance the suppression applies to, at the scope recorded with it — instance `person_id + suppression_scope`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a condition that stops communication: permission withdrawn, a frequency policy, a contactability failure, a higher-priority journey taking ownership, a legal or policy restriction, a cooldown, a duplicate, an existing human resolution, or a temporary incident
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the person
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Suppression is not deletion. Nothing is removed; sending is stopped.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Release is not replay. What was held is discarded and current eligibility is recalculated.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Different reasons carry different scopes, and a suppression without its scope cannot be released correctly.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Suppression we impose on ourselves is a separate state from permission the person gave us. A sender-side hold is not an unsubscribe, it is recorded against our own sending rather than against their consent, and nobody may read it as a decision they made.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Releasing a sender-side suppression asks for permission again rather than switching sending back on. Silence long enough to suppress for is not consent that survived it.

**Stops when.**
- `x.persistent` [suppression] suppressed until the underlying state changes — re-entry: a change to the state that caused it opens a new evaluation. Nothing was deleted here - the person, their history and their record are intact, and only sending is stopped
- `x.released` [success] suppression released; current state re-evaluated, backlog discarded — re-entry: any new suppression condition opens its own instance with its own reason and scope


**Configure.**
- `communication_suppression.release` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — A review horizon appropriate to the reason.

**Required data.** Events: `suppression_condition_became_active` (a condition that stops communication: permission withdrawn, a frequency policy, a contactability failure, a higher-priority journey taking ownership, a legal or policy restriction, a cooldown, a duplicate, an existing human resolution, or a temporary incident); `release_condition_met` (the condition recorded to release the suppression is met). Attributes: `person_id`, `suppression_scope`, `reason`, `release_condition`, `suppression_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.persistent, x.released · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** communication suppression · do-not-contact · suppression list · send hold · global suppression

## FBK-48 · Declared Context Recalculation

Silent lifecycle state · category feedback · slug `declared-context-recalculation`

**Objective.** Let something a person has told us about their situation reach the decisions that actually depend on it, and nothing else.

**Trigger.** `relevant_context_explicitly_declared` (the person stating a need, goal, situation or context relevant to what we do) [declared]. Requires: the person stating a need, goal, situation or context relevant to what we do. Not enough on its own: behaviour implying a need; a segment they were placed in by a model.

**Entity.** person or account plus the declared attribute, at the scope it was declared for — instance `account_id + person_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- the person stating a need, goal, situation or context relevant to what we do
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person or account plus the declared attribute
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Declared data is never overwritten by behavioural inference.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The attribute is used only where a decision genuinely depends on it. Personalisation that references something for the sake of referencing it reveals the mechanism.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A need or preference signal is not permission to communicate.

**Stops when.**
- `x.applied` [success] declared context stored and applied where it changes a decision — re-entry: a change or a revalidation of the same attribute opens a new instance
- `x.stored` [success] stored; no orchestration triggered — re-entry: a future decision that depends on it reads it then - an attribute that changes nothing today is still worth holding


**Required data.** Events: `relevant_context_explicitly_declared` (the person stating a need, goal, situation or context relevant to what we do). Attributes: `account_id`, `person_id`, `declared_context`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.applied, x.stored · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** declared context recalculation · zero-party data · declared need · self-declared preference signal

## FBK-50 · Relationship State Reassessment

Silent lifecycle state · category feedback · slug `relationship-evidence-accumulation`

**Objective.** Accumulate signals as dated evidence, and let a relationship label exist only where a policy defined what it means.

**Trigger.** `meaningful_relationship_signal` (a signal that says something about the relationship: positive or negative feedback, a successful outcome, a repeated contribution, support friction, a declared preference, an engagement change, an advocacy action) [behavioral]. Requires: a signal that says something about the relationship: positive or negative feedback, a successful outcome, a repeated contribution, support friction, a declared preference, an engagement change, an advocacy action. Not enough on its own: an open or a click, which describes a message rather than a relationship.

**Entity.** person or account plus the relationship context the signal was observed in — instance `account_id + relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a signal that says something about the relationship: positive or negative feedback, a successful outcome, a repeated contribution, support friction, a declared preference, an engagement change, an advocacy action
- no instance of this journey is already open for the person or account plus the relationship context the signal was observed in
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One signal does not silently create a permanent label. LOYAL, ADVOCATE, AT_RISK, VIP and DETRACTOR exist only where a policy defines what they mean and what removes them.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Evidence decays, and how fast depends on what is being assessed rather than on a single global rule.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Signals stay scoped to the entity they were observed on.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Communication engagement is not relationship evidence and does not enter this store.

**Stops when.**
- `x.owned` [success] recorded as evidence; the action is owned elsewhere — re-entry: the journey that owns the action is triggered by the same signal independently - this one records, it does not dispatch, and duplicating the dispatch here is how two systems respond to one event
- `x.evidence` [success] evidence recorded; state unchanged — re-entry: the next signal is weighed against a slightly larger set
- `x.reassessed` [success] relationship state reassessed against the current evidence set — re-entry: further signals may move it again, in either direction
- `x.no-label` [success] evidence recorded; no label created — re-entry: a policy defining the label makes it assignable later, against this same evidence - a label nobody defined is one nobody can dispute, remove, or explain to the person carrying it


**Required data.** Events: `meaningful_relationship_signal` (a signal that says something about the relationship: positive or negative feedback, a successful outcome, a repeated contribution, support friction, a declared preference, an engagement change, an advocacy action). Attributes: `account_id`, `relationship_id`, `relationship_evidence`, `relationship_state_history`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.owned, x.evidence, x.reassessed, x.no-label · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** relationship state reassessment · loyalty state · relationship evidence · customer state labelling

## TIM-62 · Overdue State Recalculation

Silent lifecycle state · category time · slug `due-state-change`

**Objective.** Change what an unresolved obligation costs operationally when it becomes due or overdue, rather than only announcing that it has.

**Trigger.** `due_state_changed` (an obligation entering DUE_SOON, DUE or OVERDUE against a real deadline) [authoritative]. Requires: an obligation entering DUE_SOON, DUE or OVERDUE against a real deadline. Not enough on its own: a report showing an item as old, with no deadline behind it.

**Entity.** the obligation or work item whose due state changed — instance `obligation_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an obligation entering DUE_SOON, DUE or OVERDUE against a real deadline
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the obligation or work item whose due state changed
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The overdue state and its consequences are business-defined. Where no consequence exists, the item is tracked rather than acted on.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Not every overdue item requires customer communication. Most overdue work is an internal problem.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Due-state changes append to the deadline history rather than overwriting the original deadline.

**Stops when.**
- `x.stale` [suppression] stale due-state action suppressed — re-entry: a genuinely open obligation crossing a due threshold enters properly
- `x.tracked` [success] overdue and tracked; no consequence defined — re-entry: resolution closes it and a further due threshold re-opens it. An overdue item with no defined consequence is a reporting fact, and inventing a consequence to have something to do with it is the failure this branch prevents
- handoff `h.orphan` → OWN-51 (Task Assignment) — an overdue obligation with nobody responsible for it
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — an overdue obligation whose policy escalates it
- handoff `h.consequence` → external:overdue-consequence — an overdue obligation carrying a defined operational consequence · contract: obligation_id, handed_at, reason

**Required data.** Events: `due_state_changed` (an obligation entering DUE_SOON, DUE or OVERDUE against a real deadline). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `suppressed_sends`, `deadline_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.stale, x.tracked, h.orphan, h.escalate, h.consequence · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** overdue state recalculation · overdue handling · past due · due-state change

## TIM-65 · Grace Period Management

Silent lifecycle state · category time · slug `grace-period`

**Objective.** Keep limited continuity while something recoverable is unresolved, and end the grace period at the moment recorded when it began - never later because activity happened.

**Trigger.** `primary_validity_ended_with_grace_policy` (primary validity ending, and a grace policy that applies to this entity type) [authoritative]. Requires: primary validity ending, and a grace policy that applies to this entity type. Not enough on its own: an informal tolerance nobody wrote down, which is not grace but inconsistency.

**Entity.** the subscription, account, entitlement or obligation whose primary validity ended — instance `entity_id + grace_period_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- the primary validity of the entity has ended and a grace policy exists for it
- the grace end is computed and recorded at entry from policy, in the entity's own time

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No grace period opens without a policy that defines it; an entity whose validity ended with no grace policy expires (TIM-64) rather than lingering in an invented grace state.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Activity, engagement or a partial recovery never extends the recorded grace end. The end is a fact recorded at entry.

**Stops when.**
- `x.recovered` [success] recovered within grace; active state restored — re-entry: a further lapse enters grace again, and the history of how often that happens is itself worth reading
- handoff `h.revalidate` → ACQ-06 (Eligibility Recalculation) — recovery arriving after eligibility may have changed
- handoff `h.expire` → TIM-64 (Expiry Validation) — grace ending with the entity due to expire
- handoff `h.terminate` → external:termination-lifecycle — grace ending with access or the relationship ending · contract: entity_id, grace_period_id, person_id, grace_end, unrecovered_condition

**Configure.**
- `time.grace_end` · attribute-bound — **recommended: grace_end as recorded at entry** (confidence high) — The grace end is recorded at entry from policy and is the timeout itself. Nothing later moves it.

**Required data.** Events: `primary_validity_ended_with_grace_policy` (primary validity ending, and a grace policy that applies to this entity type); `recovery_condition_satisfied` (the condition that ends the recoverable state is satisfied). Attributes: `entity_id`, `grace_period_id`, `person_id`, `recovery_condition`, `grace_end`, `grace_policy_id` (optional: `restricted_capabilities`).

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.recovered, h.expire, h.terminate, h.revalidate · business outcome: `recovery_condition_satisfied` (the condition that ends the recoverable state is satisfied) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution entered-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: grace_extended_after_entry, service_continued_past_grace_end

**Aliases.** grace period · dunning grace · past-due grace · soft expiry


---

## ACC-71 · Entitlement Qualification

Silent lifecycle state · category access · slug `entitlement-qualification`

**Objective.** Establish whether a right actually exists, as a state distinct from being eligible for one or having paid toward one.

**Trigger.** `entitlement_basis_evaluated_or_changed` (an authoritative basis for a right: a completed purchase, an activated plan, an assigned role, an earned benefit, an effective contract, or a satisfied policy condition) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative basis for a right: a completed purchase, an activated plan, an assigned role, an earned benefit, an effective contract, or a satisfied policy condition. Not enough on its own: a payment authorised but not settled; eligibility for a plan nobody has bought; a marketing promise, which describes what we offer rather than what has been granted.

**Entity.** the person or account plus the specific entitlement, at its own scope and validity — instance `account_id + entitlement_key`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative basis for a right: a completed purchase, an activated plan, an assigned role, an earned benefit, an effective contract, or a satisfied policy condition
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the person or account plus the specific entitlement
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Eligibility is not entitlement. Being permitted to have something is not having it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A payment attempt is not an entitlement. Authorised is not settled, and settled is what establishes the right.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A marketing promise is not a granted right unless an authoritative business state confirms it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An existing entitlement is reconciled, never duplicated.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Qualified but capacity-blocked is pending rather than denied. Somebody who met every requirement and arrived when the allocation was full has not been refused, and recording it as a denial closes a case that policy may reopen with a waitlist or a later grant.

**Stops when.**
- `x.reconciled` [success] existing entitlement reconciled; no duplicate grant issued — re-entry: a further change to the basis re-opens this against the reconciled grant
- `x.pending` [no-action] PENDING_REQUIREMENT; the right is reachable and not yet held — re-entry: satisfying the named requirement re-opens this. Pending and denied are kept apart because the route back differs - one needs a step completed, the other needs the rules to change
- `x.denied` [failure] DENIED; the right is not available on this basis — re-entry: a different basis - a purchase, a role, a contract - is evaluated on its own terms
- handoff `h.provision` → ACC-72 (Entitlement Provisioning) — a right being established

**Required data.** Events: `entitlement_basis_evaluated_or_changed` (an authoritative basis for a right: a completed purchase, an activated plan, an assigned role, an earned benefit, an effective contract, or a satisfied policy condition). Attributes: `account_id`, `entitlement_key`, `basis`, `scope`, `validity`, `entitlement_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.reconciled, x.pending, x.denied, h.provision · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** entitlement qualification · entitlement check · access right established · grant or deny a right


---

## ACC-78 · Access Suspension

Silent lifecycle state · category access · slug `access-suspension`

**Objective.** Restrict defined capabilities for a reason, in the smallest scope that addresses it, while keeping restoration genuinely possible.

**Trigger.** `authoritative_suspension_decision` (a decision to suspend for a stated reason: a payment state, a security review, a policy review, a temporary operational restriction, or an administrative hold) [authoritative]. Requires: a decision to suspend for a stated reason: a payment state, a security review, a policy review, a temporary operational restriction, or an administrative hold. Not enough on its own: an outage, which removes access without anyone deciding to.

**Entity.** the account or person plus the specific capability scope being restricted — instance `account_id + capability_scope`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a decision to suspend for a stated reason: a payment state, a security review, a policy review, a temporary operational restriction, or an administrative hold
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the account or person plus the specific capability scope being restricted
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Suspended is not terminated. Suspension is designed to be reversible and its record says so.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The suspension uses the smallest scope that addresses its reason.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Restoration does not blindly return every previous capability - it hands to a journey whose job is revalidating first.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A suspension without a review point is a termination nobody authorised.

**Stops when.**
- `x.extended` [success] suspension extended; a new instance carries the new review point — re-entry: the extension runs as its own instance with its own end
- handoff `h.restore` → ACC-79 (Capability Restoration) — a suspension ending without a terminal decision
- handoff `h.terminate` → ACC-74 (Entitlement Revocation) — a review concluding that the right should end
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a suspension review point reached with no decision

**Configure.**
- `access_suspension.suspension` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The review point or the recorded expiry.

**Required data.** Events: `authoritative_suspension_decision` (a decision to suspend for a stated reason: a payment state, a security review, a policy review, a temporary operational restriction, or an administrative hold); `suspension_reason_resolved` (the reason for the suspension is resolved); `terminal_decision_recorded` (a terminal decision is recorded). Attributes: `account_id`, `capability_scope`, `reason`, `review_point_at`, `preserved_capabilities`, `suspension_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.extended, h.restore, h.terminate, h.escalate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** access suspension · suspend account · restricted access state · partial suspension


---

## ACC-79 · Capability Restoration

Silent lifecycle state · category access · slug `capability-restoration`

**Objective.** Rebuild access from what is currently valid, rather than replaying the capability set someone used to have.

**Trigger.** `restoration_condition_satisfied` (the condition that removed or restricted access appearing to be resolved) [authoritative]. Requires: the condition that removed or restricted access appearing to be resolved. Not enough on its own: time passing on a restriction whose reason was never addressed.

**Entity.** the person or account plus each capability being considered for restoration — instance `account_id + restoration_case_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- the condition that removed or restricted access appearing to be resolved
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the person or account plus each capability being considered for restoration
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Previous access is not a current access right.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Restoration never resurrects expired credentials, withdrawn permissions, expired entitlements, deleted resources or obsolete roles.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Obsolete restriction actions are invalidated as part of restoring, not left to fire afterwards.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A partial restoration names what did not come back.

**Stops when.**
- `x.restored` [success] capabilities restored against current valid state — re-entry: a further restriction and restoration is its own cycle
- `x.partial` [success] partially restored; what did not return is named — re-entry: each capability not restored re-enters when its own requirement is met - the subset that is missing is a list rather than a vague sense that something is wrong
- `x.remains` [no-action] still restricted; nothing currently qualifies for restoration — re-entry: previous access is not a current access right, and regaining it means meeting the requirements now rather than having met them before


**Required data.** Events: `restoration_condition_satisfied` (the condition that removed or restricted access appearing to be resolved). Attributes: `account_id`, `restoration_case_id`, `previous_capabilities`, `currently_valid_capabilities`, `restoration_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.restored, x.partial, x.remains · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** capability restoration · restore access · reinstatement · lift suspension


---

## IDN-87 · Authentication Risk Assessment

Silent lifecycle state · category identity · slug `authentication-failure-pattern`

**Objective.** Tell an ordinary forgotten password apart from an account under attack, without converting the first into the second.

**Trigger.** `authentication_failure_pattern` (authentication failures crossing a meaningful threshold, or matching a pattern the detection considers notable) [inferred]. Requires: authentication failures crossing a meaningful threshold, or matching a pattern the detection considers notable. Not enough on its own: one failed password attempt, which is the most common event in this category; a single failure from an unfamiliar device.

**Entity.** the account plus the authentication activity observed against it — instance `account_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- authentication failures crossing a meaningful threshold, or matching a pattern the detection considers notable
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the account plus the authentication activity observed against it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] One failed password attempt is not a compromise.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A risk model's output is not a confirmed attacker. It orders investigation and concludes nothing.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Any security response uses the smallest necessary scope.

**Stops when.**
- `x.normal` [no-action] ordinary failure pattern; nothing security-specific applied — re-entry: the usual recovery routes remain open to them, unchanged. Treating this as an attack would lock out mostly legitimate people, which is the failure mode this branch exists to hold open
- handoff `h.recovery` → IDN-88 (Account Recovery Verification) — a legitimate-looking actor with no way through normal authentication
- handoff `h.security` → IDN-90 (Account Compromise Recovery) — a failure pattern that ordinary error does not explain

**Required data.** Events: `authentication_failure_pattern` (authentication failures crossing a meaningful threshold, or matching a pattern the detection considers notable). Attributes: `account_id`, `security_signal_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.normal, h.recovery, h.security · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** authentication risk assessment · failed login pattern · brute force detection · login anomaly


---

## IDN-88 · Account Recovery Verification

Silent lifecycle state · category identity · slug `account-recovery`

**Objective.** Give someone a way back into an account they can no longer authenticate to, without that route being weaker than the one it replaces.

**Trigger.** `account_recovery_initiated` (a valid recovery request against an identified account) [authoritative]. Requires: a valid recovery request against an identified account. Not enough on its own: a failed authentication with no recovery request behind it; a support conversation about being locked out that names no identified account; a destination supplied inside the request itself.

**Entity.** the account plus this recovery case — instance `recovery_case_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a valid recovery request against an identified account
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the account plus this recovery case
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Recovery does not weaken normal security controls. It is a different route to the same assurance, not a lower one.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Unsafe credentials and sessions are invalidated before replacement access is established, not after.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Account recovery is not identity merge.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The recovery window is not restarted by repeated attempts.

**Stops when.**
- `x.expired` [timeout] recovery window closed without sufficient proof — re-entry: a new recovery request opens its own case, with this one in the history
- `x.recovered` [success] secure control of the existing account restored — re-entry: this recovered one account and merged nothing. Two identities that turn out to be one person is a different problem with different evidence
- `x.more` [no-action] further evidence may be provided within the recovery window — re-entry: the same case continues; the window is not restarted by another attempt
- `x.denied` [failure] recovery denied; account access unchanged — re-entry: a new request with genuinely new evidence may be assessed; repeating the same evidence is not new evidence
- handoff `h.security` → IDN-90 (Account Compromise Recovery) — a recovery request arriving during an open security incident
- handoff `h.review` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — recovery evidence that needs a person to weigh it

**Configure.**
- `account_recovery.proof` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — Proof is waited for until the recovery window closes; the window is never restarted by repeated attempts.

**Required data.** Events: `account_recovery_initiated` (a valid recovery request against an identified account); `proof_of_control_provided` (sufficient proof of control or identity is provided); `recovery_attempt_failed` (the recovery attempt fails). Attributes: `recovery_case_id`, `account_id`, `required_assurance`, `recovery_window_ends_at`, `recovery_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.expired, x.recovered, x.more, x.denied, h.security, h.review · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** account recovery verification · prove control · recovery proof · locked-out recovery


---

## IDN-89 · Identity Attribute Update

Silent lifecycle state · category identity · slug `identity-attribute-change`

**Objective.** Change an identity attribute safely, and reconcile everything that depended on the old value independently rather than by inheritance.

**Trigger.** `identity_attribute_change` (a change to an identity attribute, requested by the holder or received from an authoritative source) [authoritative]. Requires: a change to an identity attribute, requested by the holder or received from an authoritative source. Not enough on its own: a contact point change, which contact verification (CON-264) owns; a display-name edit with nothing depending on it; a propagation of an old value arriving after a newer change.

**Entity.** the person or account plus the specific attribute changing — instance `account_id + attribute_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a change to an identity attribute, requested by the holder or received from an authoritative source
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the person or account plus the specific attribute changing
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Changing an email does not transfer the old address's contactability or its consent to the new one.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Identity history is not silently overwritten where auditability is required.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A stale update never restores a previous value - propagation carries origin and version so a late arrival is discarded.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A sensitive change is not applied on the strength of an existing session alone.

**Stops when.**
- `x.not-applied` [invalid-state] change not applied; the attribute stands as it was — re-entry: a new request is verified on its own terms. Nothing partial was written, so no downstream system holds a value that was never confirmed
- `x.rejected` [invalid-state] change rejected as invalid — re-entry: a corrected value may be submitted
- `x.reconciled` [success] identity updated; each dependent re-evaluated on its own terms — re-entry: each dependent's own journey owns what happens next - contactability, permission and credential lifecycles are separately triggered, because they have different owners and different rules
- `x.updated` [success] identity updated; nothing depended on the previous value — re-entry: a further change to the same attribute is assessed on its own sensitivity


**Configure.**
- `identity_attribute.verification` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The verification window for this change.

**Required data.** Events: `identity_attribute_change` (a change to an identity attribute, requested by the holder or received from an authoritative source); `verification_succeeded` (the verification succeeds); `verification_failed` (the verification fails). Attributes: `account_id`, `attribute_id`, `previous_value`, `requested_value`, `verification_policy`, `dependents`, `identity_change_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.not-applied, x.rejected, x.reconciled, x.updated · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** identity attribute update · change of name · identity data change · profile identity update


---

## IDN-90 · Account Compromise Recovery

Silent lifecycle state · category identity · slug `suspected-account-compromise`

**Objective.** Limit the damage a possible compromise could do while the question is still open, and reach a conclusion that can go either way.

**Trigger.** `material_compromise_signal` (a material signal: a credential-theft indication, an unauthorised sensitive change, a credible report from the owner, a high-confidence detection, or a suspicious session combined with a consequential action) [inferred]. Requires: a material signal: a credential-theft indication, an unauthorised sensitive change, a credible report from the owner, a high-confidence detection, or a suspicious session combined with a consequential action. Not enough on its own: a single failed login; a login from an unfamiliar location with no consequential action behind it.

**Entity.** the account plus this security incident and the scope it affects — instance `incident_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a material signal: a credential-theft indication, an unauthorised sensitive change, a credible report from the owner, a high-confidence detection, or a suspicious session combined with a consequential action
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the account plus this security incident and the scope it affects
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Suspected is not confirmed. The two are separate states and containment belongs to the first.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Containment does not erase forensic or audit history.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Restoration never blindly reactivates old credentials, sessions or permissions - it rebuilds from current valid state.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Unauthorised financial or business actions are corrected through their own dispute lifecycles, not from inside the incident.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Containment uses the smallest scope the evidence justifies, because most signals here are false positives.

**Stops when.**
- `x.continued` [timeout] incident open; scoped restriction continues — re-entry: the investigation continues and reaches its conclusion on its own. Suspected remains suspected - it does not become confirmed by lasting longer
- handoff `h.recover` → ACC-79 (Capability Restoration) — a confirmed compromise moving to secure restoration
- handoff `h.lift` → ACC-79 (Capability Restoration) — a cleared incident whose restrictions should be lifted
- handoff `h.review` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — an incident the automated path could not resolve within its SLA

**Configure.**
- `suspected_account.resolution` · decision-sla — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The resolution SLA for this incident class.

**Required data.** Events: `material_compromise_signal` (a material signal: a credential-theft indication, an unauthorised sensitive change, a credible report from the owner, a high-confidence detection, or a suspicious session combined with a consequential action); `owner_verified` (the account owner is verified); `security_review_concluded` (the security review concludes). Attributes: `incident_id`, `account_id`, `signal`, `scope`, `containment_applied`, `review_sla`, `security_incident_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.continued, h.recover, h.lift, h.review · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** account compromise recovery · account takeover response · compromise containment · security incident (account)


---

## REL-91 · Relationship Validation

Silent lifecycle state · category structure · slug `relationship-creation`

**Objective.** Create a link between two entities only where the relationship itself has an authoritative basis, and keep it a link rather than a consolidation.

**Trigger.** `relationship_creation_requested_or_detected` (a request to create a relationship, or an authoritative detection of one, naming both entities and the relationship type) [authoritative]. Requires: a request to create a relationship, or an authoritative detection of one, naming both entities and the relationship type. Not enough on its own: a shared email address or phone number; a shared surname or address; a shared device or network; an agreement being entered that carries a term, an effective period or a renewal model, which is a continuing relationship rather than a structural link.

**Entity.** the relationship, plus the two entities it connects, in the direction it was created — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a request to create a relationship, or an authoritative detection of one, naming both entities and the relationship type
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the relationship
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A shared attribute is not proof of a relationship. Two people can share an address, a surname and a device and be unrelated.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Creating a relationship is not an identity merge.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A long-lived link is not an agreement. Where the relationship carries a term, an effective period, a renewal model or lapse semantics, it belongs to the continuing-relationship lifecycle and not here.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Direction is preserved where the relationship's semantics are directional.

**Stops when.**
- `x.pending` [no-action] PENDING_EVIDENCE; the relationship is not active — re-entry: sufficient basis arriving later activates it. Nothing flows from a pending relationship, which is the difference between pending and active
- `x.rejected` [invalid-state] REJECTED; no relationship created — re-entry: a different basis is assessed on its own terms
- `x.active` [success] ACTIVE_RELATIONSHIP — re-entry: changes to it are REL-92's, and its ending is REL-93's
- handoff `h.verify` → IDN-82 (Verification Dependency Resolution) — a relationship whose creation depends on verification or acceptance

**Required data.** Events: `relationship_creation_requested_or_detected` (a request to create a relationship, or an authoritative detection of one, naming both entities and the relationship type). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `relationship_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.pending, x.rejected, x.active, h.verify · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** relationship validation · link two records · account linking · entity relationship creation


---

## REL-92 · Relationship Impact Recalculation

Silent lifecycle state · category structure · slug `relationship-change`

**Objective.** Recalculate exactly what depended on a relationship when it changes, and nothing else.

**Trigger.** `relationship_state_or_type_changed` (an authoritative change to an existing structural relationship's state or type between two entities) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative change to an existing structural relationship's state or type between two entities. Not enough on its own: a change to the plan, tier, scope or contractual terms of a continuing relationship, which changes what was agreed rather than how the entities are related; a pricing or renewal-term modification, which belongs to the agreement rather than to the link.

**Entity.** the relationship that changed and the entities connected by it — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative change to an existing structural relationship's state or type between two entities
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the relationship that changed and the entities connected by it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A relationship change is not an identity change.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A relationship change is not an ownership transfer unless responsibility actually moves.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A structural relationship change is not a change to the commercial or contractual terms of a continuing relationship.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Historical relationship periods are preserved.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Actions queued under the superseded relationship do not execute.

**Stops when.**
- `x.recorded` [success] relationship updated; nothing else recalculated — re-entry: the next change is assessed against the new relationship
- `x.recalculated` [success] dependent rights and obligations recalculated; nothing else touched — re-entry: a further change recalculates from the new state
- handoff `h.ownership` → OWN-54 (Ownership Transfer) — a relationship change that moves responsibility for an open obligation
- handoff `h.entitlement` → ACC-73 (Entitlement Recalculation) — a relationship change that alters an entitlement basis

**Required data.** Events: `relationship_state_or_type_changed` (an authoritative change to an existing structural relationship's state or type between two entities). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `relationship_log`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.recorded, x.recalculated, h.ownership, h.entitlement · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** relationship impact recalculation · relationship change · linked account change · dependency recalculation


---

## REL-93 · Entity Relationship End Reconciliation

Silent lifecycle state · category structure · slug `relationship-end`

**Objective.** Stop what a relationship was carrying forward without cancelling what it validly produced.

**Trigger.** `relationship_ended` (an authoritative end to an active structural relationship between two entities, with an effective time) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative end to an active structural relationship between two entities, with an effective time. Not enough on its own: a subscription, contract, policy or membership reaching its end, which ends a term rather than a structural link; a party's rights under an agreement stopping, which the agreement's own lifecycle owns.

**Entity.** the relationship that ended and the entities it connected — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative end to an active structural relationship between two entities, with an effective time
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the relationship that ended and the entities it connected
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Ending a relationship does not delete the historical relationship.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Ending a relationship does not automatically cancel commitments created while it existed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Unrelated relationships between the same entities remain unaffected.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Ending a structural link does not terminate an agreement that ran through it. A contract, subscription, policy or membership reaches its own end on its own terms, and inferring one from the other cancels things nobody cancelled.

**Stops when.**
- `x.ended` [success] relationship ended; history intact, obligations reconciled separately — re-entry: a new relationship between the same entities is created on its own basis, and is a different relationship rather than a revival of this one
- handoff `h.reconcile` → external:commitment-reconciliation — a relationship ending with obligations created under it still live · contract: relationship_id, handed_at, reason

**Required data.** Events: `relationship_ended` (an authoritative end to an active structural relationship between two entities, with an effective time). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `relationship_log`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.ended, h.reconcile · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** relationship end reconciliation · unlink accounts · relationship ended · link removal


---

## REL-94 · Role Authority Update

Silent lifecycle state · category structure · slug `role-change-delta`

**Objective.** Move what someone may do next, by the difference between two roles, without disturbing what they did under the old one.

**Trigger.** `role_changed` (an authoritative role change with an effective time) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative role change with an effective time. Not enough on its own: a permission edit that changes no role; an organisational change with no effective time recorded; a role change requested but not yet authorised.

**Entity.** the person or member, the role, and the account or organisation the role is held in — instance `account_id + member_id + role_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative role change with an effective time
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the person or member
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A role change is not an identity change.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A role downgrade does not invalidate actions historically performed under the previous role.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Pending decisions are revalidated against current authority before the delta is applied.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The delta is scoped - capabilities common to both roles are left alone.

**Stops when.**
- `x.applied` [success] authority and capability delta applied; prior actions intact — re-entry: a further role change is compared against the current role
- handoff `h.authority` → OWN-60 (Approval Owner Update) — a role change with decisions pending under the authority it replaces
- handoff `h.entitlement` → ACC-73 (Entitlement Recalculation) — a role change that only adds capabilities

**Required data.** Events: `role_changed` (an authoritative role change with an effective time). Attributes: `account_id`, `member_id`, `role_id`, `previous_role`, `new_role`, `effective_at`, `pending_decisions`, `role_change_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.applied, h.authority, h.entitlement · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** role authority update · role change · permission delta · admin role change

## REL-100 · Orphan Relationship Recovery

Silent lifecycle state · category structure · slug `orphaned-entity-resolution`

**Objective.** Make a missing required relationship an explicit, findable state rather than a null field that active work quietly runs against.

**Trigger.** `required_relationship_missing` (a relationship the entity requires to function being absent, invalid or removed - a work item without a required owner, a resource without a valid parent, a case without an account link, a dependent without a primary) [authoritative]. Requires: a relationship the entity requires to function being absent, invalid or removed - a work item without a required owner, a resource without a valid parent, a case without an account link, a dependent without a primary. Not enough on its own: an empty relationship field on an entity that does not require that relationship; a transient read failure of the relationship store; a relationship that is pending evidence, which validation (REL-91) owns.

**Entity.** the entity missing its required relationship, and the relationship type that is absent — instance `entity_ref + relationship_type`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a relationship the entity requires to function being absent, invalid or removed - a work item without a required owner, a resource without a valid parent, a case without an account link, a dependent without a primary
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the entity missing its required relationship
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A missing relationship never silently becomes a null field while active work continues.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A parent or owner is not guessed from weak evidence. Several candidates route to a person.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Inherited deadlines and obligations stay intact while the entity is orphaned.

**Stops when.**
- `x.resumed` [success] relationship restored; entity revalidated and resumed — re-entry: a further missing relationship opens its own orphan state
- `x.terminal` [timeout] orphaned entity handled under its policy end state — re-entry: a valid relationship becoming available later re-opens this; the entity was never silently discarded
- handoff `h.manual` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — an orphan with several possible replacements and no rule between them
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — an orphaned entity that could not be reconnected

**Configure.**
- `orphaned_entity.restore` · decision-sla — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The resolution SLA for this entity type.

**Required data.** Events: `required_relationship_missing` (a relationship the entity requires to function being absent, invalid or removed - a work item without a required owner, a resource without a valid parent, a case without an account link, a dependent without a primary); `relationship_established` (a valid relationship is restored or established). Attributes: `entity_ref`, `relationship_type`, `replacement_candidates`, `holding_scope`, `resolution_sla`, `orphan_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.resumed, x.terminal, h.manual, h.escalate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** orphan relationship recovery · orphaned record · missing parent · unowned entity


---

## TRM-105 · Responsibility Handover

Silent lifecycle state · category terminal · slug `responsibility-handover`

**Objective.** Move a role between two people at a defined moment, without changing anything before it or rewriting anything behind it.

**Trigger.** `responsibility_handover_authorized` (an authorized handover naming the outgoing actor, the incoming actor, the scope and the effective time) [authoritative]. Requires: an authorized handover naming the outgoing actor, the incoming actor, the scope and the effective time. Not enough on its own: a role change with no named incoming actor; a handover discussed but not authorised; an actor leaving with no handover recorded, which orphan handling owns.

**Entity.** the role or responsibility, its outgoing holder and its incoming one — instance `role_id + handover_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authorized handover naming the outgoing actor, the incoming actor, the scope and the effective time
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the role or responsibility
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A future handover is not an immediate authority change.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Historical decisions by the outgoing actor remain historical facts.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Inherited deadlines do not reset at the handover.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Both actors are revalidated at the effective time, not at the time of authorisation.

**Stops when.**
- `x.cancelled` [invalid-state] handover cancelled or superseded before its effective time — re-entry: the outgoing actor retained the role throughout, so nothing has to be undone - which is the point of not moving authority at the moment of authorisation
- `x.handed-over` [success] responsibility handed over at its effective time — re-entry: a further handover is scheduled and validated on its own terms
- handoff `h.hold` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a handover into an actor who cannot hold the role
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a handover whose assumptions did not survive to its effective time

**Configure.**
- `responsibility_handover.effective` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The handover waits until its own effective time; nothing changes before it, and both actors and the target are revalidated at it.

**Required data.** Events: `responsibility_handover_authorized` (an authorized handover naming the outgoing actor, the incoming actor, the scope and the effective time); `handover_cancelled` (the handover is cancelled); `handover_superseded` (the handover is superseded by another). Attributes: `role_id`, `handover_id`, `outgoing_actor`, `incoming_actor`, `effective_at`, `inherited_deadlines`, `handover_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.cancelled, x.handed-over, h.hold, h.escalate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** responsibility handover · role handover · owner transfer · account manager change

## TRM-107 · Account Closure Reconciliation

Silent lifecycle state · category terminal · slug `closure-external-dependencies`

**Objective.** Make sure nothing that lives outside the account is assumed to have ended because the account did.

**Trigger.** `account_closure_executing_or_finalizing` (an account closure that has taken effect on the account itself) [authoritative]. Requires: an account closure that has taken effect on the account itself. Not enough on its own: a closure requested but not yet applied to the account; a subscription cancellation, which ends a commercial relationship rather than the account.

**Entity.** the closing account plus each external or commercial dependency, individually — instance `account_id + closure_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an account closure that has taken effect on the account itself
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the closing account plus each external or commercial dependency
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Closing an application account is never assumed to cancel an externally billed subscription.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An external provider's reported success is verified rather than trusted.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A failure to terminate one dependency is not hidden behind an account marked CLOSED.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Each dependency's final state is recorded independently of the account's.

**Stops when.**
- `x.finalized` [success] dependencies reconciled and recorded independently of the account — re-entry: a dependency discovered later is reconciled on its own, against the closed account's record
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a dependency that did not terminate and cannot be left outstanding

**Configure.**
- `closure_external.outcomes` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The closure policy's window for dependency resolution.

**Required data.** Events: `account_closure_executing_or_finalizing` (an account closure that has taken effect on the account itself); `dependency_outcomes_recorded` (the required dependency outcomes are recorded). Attributes: `account_id`, `closure_id`, `external_dependencies`, `coupling_evidence`, `dependency_outcomes`, `closure_policy`, `closure_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.finalized, h.escalate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** account closure reconciliation · external dependency termination · closure finalisation · linked service termination


---

## TRM-108 · Account Closure Wind-Down

Silent lifecycle state · category terminal · slug `closure-wind-down`

**Objective.** Stop normal account activity while letting the obligations that outlive closure actually finish.

**Trigger.** `account_closure_succeeded` (a closure that has been executed and verified) [authoritative]. Requires: a closure that has been executed and verified. Not enough on its own: a closure requested, executing or not yet verified; a suspension, which is designed to be reversed.

**Entity.** the closed account and the obligations still outstanding against it — instance `account_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a closure that has been executed and verified
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the closed account and the obligations still outstanding against it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A closed account does not reactivate through a stale login or a queued onboarding event.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The wind-down capability is explicitly scoped and enumerated.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A terminal relationship state does not mean the historical record is deleted.

**Stops when.**
- `x.former` [success] FORMER; the relationship is over and the historical record is intact — re-entry: a returning customer opens a new relationship rather than reviving this one. A terminal relationship state does not mean the records never existed - deleting them is a separate lifecycle with its own request, its own scope and its own authority
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a wind-down outliving its horizon

**Configure.**
- `closure_wind.winddown` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The wind-down horizon.

**Required data.** Events: `account_closure_succeeded` (a closure that has been executed and verified); `operational_obligations_completed` (all outstanding operational obligations complete). Attributes: `account_id`, `outstanding_obligations`, `wind_down_capabilities`, `wind_down_horizon`, `closure_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.former, h.escalate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** account closure wind-down · post-closure obligations · closed account state · former customer state
