## FIN-131 · Financial Obligation Tracking

Silent lifecycle state · category financial · slug `financial-obligation-lifecycle`

**Objective.** Hold what is owed as its own state, independent of any attempt to pay it and of anything sent about it.

**Trigger.** `financial_obligation_created` (an authoritative record that an amount is owed, by whom, to whom, and arising from what) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative record that an amount is owed, by whom, to whom, and arising from what. Not enough on its own: an invoice being emailed, which is a communication about an obligation rather than the obligation; a reminder being scheduled.

**Entity.** the obligation itself - its amount, its currency, its payer and the business entity it arose from — instance `obligation_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative record that an amount is owed, by whom, to whom, and arising from what
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the obligation itself
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An invoice issued is not a payment received.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An outstanding obligation is not a payment failure.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Reminder activity does not define financial truth. What is owed is established by financial events, not by what was sent about it.

**Stops when.**
- `x.adjusted` [success] obligation adjusted; prior versions preserved — re-entry: the adjusted obligation continues its own lifecycle from its new amount
- `x.aged` [timeout] outstanding past its horizon — re-entry: what happens to an aged obligation is an accounting decision - writing it off is a policy act with its own authority, and this journey does not make it
- handoff `h.satisfy` → FIN-136 (Balance Reconciliation) — an authoritative financial event applying to this obligation
- handoff `h.due` → TIM-62 (Overdue State Recalculation) — the obligation becoming due or overdue

**Configure.**
- `financial_obligation.obligation` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The obligation's own horizon - its limitation period, its write-off point, or the end of the relationship it belongs to.

**Required data.** Events: `financial_obligation_created` (an authoritative record that an amount is owed, by whom, to whom, and arising from what); `obligation_satisfied` (the obligation is satisfied in the system of record); `obligation_adjusted_or_cancelled` (an adjustment or cancellation is recorded against the obligation); `obligation_due` (the obligation becomes due or overdue). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `obligation_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.adjusted, x.aged, h.satisfy, h.due · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** financial obligation tracking · invoice outstanding · amount owed state · receivable tracking

## FIN-136 · Balance Reconciliation

Silent lifecycle state · category financial · slug `obligation-satisfaction`

**Objective.** Apply a financial event to an obligation exactly once, and release only what actually depended on that obligation.

**Trigger.** `authoritative_financial_event_against_obligation` (an authoritative payment, credit or settlement that discharges some or all of an obligation) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative payment, credit or settlement that discharges some or all of an obligation. Not enough on its own: a payment authorised but not settled; a financial event that names no obligation; a reminder or dunning event, which is communication about an obligation.

**Entity.** the obligation and the financial event being applied to it — instance `obligation_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative payment, credit or settlement that discharges some or all of an obligation
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the obligation and the financial event being applied to it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A payment success is never applied twice.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Financial satisfaction does not automatically restore unrelated suspended capabilities.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Partial payment is not full payment, and the remainder is carried explicitly.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Amounts are not netted across incompatible currencies.

**Stops when.**
- `x.partial` [success] PARTIALLY_SATISFIED; the remaining balance is explicit and still outstanding — re-entry: further financial events apply against the remaining balance. Restrictions that depended on this obligation stay in force, because it is not discharged
- `x.satisfied` [success] SATISFIED; nothing else depended on it — re-entry: a later adjustment or reversal against this obligation is its own financial event, applied to a discharged balance rather than an outstanding one
- handoff `h.overpayment` → external:overpayment-or-credit — an amount applied exceeding what was owed · contract: obligation_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.restore` → ACC-79 (Capability Restoration) — an obligation whose payment removes the reason a capability was restricted

**Required data.** Events: `authoritative_financial_event_against_obligation` (an authoritative payment, credit or settlement that discharges some or all of an obligation). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `obligation_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.partial, x.satisfied, h.overpayment, h.restore · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** balance reconciliation · payment applied · obligation satisfied · partial payment handling

## FUL-141 · Fulfillment Request Validation

Silent lifecycle state · category fulfillment · slug `fulfillment-request-acceptance`

**Objective.** Decide whether we are taking responsibility for delivering something, as a state distinct from having been asked.

**Trigger.** `fulfillment_request_submitted` (a request to deliver an identified item, service or scope to an identified recipient) [authoritative]. Requires: a request to deliver an identified item, service or scope to an identified recipient. Not enough on its own: a payment succeeding, which discharges a financial obligation and does not by itself create a fulfillment one; an item placed in a basket or a slot browsed.

**Entity.** the fulfillment request and, once accepted, the obligation it creates — instance `request_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a request to deliver an identified item, service or scope to an identified recipient
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the fulfillment request and
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A request received is not a request accepted.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Payment success alone does not mean fulfillment was accepted.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An invalid request never creates a hidden fulfillment obligation.

**Stops when.**
- `x.rejected` [invalid-state] REJECTED; no fulfillment obligation created — re-entry: a corrected request is validated on its own terms
- `x.lapsed` [timeout] request lapsed with its dependency unresolved; no obligation created — re-entry: a fresh request is validated against whatever the dependency now looks like
- handoff `h.availability` → FUL-142 (Fulfillment Allocation) — an accepted obligation requiring resources to satisfy it

**Configure.**
- `fulfillment_request.dependency` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The request's validity window.

**Required data.** Events: `fulfillment_request_submitted` (a request to deliver an identified item, service or scope to an identified recipient); `dependency_resolved` (the named dependency resolves). Attributes: `request_id`, `requested_scope`, `recipient_id`, `validity_checks`, `dependencies`, `fulfillment_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.rejected, x.lapsed, h.availability · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** fulfillment request validation · order acceptance · order validation · accept or reject a fulfilment request

## FUL-142 · Fulfillment Allocation

Silent lifecycle state · category fulfillment · slug `fulfillment-availability`

**Objective.** Establish whether the resources to satisfy an accepted obligation actually exist, in the scope and window that would serve it.

**Trigger.** `accepted_fulfillment_requires_resources` (an accepted obligation whose satisfaction requires committing a resource or capacity) [authoritative]. Requires: an accepted obligation whose satisfaction requires committing a resource or capacity. Not enough on its own: a catalog availability figure; an order accepted with no resource requirement; a payment succeeding.

**Entity.** the obligation and the resources it requires, at the location and time that would serve this recipient — instance `obligation_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an accepted obligation whose satisfaction requires committing a resource or capacity
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the obligation and the resources it requires
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Catalog availability is not allocatable availability.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An accepted order is not allocated inventory.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Concurrent allocation does not oversell - the claim, not the reading, is what commits.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Availability is checked against the scope and time that would actually serve this obligation.

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.unavailable` → FUL-150 (Fulfillment Cancellation Reconciliation) — an obligation that cannot be resourced
- handoff `h.allocate` → FUL-143 (Resource Reservation) — resources existing that can serve this obligation

**Configure.**
- `fulfillment_availability.capacity` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The obligation's tolerance window.

**Required data.** Events: `accepted_fulfillment_requires_resources` (an accepted obligation whose satisfaction requires committing a resource or capacity); `resources_available` (the required resources become available). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `required_resources`, `location`, `service_window`, `partial_policy`, `fulfillment_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.unavailable, h.allocate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** fulfillment allocation · availability check · inventory availability · capacity check


---

## FUL-143 · Resource Reservation

Silent lifecycle state · category fulfillment · slug `resource-allocation`

**Objective.** Bind specific capacity to one obligation until it is consumed or deliberately let go.

**Trigger.** `resource_selected_for_fulfillment` (a specific resource identified as the one that will serve a specific obligation) [authoritative]. Requires: a specific resource identified as the one that will serve a specific obligation. Not enough on its own: a catalog quantity being positive; an order accepted before a specific resource is identified; a reservation request that lost its race for the resource.

**Entity.** the allocation itself - a link between a specific resource quantity and one fulfillment obligation — instance `allocation_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a specific resource identified as the one that will serve a specific obligation
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the allocation itself
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Allocation is idempotent.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A cancelled fulfillment does not retain scarce resource.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A release affects only this obligation's allocation, never another's or a shared claim.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A temporary reservation carries an explicit expiry or release condition.

**Stops when.**
- `x.consumed` [success] allocation consumed by the fulfillment it was made for — re-entry: a further resource need on the same obligation is its own allocation
- `x.released` [success] released; capacity returned, other claims untouched — re-entry: the resource is available to whatever claims it next
- `x.expired` [timeout] reservation expired unconsumed; obligation still needs resourcing — re-entry: the obligation returns to the availability question with nothing held for it
- handoff `h.recheck` → FUL-142 (Fulfillment Allocation) — a reservation losing a race for a resource that had appeared available
- handoff `h.exception` → FUL-145 (Fulfillment Exception Recovery) — a reserved resource becoming unavailable before it was consumed

**Configure.**
- `resource_allocation.allocation` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The reservation's validity, where it has one.

**Required data.** Events: `resource_selected_for_fulfillment` (a specific resource identified as the one that will serve a specific obligation); `allocation_consumed` (the fulfilment consumes the allocated resource); `fulfillment_cancelled_or_changed` (the fulfilment is cancelled or its scope changes); `reserved_resource_unavailable` (the reserved resource becomes unavailable). Attributes: `allocation_id`, `obligation_id`, `resource_ref`, `quantity`, `reservation_expires_at`, `allocation_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.consumed, x.released, x.expired, h.recheck, h.exception · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** resource reservation · inventory reservation · hold stock for an order · capacity hold


---

## FUL-144 · Fulfillment Execution

Silent lifecycle state · category fulfillment · slug `fulfillment-execution`

**Objective.** Track what the obligation's scope actually reaches, rather than what an internal step reported.

**Trigger.** `fulfillment_execution_started` (an obligation with its resources allocated, entering execution) [authoritative]. Requires: an obligation with its resources allocated, entering execution. Not enough on its own: a task started inside an internal system with no allocation behind it; a dispatch label created; a payment.

**Entity.** the fulfillment obligation and the scope of it that has been satisfied — instance `obligation_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an obligation with its resources allocated, entering execution
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the fulfillment obligation and the scope of it that has been satisfied
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An internal task succeeding is not fulfillment completed where the required business outcome has not been confirmed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Partial fulfillment preserves exactly what remains owed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The whole obligation is not marked failed when a confirmed scope was successfully completed.

**Stops when.**
- `x.partial` [success] PARTIALLY_FULFILLED; completed scope preserved and remaining scope explicit — re-entry: the remaining scope continues its own execution. What was delivered is delivered, and what is owed is stated rather than implied
- handoff `h.delay` → FUL-146 (Delivery Delay Alert) — fulfillment exceeding its expected window
- handoff `h.dispatch` → FUL-147 (Delivery Outcome Tracking) — a prepared item or service passing to a delivery executor
- handoff `h.confirm` → FUL-149 (Delivery Acceptance Finalization) — fulfillment whose completion is itself the delivery
- handoff `h.exception` → FUL-145 (Fulfillment Exception Recovery) — a material exception during execution
- handoff `h.remedy` → FUL-150 (Fulfillment Cancellation Reconciliation) — a scope that cannot be fulfilled

**Configure.**
- `fulfillment_execution.execution` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The expected fulfillment window.

**Required data.** Events: `fulfillment_execution_started` (an obligation with its resources allocated, entering execution); `execution_outcome_reported` (execution reports an outcome); `fulfillment_exception` (a material exception occurs during fulfilment). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `allocated_resources`, `expected_window`, `satisfied_scope`, `remaining_scope`, `fulfillment_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.partial, h.delay, h.dispatch, h.confirm, h.exception, h.remedy · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** fulfillment execution · order in progress · service delivery progress · execution tracking


---

## FUL-145 · Fulfillment Exception Recovery

Silent lifecycle state · category fulfillment · slug `fulfillment-exception`

**Objective.** Change only the part of an obligation the operational problem actually touches.

**Trigger.** `material_fulfillment_exception` (an operational problem affecting fulfillment: a resource unavailable, a damaged item, a provider unavailable, an incorrect configuration, capacity lost, a destination problem, a dependency failure or a quality failure) [authoritative]. Requires: an operational problem affecting fulfillment: a resource unavailable, a damaged item, a provider unavailable, an incorrect configuration, capacity lost, a destination problem, a dependency failure or a quality failure. Not enough on its own: a delay on its own, which changes timing rather than the ability to fulfill.

**Entity.** the exception and the scope of the obligation it affects — instance `exception_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an operational problem affecting fulfillment: a resource unavailable, a damaged item, a provider unavailable, an incorrect configuration, capacity lost, a destination problem, a dependency failure or a quality failure
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the exception and the scope of the obligation it affects
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An exception is not a cancellation.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A substitute is never assumed acceptable where the recipient would care about the difference.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Existing successfully fulfilled scope is preserved through the exception.

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.resume` → FUL-144 (Fulfillment Execution) — an exception resolved with the obligation continuing
- handoff `h.delay` → FUL-146 (Delivery Delay Alert) — an exception that changes only when the obligation is met
- handoff `h.terminal` → FUL-150 (Fulfillment Cancellation Reconciliation) — an exception that ends the affected scope of the obligation

**Configure.**
- `fulfillment_exception.approval` · response-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The approval window.

**Required data.** Events: `material_fulfillment_exception` (an operational problem affecting fulfillment: a resource unavailable, a damaged item, a provider unavailable, an incorrect configuration, capacity lost, a destination problem, a dependency failure or a quality failure); `substitute_approved` (the substitute is approved); `substitute_declined` (the substitute is declined). Attributes: `exception_id`, `obligation_id`, `affected_scope`, `substitute_candidate`, `approval_policy`, `fulfillment_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.resume, h.delay, h.terminal · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** fulfillment exception recovery · supply exception · damaged item handling · out-of-stock after order

## FUL-147 · Delivery Outcome Tracking

Silent lifecycle state · category fulfillment · slug `dispatch-and-delivery-tracking`

**Objective.** Transfer execution to whoever performs the delivery while the obligation stays ours and stays open.

**Trigger.** `fulfillment_handed_to_delivery_executor` (a prepared item or service passing to a carrier, technician or other delivery executor) [authoritative]. Requires: a prepared item or service passing to a carrier, technician or other delivery executor. Not enough on its own: a label printed or a job created, which prepares a dispatch rather than performing one.

**Entity.** the handoff to a delivery executor, and the obligation behind it — instance `dispatch_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a prepared item or service passing to a carrier, technician or other delivery executor
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the handoff to a delivery executor
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Dispatched is not delivered.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A tracking update is not necessarily a final delivery.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A provider timeout is not a delivery failure.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Duplicate delivery events are idempotent.

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.reconcile` → external:external-status-reconciliation — a delivery whose outcome could not be established · contract: order_id, obligation_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.confirm` → FUL-149 (Delivery Acceptance Finalization) — an authoritative delivery confirmation
- handoff `h.failed` → FUL-148 (Failed Delivery Recovery) — a confirmed failed delivery attempt
- handoff `h.delay` → FUL-146 (Delivery Delay Alert) — the executor reporting the delivery will be later

**Configure.**
- `dispatch_and.delivery` · external-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The expected delivery window plus its tolerance.

**Required data.** Events: `fulfillment_handed_to_delivery_executor` (a prepared item or service passing to a carrier, technician or other delivery executor); `delivery_confirmed` (delivery is confirmed by the executor or the recipient); `delivery_failed` (the delivery attempt is confirmed failed); `delivery_delay_reported` (the executor reports a meaningful delay). Attributes: `dispatch_id`, `obligation_id`, `executor`, `expected_delivery_window`, `delivery_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.reconcile, h.confirm, h.failed, h.delay · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

## FUL-149 · Delivery Acceptance Finalization

Silent lifecycle state · category fulfillment · slug `delivery-acceptance-finalization`

**Objective.** Separate arriving from being agreed to have arrived correctly, wherever that difference has business meaning.

**Trigger.** `authoritative_delivery_completion` (an authoritative confirmation that the recipient has what was owed) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative confirmation that the recipient has what was owed. Not enough on its own: a carrier scan that is not a completion; a dispatch; an internal task closing.

**Entity.** the delivered fulfillment and the recipient whose acceptance may still be required — instance `obligation_id + delivery_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative confirmation that the recipient has what was owed
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the delivered fulfillment and the recipient whose acceptance may still be required
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Delivered is not accepted where acceptance is contractually meaningful.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No acceptance window is invented beyond what policy defines.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Proof of delivery remains attached to the delivery history.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Finalisation does not erase later rights that policy independently provides.

**Stops when.**
- `x.finalized` [success] FINALIZED; the obligation is discharged and the remaining scope is zero — re-entry: later rights that policy provides independently are exercised on their own terms and do not reopen this state
- handoff `h.issue` → REM-151 (Post-Purchase Issue Recovery) — an issue raised within a valid post-delivery window

**Configure.**
- `delivery_acceptance.acceptance` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The acceptance deadline defined by the contract or policy.
- `delivery_acceptance.window` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The issue window closing.

**Required data.** Events: `authoritative_delivery_completion` (an authoritative confirmation that the recipient has what was owed); `delivery_accepted` (the recipient accepts what was delivered); `delivery_issue_raised` (the recipient raises an issue about the delivery). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `delivery_id`, `acceptance_required`, `acceptance_window_ends_at`, `issue_window_ends_at`, `proof_of_delivery`, `delivery_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.finalized, h.issue · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** delivery acceptance finalization · proof of delivery · acceptance window · post-delivery issue window

## FUL-150 · Fulfillment Cancellation Reconciliation

Silent lifecycle state · category fulfillment · slug `fulfillment-cancellation`

**Objective.** Stop what remains of an obligation while keeping everything that already happened.

**Trigger.** `fulfillment_cancellation_effective` (an authoritative cancellation that has taken effect on the obligation) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative cancellation that has taken effect on the obligation. Not enough on its own: a cancellation requested, which is an intent until it becomes effective.

**Entity.** the obligation, split into what is completed, what is in progress and what has not started — instance `obligation_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative cancellation that has taken effect on the obligation
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the obligation
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A cancellation requested is not a cancellation.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A cancellation for fraud, compliance or manual review suppresses recovery outreach entirely. The reason it was cancelled is the reason not to chase it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Recovery is scoped to the specific obligation that was cancelled. A later unrelated purchase does not close it, and counting one as a recovery overstates what the intervention did.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Cancellation does not erase completed fulfillment history.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Resource release affects only unused allocation belonging to this obligation.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A financial refund is a separate lifecycle with its own decision.

**Stops when.**
- `x.cancelled` [success] cancelled; remaining obligation stopped, completed scope preserved — re-entry: the remaining obligation is explicitly zero for the cancelled scope and unchanged for whatever was delivered. A new request for the same thing is a new obligation
- handoff `h.financial` → FIN-137 (Refund Request) — a cancellation with a financial consequence

**Required data.** Events: `fulfillment_cancellation_effective` (an authoritative cancellation that has taken effect on the obligation). Attributes: `obligation_id`, `fulfillment_log`, `suppressed_sends`, `allocation_log`, `delivery_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.cancelled, h.financial · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** fulfillment cancellation reconciliation · order cancellation · cancel an order in progress · stop remaining fulfilment

## REM-156 · Corrective Reperformance

Silent lifecycle state · category remedy · slug `correction-reperformance`

**Objective.** Produce the outcome that should have been produced, while the record still shows that the first one was wrong.

**Trigger.** `correction_or_reperformance_authorized` (a correction or reperformance authorised as the remedy for an identified defect) [authoritative]. Requires: a correction or reperformance authorised as the remedy for an identified defect. Not enough on its own: a complaint with no remedy decision behind it; a refund, which is a different remedy; an internal task created without an authorised correction.

**Who enters.**
- a correction or reperformance authorised as the remedy for an identified defect
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the original fulfillment or service
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The original fulfillment is not rewritten as though it had always been correct.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The original error history is preserved alongside the correction.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An internal task completing is not a corrected business outcome.

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.verify` → REM-158 (Remedy Outcome Verification) — a correction producing an outcome
- handoff `h.alternative` → REM-157 (Remedy Confirmation) — a correction that could not produce the required outcome
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a correction outliving its deadline

**Configure.**
- `correction_reperformance.correction` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The correction deadline.

**Required data.** Events: `correction_or_reperformance_authorized` (a correction or reperformance authorised as the remedy for an identified defect); `correction_produced` (a corrected outcome is produced); `correction_failed` (the correction fails). Attributes: `correction_id`, `original_fulfillment_id`, `required_outcome`, `correction_deadline_at`, `remedy_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.verify, h.alternative, h.escalate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

## SUB-161 · Relationship Activation

Silent lifecycle state · category subscription · slug `continuing-relationship-creation`

**Objective.** Keep the existence of a continuing agreement apart from the moment it actually starts running.

**Trigger.** `continuing_relationship_creation_authorized` (an authorised agreement to enter a continuing relationship, with parties, scope and a term) [authoritative]. Requires: an authorised agreement to enter a continuing relationship, with parties, scope and a term. Not enough on its own: a payment succeeding, which funds a relationship without being what starts it - some contracts activate on signature, some on provisioning, some on a regulatory date; a plan being selected in an interface; a structural link being created between two entities, which connects them without agreeing a term.

**Entity.** the continuing relationship record - a subscription, contract, membership, policy, licence or service agreement — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authorised agreement to enter a continuing relationship, with parties, scope and a term
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the continuing relationship record
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A record created is not a relationship active.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Payment success alone does not define every contract's activation.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A structural link is not an agreement. A person belonging to an organisation, or one account being the parent of another, is a relationship with no term to run, and it belongs to the entity-structure lifecycle rather than here.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] No entitlement is granted before the authoritative effective conditions are met.

**Stops when.**
- `x.never-active` [invalid-state] created and never activated; no entitlement was ever granted — re-entry: a new agreement between the same parties is a new relationship rather than this one resuming, and it starts from its own conditions
- handoff `h.scheduled` → SUB-162 (Future Activation Revalidation) — a relationship created with a future effective start
- handoff `h.entitlement` → ACC-71 (Entitlement Qualification) — an active continuing relationship whose entitlements now need qualifying

**Configure.**
- `continuing_relationship.requirement` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The window policy allows a created but unstarted relationship to remain open.

**Required data.** Events: `continuing_relationship_creation_authorized` (an authorised agreement to enter a continuing relationship, with parties, scope and a term); `activation_requirements_satisfied` (the outstanding activation requirements are satisfied); `agreement_withdrawn` (the agreement is withdrawn before it starts). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `parties`, `effective_at`, `activation_requirements`, `relationship_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.never-active, h.scheduled, h.entitlement · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** relationship activation · subscription start · contract activation · membership activation

## SUB-162 · Future Activation Revalidation

Silent lifecycle state · category subscription · slug `future-effective-activation`

**Objective.** Activate a future-dated relationship from what is true at the effective time, not from what was true when it was scheduled.

**Trigger.** `relationship_created_with_future_start` (a created relationship whose effective start is in the future) [authoritative]. Requires: a created relationship whose effective start is in the future. Not enough on its own: a relationship effective immediately; a scheduled activation with no relationship record behind it; a cancellation before start, which suppresses the activation rather than starting it.

**Entity.** the future-dated continuing relationship and its scheduled activation — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a created relationship whose effective start is in the future
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the future
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A future agreement is not a currently active relationship.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A scheduled activation never executes blindly if its prerequisites changed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A pre-start cancellation suppresses the stale activation job.

**Stops when.**
- `x.cancelled-before-start` [invalid-state] cancelled before it started; never active and never billed — re-entry: a new agreement is a new relationship. This one is closed at a state it never left
- `x.failed` [failure] reached its effective date and did not activate — re-entry: the prerequisites being satisfied later does not activate this relationship retroactively. A new agreement is created if the parties still want one
- handoff `h.entitlement` → ACC-71 (Entitlement Qualification) — a future-dated relationship activating at its effective time

**Configure.**
- `future_effective.effective` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The scheduled activation waits until its own effective time and revalidates there; nothing activates from what was true when it was scheduled.
- `future_effective.note_budget` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — This loop runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; when it is spent the instance takes its timeout path (GLB-24).
- `future_effective.hold` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The hold window the governing terms allow.

**Required data.** Events: `relationship_created_with_future_start` (a created relationship whose effective start is in the future); `cancellation_confirmed` (an authoritative cancellation recorded against the relationship; an effective end date); `prerequisite_changed` (a material prerequisite changes before the effective time); `activation_requirements_satisfied` (the outstanding activation requirements are satisfied). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `relationship_log`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.cancelled-before-start, x.failed, h.entitlement · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** future activation revalidation · scheduled start · future-dated subscription · activation at effective date

## SUB-165 · Renewal Payment Recovery

Silent lifecycle state · category subscription · slug `renewal-payment-failure`

**Objective.** Hold a renewal whose payment failed in the state the grace policy defines - active, restricted or pending - until the payment is recovered, an authorised alternate resolution is agreed, or the grace deadline passes; the communication belongs to payment failure recovery.

**Trigger.** `renewal_payment_failed` (an authoritative failure of a payment a renewal depends on) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative failure of a payment a renewal depends on. Not enough on its own: a payment attempt erroring in transit, which is unknown rather than failed - and treating unknown as failed puts relationships into grace over payments that succeeded.

**Entity.** the continuing relationship and the renewal obligation whose payment failed — instance `subscription_id + renewal_cycle_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a renewal payment has failed on a subscription with a defined grace policy
- the renewal was decided and executed up to the payment step
- no recovery state instance is already open for this cycle

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A subscription class with no defined grace policy goes to decision resolution (DEC-181); no grace state is invented.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] This journey holds the relationship state; every message about the failed payment is payment failure recovery's (FIN-134), never a second one from here.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A recovered payment completes the renewal; an authorised alternate resolution does the same on its own terms.

**Stops when.**

- handoff `h.undefined` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a failed renewal payment with no defined grace or lapse semantics
- handoff `h.complete` → SUB-164 (Renewal Execution) — a recovered renewal obligation with its other requirements intact
- handoff `h.end` → SUB-170 (Continuing Relationship End Reconciliation) — a renewal that lapsed after its recovery window closed

**Configure.**
- `renewal_payment.grace_deadline` · attribute-bound — **recommended: the grace deadline the policy defines for this subscription class** (confidence high) — The grace state lasts exactly as long as the grace policy for this subscription class defines; its end is the consequence, never an extension invented here.

**Required data.** Events: `renewal_payment_failed` (an authoritative failure of a payment a renewal depends on); `obligation_satisfied` (the obligation is satisfied in the system of record); `alternate_resolution_agreed` (an authorised alternate resolution of the obligation is agreed). Attributes: `subscription_id`, `renewal_cycle_id`, `grace_policy_id`, `grace_deadline_at`, `failed_at`, `obligation_id` (optional: `restriction_scope`, `alternate_resolution_ref`).

**Measurement.** journey outcome: handoff h.complete, h.end, h.undefined · business outcome: `obligation_satisfied` (the obligation is satisfied in the system of record) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution entered-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: duplicate_payment_message, grace_state_invented, end_before_grace_deadline

**Aliases.** renewal payment recovery · renewal dunning (state) · failed renewal charge · grace period · involuntary churn (renewal)

## SUB-166 · Plan Change Validation

Silent lifecycle state · category subscription · slug `terms-change`

**Objective.** Apply an authorized change to a running relationship at the right time, as a delta against whatever is actually there then.

**Trigger.** `relationship_change_requested` (an authorized request to change a running term-bearing relationship's plan, tier, scope or terms) [authoritative]. Requires: an authorized request to change a running term-bearing relationship's plan, tier, scope or terms. Not enough on its own: someone asking about a different plan, which is a question rather than an authorized change; a structural relationship between the entities changing type or state, which changes how they are related rather than what was agreed.

**Entity.** the continuing relationship and the change request raised against it — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authorized request to change a running term-bearing relationship's plan, tier, scope or terms
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the continuing relationship and the change request raised against it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A change requested is not a change applied.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Historical terms are never rewritten.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A structural link changing type or state is not a plan or terms change merely because the same entities are involved.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A scheduled upgrade or downgrade never executes against a relationship that has ended or materially changed without revalidation.

**Stops when.**
- `x.rejected` [invalid-state] change rejected; the current terms stand — re-entry: the same change may become permitted at a different point in the relationship - at a term boundary, or after a minimum period - and is requested again then
- `x.void` [invalid-state] scheduled change voided before it took effect — re-entry: the change can be requested again against whatever relationship exists now, and is evaluated on those terms
- `x.not-applied` [invalid-state] change not applied; the current terms remain in force unchanged — re-entry: the change can be requested again once the blocking dependency is available
- `x.applied` [success] change applied as a new terms version; prior terms preserved — re-entry: further changes are new requests evaluated against this version rather than against the original agreement
- handoff `h.undefined` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a change request the governing terms do not address
- handoff `h.review` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a scheduled change whose base terms no longer exist
- handoff `h.entitlement` → ACC-73 (Entitlement Recalculation) — an applied change that alters entitlement scope
- handoff `h.financial` → FIN-131 (Financial Obligation Tracking) — an applied change with a financial consequence

**Configure.**
- `terms_change.effective` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — A scheduled change waits until its own effective time and is revalidated there against the relationship as it then stands.

**Required data.** Events: `relationship_change_requested` (an authorized request to change a running term-bearing relationship's plan, tier, scope or terms); `relationship_ended` (an authoritative end to an active structural relationship between two entities, with an effective time); `change_withdrawn` (the change request is withdrawn). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `change_log`, `suppressed_sends`, `relationship_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.rejected, x.void, x.not-applied, x.applied, h.undefined, h.review, h.entitlement, h.financial · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** plan change validation · upgrade or downgrade · change of terms · plan switch

## SUB-167 · Cancellation Effective-Date Resolution

Silent lifecycle state · category subscription · slug `cancellation-request`

**Objective.** Establish whether and when a relationship will end, while the current term keeps running until it does.

**Trigger.** `cancellation_requested` (an authorized request to end a continuing relationship) [declared]. Requires: an authorized request to end a continuing relationship. Not enough on its own: someone asking how to cancel, or viewing a cancellation page, which is intent rather than a request.

**Entity.** the continuing relationship and the cancellation request raised against it — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authorized request to end a continuing relationship
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the continuing relationship and the cancellation request raised against it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A cancellation requested is not a relationship cancelled.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Non-renewing is not currently inactive.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Current entitlement is never ended early when the cancellation is end-of-term.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Cancellation semantics are never invented.

**Stops when.**
- `x.blocked` [failure] cancellation blocked; the relationship continues unchanged and the request stands — re-entry: the request proceeds once the blocker clears. Nothing about the relationship changed in the meantime, including its entitlements

- handoff `h.undefined` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a cancellation request against a relationship with no defined cancellation semantics

**Required data.** Events: `cancellation_requested` (an authorized request to end a continuing relationship). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `cancellation_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.blocked, h.authority, h.undefined, h.end, h.scheduled · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** cancellation effective-date resolution · cancel subscription request · when a cancellation takes effect · cancellation request handling

## SUB-168 · Scheduled Cancellation Revalidation

Silent lifecycle state · category subscription · slug `scheduled-termination`

**Objective.** Stop a scheduled end from executing against a relationship the counterparty has since chosen to keep.

**Trigger.** `scheduled_cancellation_effective_time_reached` (a scheduled termination whose effective time has arrived) [authoritative]. Requires: a scheduled termination whose effective time has arrived. Not enough on its own: a scheduled end whose effective time has not arrived; a cancellation requested but not scheduled; a renewal decision, which is its own lifecycle.

**Entity.** the continuing relationship and the scheduled termination standing against it — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

- a scheduled termination whose effective time has arrived
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the continuing relationship and the scheduled termination standing against it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A scheduled cancellation is version-aware.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A reactivation, resubscription or change after scheduling invalidates the old cancellation.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Termination never deletes relationship history.

**Stops when.**
- `x.suppressed` [suppression] stale termination suppressed; the relationship continues on its current version — re-entry: a fresh cancellation against the current relationship is a new decision with its own effective end. The suppressed one stays in the record as something that was decided and then overtaken
- handoff `h.end` → SUB-170 (Continuing Relationship End Reconciliation) — a scheduled termination executing against a still-valid cancellation

**Required data.** Events: `scheduled_cancellation_effective_time_reached` (a scheduled termination whose effective time has arrived). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `cancellation_log`, `suppressed_sends`, `relationship_log`.

## SUB-169 · Relationship Suspension

Silent lifecycle state · category subscription · slug `relationship-suspension`

**Objective.** Hold a relationship in a state where it cannot operate normally and has not ended.

**Trigger.** `relationship_suspension_condition` (an authoritative suspension or hold condition - financial, administrative, security, eligibility, operational or otherwise policy-defined) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative suspension or hold condition - financial, administrative, security, eligibility, operational or otherwise policy-defined. Not enough on its own: a risk signal, which is a reason to look rather than a decision to suspend; a single failed payment, which has its own recovery lifecycle before it reaches this one.

**Entity.** the continuing relationship and the suspension standing against it — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative suspension or hold condition - financial, administrative, security, eligibility, operational or otherwise policy-defined
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the continuing relationship and the suspension standing against it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Suspended is not cancelled.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A suspension never silently resets renewal or end dates unless governing policy explicitly does so.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Restoration uses current terms rather than a historical snapshot.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A suspension always states the condition that would restore it.

**Stops when.**
- `x.restored` [success] restored to ACTIVE on current terms — re-entry: a further suspension is a new suspension with its own reason and its own restoration condition. The previous one stays in the record
- handoff `h.review` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a suspension reaching its maximum duration undecided
- handoff `h.end` → SUB-170 (Continuing Relationship End Reconciliation) — a suspended relationship that will not resume

**Configure.**
- `relationship_suspension.suspension` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The maximum suspension duration policy defines.

**Required data.** Events: `relationship_suspension_condition` (an authoritative suspension or hold condition - financial, administrative, security, eligibility, operational or otherwise policy-defined); `suspension_reason_resolved` (the reason for the suspension is resolved); `terminal_decision_recorded` (a terminal decision is recorded). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `relationship_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.restored, h.review, h.end · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** relationship suspension · subscription on hold · pause subscription · relationship hold

## SUB-170 · Continuing Relationship End Reconciliation

Silent lifecycle state · category subscription · slug `continuing-relationship-end`

**Objective.** Stop what the relationship was granting, while everything it created keeps its own lifecycle.

**Trigger.** `relationship_end_effective` (an authoritative end of a term-bearing continuing relationship taking effect, by cancellation, non-renewal, expiry, termination or lapse) [authoritative]. Requires: an authoritative end of a term-bearing continuing relationship taking effect, by cancellation, non-renewal, expiry, termination or lapse. Not enough on its own: a cancellation requested, which sets a date rather than reaching one; a payment failing, which may or may not ever become an ending; a structural relationship between the parties ending, which removes a link rather than a term.

**Entity.** the ended continuing relationship and the state that depended on it — instance `relationship_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authoritative end of a term-bearing continuing relationship taking effect, by cancellation, non-renewal, expiry, termination or lapse
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the ended continuing relationship and the state that depended on it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A relationship ending is not an account closure.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A relationship ending is not data deletion.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A relationship ending is not the removal of a structural link. The parties can stay exactly as connected as they were, and unlinking them is a decision nobody made here.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A relationship ending never erases historical entitlements, payments or fulfilled obligations.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Commitments created while active are reconciled separately and can outlive the relationship.

**Stops when.**
- `x.former` [success] FORMER, EXPIRED or TERMINATED; the account, the history and the surviving obligations all stand — re-entry: a new subscription, contract or membership is a new relationship rather than this one resuming. Its history remains readable, and everything it legitimately created during its term remains owed until its own lifecycle resolves it
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a relationship wind-down outliving its window

**Configure.**
- `continuing_relationship.winddown` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — Relationship-specific obligations still running at the end are given the wind-down window policy defines; past it the wind-down escalates to ownership.

**Required data.** Events: `relationship_end_effective` (an authoritative end of a term-bearing continuing relationship taking effect, by cancellation, non-renewal, expiry, termination or lapse); `wind_down_completed` (the wind-down completes). Attributes: `relationship_id`, `relationship_log`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.former, h.escalate · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** relationship end reconciliation · subscription ended · contract expiry reconciliation · post-termination obligations

## SCH-172 · Temporary Slot Hold

Silent lifecycle state · category scheduling · slug `slot-hold`

**Objective.** Protect specific capacity for a bounded moment while a booking is being completed, without pretending it is a booking.

**Trigger.** `temporary_hold_granted` (a hold granted against real, currently free capacity on an identified slot) [authoritative]. Requires: a hold granted against real, currently free capacity on an identified slot. Not enough on its own: a hold requested, which is not a hold granted - under contention two requests can both be made and only one can succeed, and treating the request as the grant overbooks by exactly the number of losers.

**Entity.** the hold, the capacity it protects and the requester who owns it — instance `hold_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a hold granted against real, currently free capacity on an identified slot
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the hold
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A hold is not a confirmed reservation.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] An expired hold consumes no capacity.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Hold creation and release are both idempotent.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Concurrent holds respect real capacity rather than the capacity each of them assumed.

**Stops when.**
- `x.consumed` [success] consumed into a confirmed reservation; capacity never passed back through free — re-entry: the reservation now owns the capacity. A cancellation releases it through the cancellation lifecycle rather than through this hold
- `x.expired` [timeout] EXPIRED; capacity released and available again — re-entry: the same requester can take a new hold on the same slot if it is still free, which is a new hold rather than an extension of this one
- `x.released` [success] RELEASED; capacity available again — re-entry: a repeated release against this hold is a no-op rather than a second return of capacity


**Configure.**
- `slot_hold.hold` · attribute-bound — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The hold's expiry.

**Required data.** Events: `temporary_hold_granted` (a hold granted against real, currently free capacity on an identified slot); `booking_confirmed` (the booking is confirmed); `hold_released` (the hold is explicitly released); `booking_intent_abandoned` (the booking intent behind the hold is abandoned). Attributes: `hold_id`, `slot_ref`, `requester_id`, `expires_at`, `hold_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit x.consumed, x.expired, x.released · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** temporary slot hold · slot reservation hold · booking capacity hold · checkout timer for bookings


---

## SCH-173 · Reservation Validation

Silent lifecycle state · category scheduling · slug `reservation-request`

**Objective.** Turn a request for a specific time into a commitment both sides can rely on, or say clearly that it did not.

**Trigger.** `reservation_requested` (a request to reserve an identified slot on an identified resource or service) [declared]. Requires: a request to reserve an identified slot on an identified resource or service. Not enough on its own: a payment attempt for the booking, which funds it without confirming it unless the booking semantics explicitly make payment the confirming act; a slot being held, which protects capacity without committing anyone to anything.

**Entity.** the reservation request, the requester and the resource or service it names — instance `reservation_request_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a request to reserve an identified slot on an identified resource or service
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the reservation request
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A request submitted is not a reservation confirmed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Previously displayed availability may have changed and is revalidated rather than trusted.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A payment attempt alone does not confirm a reservation unless the authoritative booking semantics say so.

**Stops when.**
- `x.already` [success] already confirmed; the existing reservation stands and no second one was created — re-entry: a genuinely different booking is a different request. A resubmitted one resolves here rather than consuming a second slot
- `x.lapsed` [timeout] request lapsed; capacity released and nothing committed — re-entry: a new request is evaluated against current availability, which may no longer include the slot that was being held
- handoff `h.alternative` → SCH-171 (Availability Evaluation) — a reservation that could not be made against the requested slot
- handoff `h.prepare` → SCH-174 (Reservation Readiness) — a confirmed reservation with time still to run before it

**Configure.**
- `reservation_request.pending` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The pending window the booking semantics allow.

**Required data.** Events: `reservation_requested` (a request to reserve an identified slot on an identified resource or service); `dependency_resolved` (the named dependency resolves); `request_withdrawn` (the requester withdraws the request). Attributes: `reservation_request_id`, `requester_id`, `resource_ref`, `slot_ref`, `booking_requirements`, `reservation_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.already, x.lapsed, h.alternative, h.prepare · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** reservation validation · booking request validation · confirm a booking · booking capacity check


---

## SCH-174 · Reservation Readiness

Silent lifecycle state · category scheduling · slug `reservation-preparation`

**Objective.** Establish, by the pre-service checkpoint, that every prerequisite of a confirmed reservation is met - escalating what is not to ownership - so that the start-time journey begins from a booking that is actually ready.

**Trigger.** `reservation_confirmed` (a reservation confirmed with a scheduled time still in the future) [authoritative]. Requires: a reservation confirmed with a scheduled time still in the future. Not enough on its own: a booking requested but not confirmed; a confirmation with no prerequisites defined, which has nothing to make ready; a reminder having been sent, which says nothing about readiness.

**Entity.** the confirmed reservation and the preparation running against it — instance `booking_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a reservation is confirmed with its prerequisites defined
- the pre-service checkpoint for its service class is defined
- no readiness instance is already open for this booking

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A booking that materially changes - moved, reassigned, cancelled - supersedes the open readiness instance; readiness is re-established against the new booking, never carried over.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] This journey sends nothing to the customer; prerequisites the customer must meet are prompted by the reminder journey (SCH-266), and what remains unmet at the checkpoint goes to ownership.

**Stops when.**
- `x.superseded` [invalid-state] preparation superseded by a change to the booking — re-entry: the reschedule or cancellation owns what happens next. Preparation is re-derived against the new booking rather than carried across, because a different time can need different things
- `x.upcoming` [success] READY or UPCOMING; the commitment stands and its conditions are in the state recorded — re-entry: the revalidation at service time reads the booking's current state rather than this readiness record, which was true when it was written
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a critical prerequisite unresolved at the pre-service checkpoint

**Configure.**
- `scheduling.readiness_checkpoint` · reminder-before-attribute — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The checkpoint is the lead the pre-service policy defines ahead of the scheduled time for this service class; what is unmet at the checkpoint is escalated, not waited on further.

**Required data.** Events: `reservation_confirmed` (a reservation confirmed with a scheduled time still in the future); `prerequisites_completed` (every customer-owed prerequisite is recorded complete); `booking_materially_changed` (the booking is cancelled, moved or materially changed). Attributes: `booking_id`, `scheduled_at`, `prerequisites`, `checkpoint_lead`, `provider_id` (optional: `prerequisite_status`, `owner_id`).

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.upcoming, x.superseded, h.escalate · business outcome: `prerequisites_completed` (every customer-owed prerequisite is recorded complete) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution entered-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: start_on_unready_booking, readiness_carried_over_change

**Aliases.** reservation readiness · booking preparation · pre-service checklist · prerequisite tracking · readiness checkpoint


---

## SCH-175 · Reschedule Validation

Silent lifecycle state · category scheduling · slug `reschedule`

**Objective.** Move a commitment to a new time without ever leaving the customer holding neither.

**Trigger.** `reschedule_requested` (an authorized request to move an existing confirmed reservation) [declared]. Requires: an authorized request to move an existing confirmed reservation. Not enough on its own: someone viewing other times, which is looking rather than asking to move.

**Entity.** the existing reservation and the reschedule request raised against it — instance `booking_id + reschedule_request_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authorized request to move an existing confirmed reservation
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the existing reservation and the reschedule request raised against it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A reschedule requested is not a reschedule completed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The original slot is never released before the replacement is secured unless policy explicitly requires it.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The historical original time remains auditable.

**Stops when.**
- `x.original-stands` [failure] original reservation preserved unchanged; no move took place — re-entry: the reschedule can be attempted again against different availability. Cancelling the original is a separate decision the customer makes explicitly
- `x.rescheduled` [success] moved; the new time is committed and the original is preserved in the record — re-entry: a further move is a new reschedule against this reservation, and it joins the same history rather than replacing it
- handoff `h.prepare` → SCH-174 (Reservation Readiness) — a rescheduled booking whose preparation requirements have changed

**Required data.** Events: `reschedule_requested` (an authorized request to move an existing confirmed reservation). Attributes: `booking_id`, `reschedule_request_id`, `requested_time`, `replacement_candidate`, `preparation_requirements`, `reservation_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.original-stands, x.rescheduled, h.prepare · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** reschedule validation · move a booking · change appointment time · rebooking


---

## SCH-176 · Reservation Cancellation Reconciliation

Silent lifecycle state · category scheduling · slug `reservation-cancellation`

**Objective.** End a future time commitment cleanly, returning the capacity and leaving the money to be decided elsewhere.

**Trigger.** `reservation_cancellation_effective` (an authorized cancellation of a confirmed reservation, taking effect) [authoritative]. Requires: an authorized cancellation of a confirmed reservation, taking effect. Not enough on its own: a cancellation requested, which is a request and may be blocked or may be for a later effective time; someone not responding to a reminder, which is silence rather than a cancellation.

**Entity.** the reservation and the cancellation acting on it — instance `booking_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- an authorized cancellation of a confirmed reservation, taking effect
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the reservation and the cancellation acting on it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A cancellation requested is not a cancellation effective.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A cancellation is not a no-show.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Cancellation never deletes reservation history.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] The refund or fee decision belongs to the financial lifecycle rather than to this one.

**Stops when.**
- `x.cancelled` [success] CANCELLED; capacity released, history preserved, nothing owed — re-entry: a new booking is a new reservation rather than this one resuming, and this cancellation stays part of the record either way
- handoff `h.provider` → SCH-180 (Booking Reschedule) — a cancellation originating on the provider side
- handoff `h.reconcile` → external:external-status-reconciliation — a cancellation the external booking system has not confirmed · contract: booking_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.refund` → FIN-137 (Refund Request) — a cancelled reservation that was paid for
- handoff `h.fee` → FIN-131 (Financial Obligation Tracking) — a cancellation the terms attach a fee to

**Required data.** Events: `reservation_cancellation_effective` (an authorized cancellation of a confirmed reservation, taking effect). Attributes: `booking_id`, `cancellation_log`, `reservation_log`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.cancelled, h.provider, h.reconcile, h.refund, h.fee · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** reservation cancellation reconciliation · cancel a booking · appointment cancellation · booking cancelled


---

## SCH-177 · Pre-Service Revalidation

Silent lifecycle state · category scheduling · slug `scheduled-start`

**Objective.** Start a service from what the booking is at its start time, and establish attendance from evidence rather than from the fact that a reminder went out.

**Trigger.** `pre_start_window_reached` (the defined pre-start or start window for a scheduled occurrence being reached) [authoritative]. Requires: the defined pre-start or start window for a scheduled occurrence being reached. Not enough on its own: a reminder having been sent, which says nothing about the start; the scheduled time itself passing - that is the arrival window, not its opening; a provider-side readiness check, which SCH-174 owns.

**Entity.** the confirmed reservation and the occurrence about to begin — instance `booking_id + occurrence_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- the booking's pre-start window has been reached in the booking's own local time
- the booking was confirmed and has not been superseded by a later confirmation

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A start that no longer matches authoritative state - cancelled, moved, reassigned - is suppressed: no service is begun and no attendance is recorded against a booking that is not the one the person holds.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A cancellation arriving inside the start window is a cancellation, not a no-show; it hands to cancellation reconciliation and nothing here treats it as a miss.

**Stops when.**
- `x.suppressed` [invalid-state] stale start suppressed; no service was begun and no attendance state was written — re-entry: the booking's current version has its own scheduled occurrence, which revalidates on its own terms when it arrives
- handoff `h.provider-exception` → SCH-180 (Booking Reschedule) — a booking that cannot be delivered by its assigned provider or resource
- handoff `h.escalate` → OWN-55 (Ownership Escalation) — a service blocked at its own start time
- handoff `h.attended` → SCH-178 (Service Completion) — attendance established for a revalidated booking
- handoff `h.cancelled` → SCH-176 (Reservation Cancellation Reconciliation) — a cancellation arriving inside the start window
- handoff `h.missed` → SCH-179 (No-Show Validation) — an arrival window closing with no attendance established

**Configure.**
- `scheduling.arrival_window` · attribute-bound — **recommended: scheduled_at plus the arrival tolerance policy records** (confidence high) — The arrival window is the tolerance after the scheduled start that the service's own attendance semantics allow. Its end is what turns a late arrival into a miss.

**Required data.** Events: `pre_start_window_reached` (the defined pre-start or start window for a scheduled occurrence being reached); `attendance_recorded` (attendance or check-in is authoritatively established); `booking_cancelled` (the booking is cancelled in the system of record). Attributes: `booking_id`, `occurrence_id`, `person_id`, `scheduled_at`, `timezone`, `provider_id`, `arrival_evidence_source` (optional: `grace_policy_id`).

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff h.attended, h.missed, h.cancelled, h.provider-exception, x.suppressed · business outcome: `attendance_recorded` (attendance or check-in is authoritatively established) per instance, observed in this journey, attribution entered-before-event, comparison not-applicable · guardrails: service_started_on_stale_booking, attendance_recorded_without_evidence

**Aliases.** pre-service check · arrival window · check-in · start-time revalidation


---

## SCH-178 · Service Completion

Silent lifecycle state · category scheduling · slug `service-attendance`

**Objective.** Separate the fact that someone turned up from the question of whether they got what they came for.

**Trigger.** `service_commencement_established` (attendance or service commencement established authoritatively) [authoritative]. Requires: attendance or service commencement established authoritatively. Not enough on its own: a reminder being opened or clicked, which is interest rather than attendance; arriving at a location, where the booking semantics require a check-in to establish attendance.

**Entity.** the reservation occurrence and the service delivered inside it — instance `booking_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- attendance or service commencement established authoritatively
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the reservation occurrence and the service delivered inside it
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Check-in is not service completed.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Attendance is not a successful outcome.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Partial service is never represented as full completion.

**Stops when.**
- `x.completed` [success] COMPLETED; attendance and delivery both established — re-entry: a problem raised afterwards about what was delivered is a post-completion issue and is assessed on its own terms
- handoff `h.reconcile` → external:side-effect-reconciliation — an occurrence that began and whose outcome was never established · contract: booking_id, handed_at, reason
- handoff `h.remainder` → REM-157 (Remedy Confirmation) — a scheduled service delivered only in part
- handoff `h.reschedule` → SCH-175 (Reschedule Validation) — remaining scope needing a further scheduled occurrence
- handoff `h.provider` → SCH-180 (Booking Reschedule) — a service that could not proceed for provider-side reasons after the customer arrived
- handoff `h.rebook` → SCH-175 (Reschedule Validation) — a service that could not proceed because a customer-side requirement was absent

**Configure.**
- `service_attendance.service` · observation-window — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — The scheduled duration plus its tolerance.
- `service_attendance.interrupt_budget` — **CONFIG_REQUIRED** — This loop runs against a budget fixed when the instance opened; when it is spent the instance takes its timeout path (GLB-24).

**Required data.** Events: `service_commencement_established` (attendance or service commencement established authoritatively); `service_completion_recorded` (the service completes); `service_interrupted` (the service is interrupted). Attributes: `booking_id`, `occurrence_log`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.completed, h.reconcile, h.remainder, h.reschedule, h.provider, h.rebook · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** service completion · appointment completed · service delivered · attendance versus outcome


---

## SCH-179 · No-Show Validation

Silent lifecycle state · category scheduling · slug `no-show`

**Objective.** Establish that one confirmed booking did not happen because the customer did not attend, having ruled out every other explanation.

**Trigger.** `service_window_passed_without_commencement` (a confirmed occurrence whose service and arrival windows have both closed with no commencement established) [authoritative]. Requires: a confirmed occurrence whose service and arrival windows have both closed with no commencement established. Not enough on its own: the scheduled start time having passed, which does not close the arrival window and concludes nothing about someone who is running late.

**Entity.** the single reservation occurrence that did not take place — instance `booking_id`, one-active-per-key

**Who enters.**
- a confirmed occurrence whose service and arrival windows have both closed with no commencement established
- no instance of this journey is already open for the the single reservation occurrence that did not take place
- hard gates (GLB-31) allow communication for this purpose

**Suppressed when.**
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A no-show is never inferred before the relevant service and arrival windows close.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A no-show is not a cancellation.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] Fees and penalties are never invented.
- [CANONICAL_RULE] A late-arriving attendance event reconciles against the recorded no-show state.

**Stops when.**
- `x.suppressed` [suppression] no-show suppressed; the booking was cancelled or moved and nothing is attributed — re-entry: the rescheduled occurrence has its own window and is assessed on its own terms when it arrives
- `x.closed` [success] NO_SHOW recorded; no consequence beyond the record — re-entry: an attendance event arriving after this reconciles against the recorded no-show rather than being ignored - a late system update is a reason to correct the record, not evidence that the record was right
- handoff `h.provider` → SCH-180 (Booking Reschedule) — a missed occurrence the provider side could not have delivered
- handoff `h.undefined` → DEC-181 (Decision Request) — a missed occurrence with no defined attendance semantics
- handoff `h.attended` → SCH-178 (Service Completion) — an arrival inside the grace period the semantics allow
- handoff `h.rebook` → SCH-171 (Availability Evaluation) — a missed appointment policy allows to be rebooked
- handoff `h.fee` → FIN-131 (Financial Obligation Tracking) — a no-show the terms attach a charge to

**Required data.** Events: `service_window_passed_without_commencement` (a confirmed occurrence whose service and arrival windows have both closed with no commencement established). Attributes: `booking_id`, `occurrence_log`, `suppressed_sends`.

**Measurement.** journey outcome: exit-or-handoff x.suppressed, x.closed, h.provider, h.undefined, h.attended, h.rebook, h.fee · no business outcome (see the review note) · guardrails: state_written_on_stale_entity

**Aliases.** no-show validation · missed appointment detection · did not attend · no-show record
